# OoT (Switch 2) modding workspace

Game data: the extracted romfs at `%USERPROFILE%\Games\Hookshot\romfs` (from the Hookshot build). The game runs on the Tears of the Kingdom engine family: zstd files with dictionaries, SARC, BYML, AINB node graphs.

Python: `venv\Scripts\python.exe` (Python 3.11, with `ainb` and `pyzstd`).

## Tools

- `tools\nxfmt.py`: zstd (with the game's ZsDic), SARC and BYML readers.
- `tools\nxpack.py`: SARC file replacement; zstd compression with the same dictionary as the original file. Unmodified archives rebuild byte for byte.
- `tools\byml.py`: BYML read/write with exact scalar types; 19 of 21 RSDB tables rebuild byte for byte (ActorInfo: same content, different sharing; Tag: binary nodes, unsupported).
- `tools\msbt.py`: message (text) files. This game's MSBT text is UTF-8. All 540 US English files round-trip byte for byte.
- `tools\ainbx.py`: AINB load/save on top of the `ainb` package, with two writer fixes (transition indices, query order). 101 of 108 `Sequence` graphs rewrite byte for byte, every menu graph included; `ainbx.from_dict(d, like=original)` (editable dict form) 98 of 108. Not exact: `AfterChangeBanc`, `BeforeChangeBanc`, `DestroySceneCommon(.110)`, `ShortCut`, `UpdateShortCutOpenHoldFrameCounter`, `LoadBancImpl`, `BeforeInitializeCommon`, `BeforeTitle(.110)`; don't edit those with it yet.
- `tools\rstb.py`: the resource size table (read, change, write; rebuilds byte for byte).
- `tools\a64.py`: reverse-engineering helpers for the executable image (`image.bin`, loaded at 0x80000000; `main` starts at 0x80020000): string search, ADRP+ADD/LDR cross references, capstone disassembly, function bounds (`pacibsp`), per-function strings/calls. Needs `capstone`, `numpy` (installed in the venv).
- `tools\exepatch.py`: code patches for `main` as an IPS32 file (`exefs\<build id>.ips`; file offset = 0x100 + address - 0x80020000), with keystone (`keystone-engine`) assembly, checks of the replaced instructions, and a code cave in the zero padding after `.text` (0x83c661d0-0x83c67000). The emulator's built-in "Dynamic FPS by Dark" patch owns main+0x3C46200-0x3C46278 and is silently skipped if a mod touches it, so our cave starts at main+0x3C46C00 (0x83c66c00) with the signature words 1337, 69420. Build ID of v1.1.1 `main`: `E0F3CCFAF50065AE4EA01EB5AEB13B1A45DA3348`. Instructions keystone does not know (`retab`, `ldset w13, w14, [x12]`, `ldclr w13, w14, [x12]`) are listed in `exepatch.PSEUDO` and written by their name; never use `.long` inside assembly text: keystone then computes every later absolute branch target 4 bytes off per `.long`. `Patcher.rodata(name, blob)` puts tables in the zero padding after `.rodata` (0x8486c100-0x8486d000, `RODATA_CAVE`; one mod only, so far Character models in `--npcs`).
- `tools\bympatch.py`: in-place BYML edits that keep every existing byte (change inline ints, append a dict to an array; the grown array goes to the end of the file). For tables `byml.py` cannot rebuild exactly, like the GameDataList (big-endian; a rewrite comes out 13 KB larger).
- `tools\gamedata.py`: GameData flag hash (murmur3_32 of the full name) and the save-size bookkeeping rules.
- Code cave split between mods that ship their own `.ips` (all are applied together): `Header four tabs` 0x83c66c00-0x83c67000, `Character models` 0x83c66800-0x83c66c00 (`exepatch.CAVE_MODELS`); each part starts with the signature words. `build.py` refuses overlapping patches across mods.
- `tools\live.py`: live GameData flags and menu actors through the debug server (see the player-model section).
- `tools\livetrace.py <out file> [seconds]`: records the menu actor manager from the running game about 180 times a second through the debug server (read only), one line per value that changed: display type, camera block 0, our flags, and for the camera, menu slots 0 / 1 / 4 and the backdrop the actor's state, flag words, position and pending position request. Start it, do the thing in game, read the file (it settled the transition's version 2 in one press).
- `tools\package.py`, one folder (2026-11-12): a package now holds ONE mod folder, `OoT Character Select`, made by `merge()` from the working folders the way the emulator sees them together: of a file several folders have, the first folder's in name order (the lite build: 535 files, 8 shadowed ones dropped), and the six code patches as one IPS32 file (no byte in two of them; applied, the same 5,569 bytes). The size table that wins is the voices folder's; it must have every entry of the others at least as large (it raises one, the list it replaces). `--install` puts the one folder into the normal emulator in place of the working folders; a package made while it is installed is compared with it byte for byte. `--full` only changes the character count in the text. What follows describes the collection step, which is unchanged.
- `tools\package.py <version>`: the package for other people (2026-11-10; since v0.1.1, 2026-11-11, eight folders: the five of the full build, the cloth and dress test folders and the voices folder from `build-voice\mod`, under their tested names): the installed folders as `dist\OoT-Character-Select-<version>.zip` with `INSTALL.txt`, `SHA256SUMS.txt` and the code patches' listings. It refuses unless `build\` is byte for byte what is installed (i.e. what was played), leaves out the 10 Retro Link files (not ours to pass on; nothing in the package can ask for them), accepts data files only, and writes the zip with fixed dates and order, so the same build gives the same zip and sum. `--max-mb <size>` (2026-11-12, for GameBanana's limit of 209.7 MB per file; used with 200) cuts the mod files in order into parts under that size, each with its own `INSTALL.txt`, sums and the listings; the parts unpack into the same folders (`NPC characters` alone is 267 MB, so it is cut).
- `tools\publish.py <folder>`: the public source tree (2026-11-10; pushed from `C:\Repos\OoT-Character-Select` to github.com/snaildev99/OoT-Character-Select): `mods\*.py`, `tools\*.py`, this file as `docs\NOTES.md` and `publish\*` (the public README with the package sums, the licence), with the author's first name replaced by the handle and profile paths without the user name; it fails if anything personal is left. Nothing of the game, no builds, no `assets`, no `research`, no session notes. Commits there use the account's no-reply address (set in that folder only). After a new package: put its sum into `publish\README.md`, run this, commit and push.
- `tools\bntxwrite.py`: adds textures to a layout archive's texture file (`add_textures(data, [(name, rgba)])`: ASTC 4x4 sRGB through the `astc-encoder-py` package, installed into the venv on 2026-11-10; the console's block order; the file's dictionary and relocation table rebuilt). Run by itself it checks the game's three files (see the layouts section).
- `tools\dbg.py` + `PLAY-debug.bat`: the emulator's debug server (`EDEN_NX2_DEBUG_SERVER=127.0.0.1:6545`, built into Fritchii): status, memory search and reads of the running game. Never screenshots.
- Signature values for things our mods add: header preset 1337, `CharacterMenu.module` blackboard ID 69420, node GUIDs `00001337-...`, code cave marker 1337/69420. Category stays 7 (the game uses it as a shift and a table index).
- `tools\extract.py <archive.zs> <out dir>`: unpack a SARC/blarc. `tools\strs.py <file>`: printable strings. `tools\grepimg.py <image.bin> <regex>`: strings in the game executable. `venv\Scripts\ainb.exe <file>.ainb`: AINB to JSON.
- `tools\layout.py`: UI layouts (`.buil`) as an object tree, read and write. All 164 layouts (Common, Title, ZeldaNotes, Boot) rebuild byte for byte, and also after moving every object (proves every pointer is known). Schema from the game's relocation code (see the layout section). `tools\uiedit.py`: editing on top of it: pane views (position, size, scale, visibility, ...), cloning a pane with what it owns, inserting pane blocks with every index renumbered (including the material / pane indices repeated inside picture, text, capture and list records), new animations (constants, Hermite / linear / one-key-per-frame "state" curves, driving a part's own animation with `drive()`), `anim_values()` (what an animation sets at a frame, for offline checks), text settings (`set_text`), colour palette, `pane_rects` / `part_bounds` (where a part's panes are, from the records), `validate()` (passes on all 164). `tools\bntx.py`: the UI texture archives (`Common...bntx.zs`: list, decode a texture to RGBA numbers, `stats()`; block-linear deswizzle, BC1/3/4/5/7, ASTC, RGBA8). `tools\cityhash.py`: CityHash64 (the layouts' name hash). `tools\sarcedit.py`: SARC rebuilt from its entry list, so files can be added (the layout and Mals archives rebuild byte for byte). `tools\uimap.py`: text map of every layout (`research\ui-map\`).
- `tools\uienv.py` + `PLAY-debug-ui.bat` + `install-ui.bat`: the UI sandbox's own emulator, a portable copy of the current Fritchii (hard links) in `C:\Repos\Lichii\dist\Fritchii-ui` with its own mods folder (`user\load`, set in its copy of the config), sharing saves (the config pins NAND/SD to `%APPDATA%\eden`; the emulator re-reads the user profile from there after loading the config, the stray `user\nand\...\profiles.dat` it writes first is not used), keys and shader caches (junctions). Its config is copied from the normal one on every install, with every mod of its own folder switched on. Its log is `user\log\eden_log.txt` (a hang shows as "Force stopping EmuThread" at shutdown without a crash before it). Debug server on 6546. Don't run it at the same time as the normal emulator.
- `tools\bfres.py`: model files (FRES v0.10.4): reader and skeleton editor (bone list, bone dictionary, matrix-to-bone table, the shapes' bone indices, inverse bind matrices, model rename). Edits never move existing data: new arrays and strings go where the relocation table was, the table (with entries for the new pointers) follows them. Checked on the game's files: the dictionary builder reproduces all 2,158 dictionaries of the character models byte for byte and its lookup is the game's; bone records round trip; rest matrices from the bone records match the stored inverse bind matrices (worst 1.1e-5) on all 129 `Npc_*` / `Player_*` models. `tools\npcrig.py`: an NPC skeleton as a player skeleton (see the NPC section) and `check_rig()`, the proof per file.
- `tools\npcenv.py` + `PLAY-debug-npc.bat` + `install-npc.bat`: the NPC characters build's own emulator, a portable copy of the current Fritchii in `C:\Repos\Lichii\dist\Fritchii-npc` (same scheme as the UI sandbox's: own mods folder, shared saves / keys / shader caches). Debug server on 6547. `tools\npclive.py`: logs the stored character number and the menu actors of that copy during a test (`build-npc\live-log.txt`); memory reads only; one logger serves a whole session (after a game closes or crashes it waits for the next).
- `tools\npcportrait.py` + `tools\npcportrait_blender.py` (experiment, 2026-11-10): character portraits on a transparent background, rendered outside the game. Step 1 (normal venv) writes a model's rest-pose geometry and decoded textures as arrays to `build-npc\portraits\<model>\` (vertex and index buffer layout in the file's head; smooth shapes are stored in model space, eyes are rigid to their bones; some texture names in a model do not exist as files, e.g. Saria's `Mt_Eyebrow_<model>_Alb`: the shared `Eyebrow_CmnTex_Chara_*` is used; eyebrow and eyelash colour textures are grey masks; eye textures are shared: sclera everywhere, the iris in the middle 0.27 of the eye's uv square by the mask texture, uv centre = front of the eyeball). Step 2 (`venv-blender`: Python 3.11 with `pip install bpy==4.5.16`, Blender as a module, 720 MB, nothing installed system-wide) renders head and shoulders twice, `build-npc\portraits\<name>_studio.png` and `<name>_cinematic.png` (1024 x 1024), and prints coverage, brightness and eye / cheek probe numbers. The materials are approximations of the game's shader (list in the tool's head); whether the result is good enough is snaildev's call, otherwise portraits come from the game.
- `tools\npcshots.py`: saves game frames during the portrait run (see the NPC characters entry); reads memory and asks the emulator for screenshots, presses nothing.
- `tools\npccutout.py`: cut-out portraits from those frames (pairs the last black and the first white frame per character, transparency from the difference in linear light, head-and-shoulders square from the model's Head bone height); needs Pillow (in the venv). Prints numbers only. `--fit` is the set in use (`build-npc\portraits\cut-fit`, with snaildev's per-character corrections in `ADJUST`); `--out=<folder>` writes somewhere else, for comparing a change with the set in place before replacing it; numbers after the options re-cut only those characters. `tools\npcshots.py --once` records one game only. `--newest` re-cuts from the latest run instead of the frames a portrait in place was cut from. `tools\npcbackdrop.py`: read-only log of the menu backdrop actor's and its model part's scale (for the backdrop experiment).
- `tools\npcauto.py`: NOT IN USE, never run against a game: snaildev tests in the game himself (2026-11-09: "No, I'll test it myself"); don't run it or start an emulator unless he asks. What it is built on: the debug server can press buttons too: method `input_sequence`, steps `{"buttons": [...], "left_stick": [x, y], "right_stick": [x, y], "frames": n}` on player 1, counted in presented frames (at most 1,000 steps / 36,000 frames per call; buttons A B X Y L R ZL ZR Plus Minus Up Down Left Right StickL StickR), plus `log_tail` (`after`, `limit`, `contains`) and `status` (presents, fps, paused). (Methods known from the emulator source `src\core\debugger\nx2_debug_tools.cpp` and the strings in Fritchii 5.5's exe, not from use.) The tool's design: its own emulator copy (`...\Fritchii-npc-auto`, debug server 6548; removed again) that shares nothing a person's emulator writes (own save copies, own empty shader cache, sound off, no physical controller, window minimized), `ring` = D-pad right through the character ring with a memory check after every swap, `field <number>` = write the number into its save copies (`progress.sav`: the flag's hash, then the value; verified on copies: one byte changes), boot, run / roll / swing / guard.
- `tools\seqmap.py` (field swap session): every sequence graph as a compact text outline, one line per node (index, node name, inputs as `name=value` or `name<-node.output`, `~` = left at the declared default, properties that differ from the usual, children / transitions): `venv\Scripts\python.exe tools\seqmap.py research\seq-map` writes `research\seq-map\<graph>.txt` for all 108; `--find <text>` lists the nodes of all graphs that mention a text (a node name, a screen, a flag). Read only.
- `tools\fieldenv.py` + `PLAY-debug-field.bat` + `install-field.bat`: the field swap build's own emulator, a portable copy of the current Fritchii in `C:\Repos\Lichii\dist\Fritchii-field` (same scheme as the UI sandbox's and the NPC build's: own mods folder, shared saves / keys / shader caches, every mod of its folder switched on at each install). Debug server on 6549. `tools\fieldlive.py`: logs that copy during a test (`build-field\live-log.txt`): the stored character, the "swap pending" flag, the counters and the remembered position our routines keep, and (once a swap has run) every resident actor's state; memory reads only; one logger serves a whole session. `tools\fieldcam.py [seconds]`: records the player camera around a swap about 600 times a second (the camera system object, the camera actor's pose, its component's 0x1200 bytes, the view, the player's pose and camera part, our counters) into `build-field\cam-trace.pkl` (`fieldcam.load()`), notes in `build-field\cam-trace.txt`; memory reads only; it ends when the game closes; don't run it together with `fieldlive.py`.
- `tools\bwav.py` (voices): reads the sound archives and clips. `venv\Scripts\python.exe tools\bwav.py list Player_Child Vo_` lists an archive's assets (length, whether streamed), `... wav <asset> <out.wav> [bank]` decodes one clip. It also has the writers the voice mod uses: `encode_adpcm` and `rewritten` (a clip in the game's format), `bars_parts` and `bars_build` (an archive taken apart and put together; 1037 of the game's 1152 archives come back byte for byte, among them every player and voice archive; the others hold six-channel clips or a stray last byte). `tools\voicesamples.py` writes listening samples (Link's clips reshaped towards Saria, several methods, plus her own clips) to `build-voice\samples`; needs the `praat-parselmouth` package in the venv. `tools\voiceai.py` adds AI voice conversion samples (files 7 to 9) and runs in its own environment: `venv-voice\Scripts\python.exe tools\voiceai.py` (`--check` prints how close each sample is to Saria's voice by speaker embedding). It uses Seed-VC, cloned into `build-voice\ai\seed-vc` (GPL-3.0, an outside tool, not part of our source; archived upstream since 2025-11; its models, 2.4 GB, download into its `checkpoints` folder on the first run). `venv-voice` is Python 3.10 with PyTorch 2.4.0 for CUDA 12.1 and the packages `inference.py` needs (5 GB; not its whole `requirements.txt`), plus `keystone-engine`, `capstone` and `unicorn` for the voice mod's code patch.

`research\` holds the menu graphs (`menu-ainb-json`), the Common UI layouts (`layouts-common`), and lists of screen classes and sequence nodes found in the executable.

## Mods

`install-mods.bat` (= `mods\build.py --install`) builds every mod in `MODS` from the untouched romfs into `build\<mod>\romfs`, copies it into `%APPDATA%\eden\load\0400486024DEC000\<mod>` (shared by all emulator builds) and removes retired folders. It refuses while the emulator runs. Each mod asserts the original values it changes; files that grow or are new get entries in one combined size table, shipped as identical copies in `Characters page` and `Character models` (whichever the emulator picks has every entry; new paths are checked for crc32 collisions). New graph nodes get stable GUIDs, so rebuilds are identical.

- `Characters page` (`characters_page`): L/ZL on Key Items -> Key Items' exit animation (`CloseAllMinusMenuContentScreen` with `IsCloseImportantToChallenge`), "tab left" sound, loads our screen -> `Prev`. `MinusMenu.module` routes `Prev` of both ImportantMenu nodes to a new module node `CharacterMenu.module` (Next -> ImportantMenu node 20, Main -> 4, PouchMenu -> 24, Event -> 12). `Sequence/CharacterMenu.module.ainb`: opens screen `CharacterMenu_00` with `InFromChallenge`, applies env set "Pouch", display type 0 (3D Link); R/ZR -> `InChallenge`, close + unload -> `Next`; B/− (if closable) -> `Main`; `MinusMenuToMain.module` closes + unloads `CharacterMenu_00` (if open) in its `CloseScreenWithPauseMenuBG` step, the same frame as the header and the other pages. New row `CharacterMenu_00` in `RSDB/UIScreen` (placeholder layout `ImportantMenu_01`, no class, draw unit 1, sort key 5000, loaded on demand, `IsHeapReverse`). Y / X (`CHARACTER_BUTTONS`): set `CharacterMenu_SelectedCharacter` to 0 (Link) / 1 (Retro Link) and rebuild the menu's 3D Link, one step per frame (`IsUpdateNextInFrame` false): set `CharacterMenu_KeepUICamera`, display 4, `SeqOneShotDeletePouchPlayer`, wait, `SeqOneShotCreatePouchPlayer` child/adult by `Sequence_IsChildhood`, wait, `SeqSyncPlayerEquipmentAndGameData` pouch only, `WaitEquip.module`, `SeqOneShotCreateUICamera` if the area uses one (a no-op while the camera is kept), wait, display 0, clear the keep flag; checked after B/−/R/ZR. Works in game (snaildev, 2026-11-09), with a dark overlay while the view is hidden. For other graphs (the UI sandbox) `character_swap_steps()` returns the same steps as one nested sequence that runs one step per frame whatever the caller's sequence does. Without display 4 (tried on D-pad Left / Right) the overlay looked the same, so it is not the hidden view. The overlay is the floor spotlight of the menu scene turning black from the moment the old Link is deleted until the new one shows (same shape and soft edge as the spotlight; snaildev, 2026-11-09): the menu lighting needs Link present, most likely his shadow (no caster -> the spot reads as fully shadowed). With set / delete / clear of the keep flag in one frame the camera was still deleted, so the steps run one per frame and the flag stays set until Link is shown.

- `Character models` (`character_models`, flag + models + exe patch, separate so it can be switched off alone): GameData Int `CharacterMenu_SelectedCharacter` (hash 0xd6b580ea, saved in progress.sav, ResetTypeValue 8, default 0), appended to the GameDataList in place with the save-size bookkeeping. Retro Link = the N64 model mods from GameBanana (snaildev's downloads `n64_young_link.rar`, `n64_link.rar`; copied to `assets\models\N64 Young Link` / `N64 Link`): `Model/Player_Child_Retro.Player_Child.bfres` and `Player_Adult_Retro.Player_Adult.bfres`, with their own copies of the textures the mods replaced (`Mt_Vest_Player_Child_*` -> `Mt_Vest_Player_Retro_*`, `Mt_Angin_Acs_Player_Adult_*` -> `Mt_Angin_Acs_Player_Retro_*`, renamed inside the model and texture files), so the game's Link is untouched. Exe patch: the 11 calls that format `Model/%s.%s.bfres` go through a hook that formats `Player_X_Retro` for `Player_Child` / `Player_Adult` when the flag is 1 (listing in `build\Character models.listing.txt`). Second flag `CharacterMenu_KeepUICamera` (Bool, not saved): while set, a patch at 0x80c35c64 skips the camera part of the game's "delete the menu Link" (only the Characters page sets it, around its delete step). Third flag `CharacterMenu_IsPageOpen` (Bool, not saved), set when the Characters page opens and cleared on R/ZR and in `MinusMenuToMain`'s step that hides the 3D view (display 5). While set, a patch at 0x8289d1b8 in the 3D UI camera update (`view_hook`; the update loads the menu manager's camera block +0x22c + 0x38 * display type: position, aim point, up, near, far, fovy and 8 more bytes) puts Link on the right third for the UI (snaildev, 2026-11-09). `VIEW = "lens"` (default): the 8 bytes after fovy are the **projection offset** (x, y floats, copied to the camera at +0xcc after the aspect ratio); an offset o moves the picture by -2 * o half screen widths (in game: +1/6 put Link on the left third, -1/6 a little left of the right third), so -(1/3 + 0.07 / 1.412) / 2 = -0.1915 puts Link's origin on the right-third line with the camera where the game has it (Link looks exactly as in the game's own view). `VIEW = "camera"` (passed in game first): camera and aim point moved by -0.541 m instead (half screen width at Link = tan(10 deg) * 4.504 * 16/9 = 1.412 m); Link is then seen from 7 degrees to the side. Neither moves the page's "light circle": that is 2D (see the layouts section). Tried and dropped: shifting the menu 3D layer's viewport with `OneShotLayerViewport` from the 3D UI camera's AI (213,0-1493,720): Link was not on the third (looked further away and tilted down) and the + menu and other 3D views stayed shifted.

- Character transition, first version (2026-11-10, seen in game): slide by the page view's picture offset, swap out of sight. snaildev: the slide shows, but (1) the light circle goes black between out and in, (2) the backdrop slides and seems to rotate, (3) the NPC build's zoom jumps while the old character leaves. Cause of 1 and 2: the backdrop is a child actor of the menu character (`PouchPlayerParam.BGActor` = `Obj_PlusMenuBackground`, in the `PouchPlayer_*` packs): it is deleted with him and it is part of the 3D picture.
- Character transition, second version (2026-11-10, seen in game): a real crossfade, D-pad cycle only (`slide=True`; `build.py --npcs`). (a) `CharacterMenu_Slide` = 200, then the game's delete node: the patch on its "delete slot 0" call (0x80c35c60) moves the character's handle from menu slot 0 to slot 1 (the + menu's item preview, unused here; emptied first) and deletes nothing. (b) The stored character is set and the new one created into slot 0 as usual; while it loads the page view patch holds it 1.6 m left of its place. (c) `CharacterMenu_Slide` = 1: the patch counts to 10, one per camera update, and each time sets both characters' positions with the game's setter 0x80964e70(actor, &vec3, 1) (the one `ExecuteMenuActorRotate` uses): old + 2.0 m * t^3, new - 1.6 m * (1 - t)^3 around the menu pivot (`ActorBasePos`: child (0, 0.3, 0), adult (0.1, -0.2, 0); age byte at menu manager +0x37a); on the first frame it calls "set visible" 0x80d2a3c0(manager, 1, 0). The vignette's dark sides are the fade. (d) `SeqOneShotDeletePouchAssignableItem` (deletes slot 1), flag 0. The zoom follows a new unsaved Int `CharacterMenu_ViewCharacter`, which the patch copies from the stored character at rest and from the middle of a slide (in `--npcs` the NPC session's `zoom_hook` / `check_zoom_hook` are called with `build.CHARACTER_FLAG` swapped to that name). All of it is run in unicorn at build time (`check_menu_hooks`: rest, other views, a whole transition frame by frame with registers and stack checked, both delete patches). Seen in game (snaildev, plus a `livetrace.py` recording of one press): the black circle is gone and the old character slides off smoothly, but the new one never shows until the page is left and entered again, and the camera dips at the press. Causes: (1) "set visible" did nothing: during the actors' update the game refuses handle access to other actors (0x8022e220 / 0x8022fbb0 return null while the actor manager's phase byte [+0x44c] is 2 or 4), and the page view patch runs inside the camera actor's update; the new character stayed asleep (state 6, never placed, position requests never applied). (2) "character created" (0x80c36870, called by the manager's update when slot 0's actor arrives) resets camera block 0 to the actor's default view, and the manager's per-frame 0x80b9a990 then glides it back to the equipment view over about half a second. Also seen: the backdrop is not deleted with the moved character, only by "delete slot 0"; the stash had deleted the + menu's item preview in slot 1.
- Character transition, third version (2026-11-10, passed in game: snaildev "Excellent", one small blip left, next entry): (a) as before, but the old character is parked in menu slot 4 (`STASH_SLOT`; slots 4 and 5 are empty in the − menu and no display type touches them). (b) New patch in "character created" (0x80c36924 -> `keep_block`): while `CharacterMenu_Slide` is not 0 the camera block is left alone. (c) After the new character is created and equipped the sequence sets display type 0: the game itself wakes, places and shows slot 0 (and snaps the camera to where it already is). While the flag is still 200 the page view patch keeps the new one hidden (it clears the actor's "shown" bit, +0x4ac bit 0x10000, with the same atomic instruction the game uses) and requests its place 1.6 m to the left every frame. (d) Flag 1: the patch sets the "shown" bit and slides both as before. (e) Flag 201 (`SLIDE_DROP`) and the delete node again: the patch deletes slot 4 instead of slot 0; flag 0. Emulated at build time including the hide / show bit, the drop and the camera block patch. In the recording the wake did discard the pending position request (the new character stood at its place for the frame it woke in), and snaildev saw no flash there, so clearing the "shown" bit in the same frame is enough.
- Character transition, shadow and zoom (2026-11-10, passed in game: snaildev "basically working perfect"). snaildev after version 3: for a few frames after the press the top of the character's shadow is cut off; Zelda's shadow stays cut off and the lighting shifts for a frame at the press; idea: zoom out during the transition for taller characters. (1) "Create the menu character" (0x80c38510) ends by overriding the menu scene's depth shadow range (near 1.7, far 6.0 instead of the scene config's 2.0 .. 8.4, metres from the camera) until the actor has arrived, about ten frames: new patch (0x80c38654 -> `keep_shadow`), not while `CharacterMenu_Slide` is not 0. (2) The zoom existed already (the NPC session's page zoom: camera position and aim point scaled about the feet by head height / Link's head height, Zelda 1.645 for a child save) and cut over in the middle of the slide. In `build.py --npcs` it is now `zoom_blend_hook` (same hook place and table): at rest the factor of `CharacterMenu_ViewCharacter`, during the count a smoothstep from that character's factor to the stored character's (the view patch now copies the view character at rest only). (3) With the camera pulled back, a tall character and its shadow lie beyond the config's far distance: the zoom patch scales the range with the factor through the game's own override (flag 2 in the scene's +0xd14, the two floats), and puts the config's numbers back with the flag off when the factor is 1 again. Emulated (`check_zoom_blend`: every table index at rest bit for bit as the NPC session's check, slides between sizes, the scene's numbers). In the recording the override holds as long as we write it; our display type 0 in the middle of a transition does put the config range back for one frame (seen when leaving Zelda: flags 0004, 2.0 / 8.4 for one sample), which snaildev did not notice. NPC session: `--npcs` replaces your `zoom_hook` / `check_zoom_hook` by these and uses your `zoom_factors()`, `menu_pivots()`, `EXPERIMENT_BASE`, `EXPERIMENTS`; your own build is as it was.
- Character transition, way out at once (2026-11-10, passed in game: snaildev "working perfect", the cloth physics react to the fling, but it "feels a bit unnatural"). snaildev: the character stood still for half a second after the press, then a very snappy slide; start the way out at once and smooth it. Where the time goes (recording): press, +0.05 s parked, +0.10 s create asked, +0.30 s actor there, +0.61 s equipment done (`WaitEquip.module`), +0.66 s slide. Now the old character leaves by itself from the frame it is parked: each camera update the patch reads where it stands (actor +0x31c) and asks for offset * 1.15 + 0.007 m (`SLIDE_OUT_GROW`, `SLIDE_OUT_START`: 6 cm after 0.1 s, in the dark after 0.33 s, stopped past 2 m after 28 frames), no count needed; the new one's slide in is 16 frames (was 10) and starts the frame after the display-type call (the two-frame wait is gone); the zoom glides with the slide in. Emulated with the position setter applied between frames.
- Slide styles (2026-11-10, compared in game; the switching is gone again, see the next entry). snaildev wants to compare typical slide transitions. `build.SLIDE_STYLES` (filled by `slide_styles()`): 1 the fling that passed, 2 carousel ease-in-out 0.4 s, 3 carousel ease-out 0.33 s, 4 carousel CSS `ease` 0.4 s, 5 carousel linear 0.27 s, 6 one after the other (ease-in out at once, ease-out in, 0.3 s each). A carousel moves both characters as one strip (1.9 m) once the new one has loaded, so it starts about 0.6 s after the press. D-pad Up / Down on the Characters page pick the style (unsaved Int `CharacterMenu_SlideStyle`; a tab sound when it changes, the other tab sound on arriving at style 1, silence at either end). With styles the page view patch takes both offsets from a table (`style_rows`: per style the two distances, a "together" byte and two rows of 40 fractions in 4.12 fixed point): the new character's frame is the slide count (now up to 39; the zoom still glides over the first 16), the old one's is either the same (carousel) or a new unsaved Int `CharacterMenu_SlideOut` that the patch counts from the frame the old one is parked and zeroes at rest. A new curve is a line of Python, no new code. Without styles (plain build, the other sessions' builds) the patch is byte for byte the one that passed (checked against the NPC build's files). The tables and the zoom factors sit in the padding after `.rodata` (`exepatch.RODATA_CAVE`, `Patcher.rodata()`: main's NSO header says rodata ends at 0x8486c080 and data starts at 0x8486d000), which freed 0x16c bytes of the code cave. Seen in game by that round: the padding after `.rodata` is readable (page, zoom and all six styles worked). snaildev's verdict: style 6 "a clear winner"; 1 and 6 were the only ones without a wait, and 1 (the fling) had a slight stutter.
- **The full build** (2026-11-10, passed in game, snaildev: every point right, response to A immediate; `mods\build.py`, what `install-mods.bat` installs now). snaildev: roll with style 6 and fold in the UI session's work. `build.py` = the Characters page is the UI session's picker, all 100 characters of the NPC session's ring, A runs the slide; `--plain` = the first version (our page, Y / X = Link / Retro Link, no NPCs); `--cycle` = our page with the NPCs and D-pad Left / Right through Link, Saria, Mido, Zelda (for work on the transition); `--npcs` is accepted as the full build's old name. How it is put together: `npc_characters.build_mods()` (its model hook; the page zoom is our `zoom_blend_hook`), then `picker_page()` = `ui_sandbox` as a library, exactly the calls of its own `main()` (`build_custom_parts`, `Picker`, `make_picker_layout`, `check_picker`, `sandbox`, `picker_graph(..., stick=True)`, `stick_patch`, `check_stick_patch`) with `ui_sandbox.BUILD_UI` = `build\`. The picker's files come out byte for byte as in the UI sandbox's tested build (layout archive, category list, screen table, messages, the stick's code patch; checked against `build-ui\`); the stick patch is the Characters page folder's own `.ips` now, and the two test folders `Character layout (test)` and `Character stick (test)` are retired (removed from the emulator on install: the first would override the picker's layout). Only the page graph differs from the sandbox's: the swap. The picker asks for `build._swap_step_list(g, 0)`, takes its first step as the store and runs the rest after a store per character; with `build.SWAP_SLIDES` that rest is one nested sequence, the slide. Three things had to change for that. (1) The picker's own check allows no "set an Int" node but the store, so the sequence moves `CharacterMenu_Slide` only by adding (`t_add_int`: +200, later -199) and never sets a drop value: the delete patch parks on 200, deletes the parked one on any other value but 0, and the page view patch ends the transition itself when it finds a count and nobody parked (flag and out-count to 0; this also puts a flag that is out of step right). (2) The picker stores the number a few frames before the swap starts, so the view character (the zoom) no longer follows the stored number at rest: it is copied when a menu character is created outside a transition ("character created" patch) and when a transition ends. (3) One transition only, style 6, as a single table row (`SLIDE_STYLE`); the formulas, the style flag and D-pad Up / Down are gone, every build (also the other sessions' own) has the table patch. Steps of the swap now: both flags in one frame, park, create, wait, equipment, display type 0, +(-199), wait 21 frames, delete (drop), keep flag off. From the press: the picker's own three frames (Decide, store, sound), ours two, then the old one moves. Emulated: the whole transition and the end / out-of-step cases (`check_menu_hooks`), the picker graph through the UI session's own model with our swap in it (32,954 presses). Confirmed in game by that round: the flags set in the same frame one frame before the park, and the Add node inside the picker.
- Neutral pose and page turns (2026-11-10, passed in game, snaildev: "All good" on every point: neutral on entry and after picks, the cursor keeps its slot, the + menu shows its own pose again; so the category alone is enough and the selector with plain children works). (1) snaildev: every character on the Characters page stands in the pose of whatever was hovered last on the + menu's equipment page (a shield: turned round; a sword: the draw; a tunic: neutral, which is what we want). The + menu stores the hovered item's category and name in the object at [0x84e98e28] (GOT 0x84c9e0d0): category +0x7c8, name string +0x6c8, written by 0x80a89ab0 on a hover (item record by name 0x805aed10, its category 0x8015fdd0) and never reset. Readers of the category: the menu character's AI (`AI/PouchPlayer.root.ainb` in the `PouchPlayer_*` packs: `ExecuteMenuActorRotate` 0x80590c50 turns him, `ShieldRotate` -120 degrees in `PouchPlayerParam`; `ExecuteSetPouchPlayerASBBParameter` / `QueryPouchPlayerASInfo` pick `PouchWait` or `PouchTurn` and the animation's parameters, 0x80a98d40) and the manager's 0x80b9a990 (copies it to +0x320 and picks the camera pair: Agony, Armor, Boot, HeadArmor, Shield, Weapon). Category 4 = armour (seen live with `Obj_Armor_Child_Normal`), 9 = a shield. The page view patch now writes category 4 while a − menu page shows (display types 2 and 3, so it is in place before our page wakes the character, and our page itself), keeps the + menu's own category in a new unsaved Int `CharacterMenu_PoseCategory` (category + 1) and gives it back the first time a + menu view shows (display type 1, or 0 off our page) unless it was changed meanwhile. Only the category is written, not the name. Not knowable offline: whether the category alone gives exactly the tunic's pose, and whether the + menu shows its old pose again without a hover. (2) ZL / ZR keep the cursor's slot (the same column in the last row the sheet has, where it ends earlier): `build.picker_graph` is now our copy of the UI session's `picker_graph` with that change, checked by its own model through a subclass of its `Picker` (`turn_to`, `after`). The orange slot after a turn is one saved-number selector per sheet used as a step inside the turn sequences, with the `Mark` stops as its children; the sandbox had it under a root node with jumps (new shape in game: an `Element_S32Selector` whose children are plain one-shot nodes). Graph 2,703 nodes. To fit the pose code, every table of ours left the code cave for the padding after `.rodata`; the UI sandbox's own build (the NPC session's original zoom and its table in the cave) fits with 4 bytes to spare, so the next routine there needs the tables of `npc_characters.zoom_hook` moved or the sandbox retired.
- Zoom on the + menu's equipment page (2026-11-10, passed in game). snaildev: as child Link, adult and other tall characters are cut off on the equipment screen. `zoom_blend_hook` no longer needs the page flag: it scales every menu-character view (display type 0), so the + menu's six category camera pairs (Agony, Armor, Boot, HeadArmor, Shield, Weapon) are scaled about the feet by the same factor, and the shadow range with them. Off our page a factor of 1 touches nothing (not even an override the game set itself), so Link's + menu is as it was. The UI sandbox's own build still uses the NPC session's page-only zoom.
- Portraits in the picker's slots (2026-11-10, passed in game on the second build: snaildev "Everything is perfect", the equipment page zoom with it). The NPC session's pictures (`build-npc\portraits\cut-fit\<nn>_<name>.png`, read at build time; 99 of 100, 25 Biggoron missing) are scaled to 256 x 256 (premultiplied for the scaling, then straight alpha again with the colour carried on into the transparent part, as the game's icons have it), encoded as ASTC 4x4 sRGB and added to a copy of the layout archive's texture file as `CharacterPortrait_000` ... `_099` plus `CharacterPortrait_None`, a plain head-and-shoulders shape for a character without a picture (`build.portrait_textures`, `tools\bntxwrite.py`). The picker layout gets one picture pane per character, `P_Face_ccc` (232 x 232 at its slot's centre), as the last children of its sheet's container `N_Sheet_ss`: the sheets come after the slot parts, so a picture is drawn over the slot's background and cursor, which is where the inventory slot draws its item icon too (`PaItemMenuBtn_00`: `N_Item_00` after the cursor panes), and the `Sheet` animation already shows and hides them (`build.add_portraits`, on the UI session's layout after its own check). First look (snaildev, 2026-11-10): the game takes the larger texture file and draws the panes at the right height and place, but four times too wide and grey. Two faults of ours, both fixed and now asserted in the build: the pane record was a copy of the page's `P_Element_00`, which is a window (pane +0x3c has a window extension index: the texture as a stretched frame), and the texture records took the component selectors of the template texture, grey + alpha (02 02 02 05; colour is 02 03 04 05). Now the pane record, material, shader pass and picture record are all the game's icon picture's (`PaReplaceIcon_00`: `P_ReplaceIcon_00`, one texture, clamp, palette white / transparent black, the same indices in both layouts), texture name ours. Layout 682 KB, 485 panes (the limit for animated panes is 512). Shipped as `Characters page\romfs\UI\Layout2Archive\Common...bntx.zs`: 251 MB unpacked, 78 MB packed (level 6; kept in `build\_cache` by content: a first build takes about three minutes, later ones 20 s); size table entry by the stock rule. It is the file the button prompt mods replace, so one of those and ours cannot both be on. Why not the game's own icon files: `UI/Tex/Icon/<name>.bntx` are bound to `PaReplaceIcon_00` by item code (no node, no layout data names them). Offline: the game's 878 textures untouched in the new file, ours decoded again and compared with the pictures, the layout read back. Not knowable offline: that a picture pane with a material transplanted from another layout draws, and that the game takes the larger texture file.
- Retro Link is not in the picker (snaildev, 2026-11-10: it is somebody else's model mod, not credited yet; it is to come back under a section of its own for mods). `build.PICKER_HIDDEN` = [1]: the stored numbers stay as they are (saves, the model hook and the portraits go by them), the picker's list leaves the number out, so a place in the list and the stored number are two things (`Picker.number`). The graph is built and checked by place (the UI session's model), then `_renumber_characters` puts the stored numbers into the store nodes and the selectors' cases before it is written, and the file read back is checked with the numbers taken back to places. A save that has the hidden number shows that character and no orange slot. 99 characters, 481 panes, 2,671 nodes; built and checked offline, not yet seen in game. The Retro Link model files are still built into `Character models` (the `--plain` build's Y / X use them); nothing in the full build offers them.
- The field swap in the full build (2026-11-10, snaildev: "we now have a proper field swap, pull in the changes"; built and checked offline, waiting for his look in the normal emulator). `build.py` uses `mods\field_swap.py` as a library (`build.field_swap_hooks`), its code unchanged, and does for `build\` what that module's own `main()` does for `build-field\`: its unsaved Bool `CharacterMenu_FieldSwapPending`, its three wrappers around our functions (the picker's A also sets the flag; the screen table gets the fade screen's row; the menu-closing graph gets the swap) and then its code patch and its own checks (`field_patch`, `check_patch`, `check_graphs`). The swap's graph and patch stay in their own folder, `Character field swap`, as tested: an override folder that sorts before ours; switched off in the Add-Ons list, the rest is the build without the swap. `install-mods.bat` now installs five folders. `--no-field-swap` builds without it and removes that folder on install (so do `--plain` and `--cycle`). Offline: the five folders of the merged build are byte for byte the ones of the field session's own build (which is what passed in its emulator copy), a `--no-field-swap` build is byte for byte what was installed before, the patch shares no byte with our patches (`write_ips`), and the three other sessions' dry runs pass. When `mods\field_swap.py` itself runs `build.main()`, the build sees that it is hooked already (`close_page_with_menu` is not its own function) and leaves the patch and the checks to that module.
- Menu actor manager slots (6 of 0x20 from +0x28: pending pointer, vtable, link +0x10, id +0x18, flag byte +0x1c): display type N shows slot N and hides slots 0-3 otherwise (0x80e01af0 -> 0x80d2a3c0(manager, visible, slot)), only when the type is set. Cave use now: shared GameData routines (`gd_bool`, `gd_int`) and the slot patch of "delete the menu Link" in 0x83c66280-0x83c66400 (`CAVE_EXTRA`, 0x24 bytes left); the camera part of that patch and the page view's two routines in 0x83c66ea0-0x83c67000 (`CAVE_EXTRA2`, the unused end of Header four tabs' part, 4 bytes left); the two "created" patches and the zoom in the patch's own part, which in the NPC build's layout (0x83c66400-0x83c66c00) has 0x4c bytes left: the padding after `.text` is nearly used up, the next routine needs another home. More of the manager (2026-11-10, code + live): the backdrop's handle is at +0x208 (same layout as a slot); 0x80d2a3c0 "set visible" for slot 0 sets or clears the actor's "shown" bit (actor +0x4ac, bit 0x10000) and wakes the actor through 0x80d2afa0 if its state [+0x2c] is 6 (asleep) or 3, or puts it to sleep through 0x80d2ac80 if it is 4 (awake), and does the same to the backdrop; the wake places the actor at `ActorBasePos` / `ActorBaseRot` of its MenuActorParam (flag 0x4000 in actor +0x4a8 until its next update). Display type 0 (0x80e01dd4) = show slot 0, hide 1-3, 0x80b9a990(manager, 1) (camera block 0 := the view for the current equipment category, +0x320 / name at +0x328; with 0 it glides there; its other caller is 0x833b4d00 in the manager's update), +0x31c = 1, camera state 3. A menu character is created asleep and hidden (0x80c36870, which also makes the backdrop at the character's position if +0x208 is empty). Actor transform: home position +0x2ec, home rotation 3x3 +0x2f8, position +0x31c, rotation 3x3 +0x328, scale +0x34c, pending position request +0x358 (x = NaN: none; flag 0x8000 in +0x4a8; applied on the actor's next update, never while asleep), pending rotation +0x364. Frame order seen in the recording: sequence nodes, then the actors' update (characters, then the camera with our patch), then drawing; the game runs these at 60 a second in the menu. The menu's 3D scene and its shadow (2026-11-10): [0x84eb50a8] holds the scenes (count +0x50, array +0x58, 0x60 each); scene 1 is the menu's ("UI" config, `gsys\ModelSceneConfigParam\UI...bgyml`: one depth shadow cascade, `DepthShadowNear00` 2.0, `DepthShadowFar0` 8.4; scene 0, the field, has 0.1 / 9.8 / 32.3 / 160). Scene +0xd60 -> +0x250 cascade count n, +0x330 -> floats ([0] near, [n] far); scene +0xd14 a flag word, bit 2 = range overridden by game code. 0x80ea3a30 (run by 0x831dfe10 when a display mode was requested: byte +0x2e6 of [0x84c9e740], written by the display-type call and by "character created") clears the flag, which brings the config's numbers back.
- `Character layout (test)` (`character_layout_test`, in `EXPERIMENTS`): a test folder. Mod folders are layered in name order and the first folder that has a file wins (yuzu LayeredFS: `patch_manager.cpp` sorts by name, `vfs_layered.cpp` takes the first hit), so this folder sorts before the others and carries its own versions of the files it changes; switched off in the emulator's Add-Ons list, everything is as before. It gives the Characters page its own layout `CharacterMenu_00` (`page_layout()`: Key Items' layout with only values changed) with the light on Link: window `W_Window_00` moved to (640, -150) (the right-third line; 150 below the screen centre so the light reaches his feet), scaled x 1.2745 to cover the screen and y 1.3117 for the height, its mask texture map set to scale x -1.7206, translate x 0.3603. Half-dark box: x -160..1440, y -996..696 (half width 800, half height 850; Key Items: 1080 x 648). Offline check of the darkness (child Link: soles 877 below the screen centre, head about 610 above): soles 0.21, boot tops 0, top of the head 0.28, floor 300 right of him 0.32; fully dark left of x = -356; the bottom screen edge under him is 65 % dark. First cut (centre (640, 0), 700 x 648, scale y 1): soles 0.96. Files: layout archive (+ 4 copies of the layout), `UI/LayoutArchiveCategory` (name added under Common), `RSDB/UIScreen` (our screen -> our layout), Mals US/EU (`LayoutMsg/CharacterMenu_00.msbt` = copy of Key Items'), and the combined size table (identical in all folders). Recipe from the UI sandbox. Passed in game with the first cut (snaildev, 2026-11-09: "looks perfect", other screens fine; lower part of the boots and his floor shadow in the dark, hence the larger, lower second cut). It stays its own override folder for now: every on/off combination of the folders is consistent that way.
- `Header four tabs` (`header_four_tabs`, exe patch + text + layout, separate so it can be switched off alone): the − header shows Characters, Key Items, Threads of Time, Map. Our page opens the overlay with preset 1337, which the patch maps to category 7 (highlights the Characters tab) and to the − tab sound set (2). The − layout comes from the end of layout animation `PlusToMinus` (tabs at x -520/0/520, tab 3 hidden); its constants are changed in place to the + layout (-690/-230/230, tab 3 visible). Without this mod the page still works (preset 20 is ignored). The patch listing is written to `build\Header four tabs.listing.txt`.
- Way back (Characters -> Key Items, R/ZR): overlap exit — start our fade-out and go to Key Items at once; Key Items' main loop closes our screen after `InChallenge` ends (a `SeqSelectorScreenOpenCloseState` child added to its node 3). Fixed the blink (snaildev, 2026-11-09); camera states 4/5 made no difference.
- Tab highlight shapes (header mod, in-place layout edits): `PaMenuCategory_00` has `P_MinusMenuBg_00` (orange material, textures `MenuHeaderSelect_04-06` = left/middle/right of a 3-tab bar) and `P_PlusMenuBg_00` (beige material, `_00-03` = 4-tab bar); `Type` shows one, `MinusMenuHeaderState` / `PlusMenuHeaderState` (texture pattern; frame = shape) pick the shape, and `MenuOverlay_00` sets each tab's frame (tab 3: − 0, + 3). The textures are masks (R = B, colour from the material). Edit: − material gets its own copy of the + texture list (appended after the string section at 0x3e20; file and section sizes grown, archive repacked with alignment 16), − state animation 0..3 (length 3, keys 0,1,2,3,3), tab 3's − frame 3.
- **Never share data between two references in a `.buil`**: the game relocates pointers by walking every reference, so shared data is relocated twice (seen live: list entries = 2*base + offset; both menus lost their tab highlights).
- Verified in game (2026-11-09): four tabs, orange 4-tab highlight shapes, "Threads of Time" text fits, − tab sound, no blink either way.
- Known, not ours: text corruption (Map tab -> Y world map -> back, or world map -> − close: garbled text and speech bubbles) happens with both mods off too (Fritchii 5.5), so it is the game or the emulator.

- The picker freeze (2026-11-12; in v0.1.0 and v0.1.1). After minutes of page turns and cursor moves in the picker the game froze, three times, once in under three minutes of only that. Each time the game's text routine (0x81535660; per glyph, records of 0xa0 bytes in the chunk tables at text object +0x20, quads of 0xe0 at +0x28, count at +0x9c) drew one of the picker's texts whose chunk tables were zero while the count was not (twice a detail text `T_CellText_ccc`), wrote to addresses near zero and called a null pointer at 0x81535980 (emulator log: "Unmapped Write ... @ 0x0...", then "Cannot execute instruction at unmapped address 0x0"). The game's committed memory was flat in the reproducing run, so it is not memory growing. The emulator's line "Accessing an unallocated region of a SparseLargeVector" before the first such write is a consequence (logged at the first access to page 0), not a cause. What stopped it: the 99 detail texts shipped empty (6,930 of the page's 9,299 characters of text); with only that changed snaildev could not freeze it. So the cause is tied to how much text the page holds or shows, but where the limit is was not found (0x8152f8c0 and 0x81531140 fill the chunk tables; 0x81531140, called only from 0x815355dc, gets each table and chunk from the allocation callback stored at 0x84cb7070, through the thunk 0x81549540, with the heap taken from its third argument's +0x20; the heap itself is unread, and a watcher of its free space during a page-turn run is the next step). The text corruption snaildev sees at the same time is not this: it is the emulator's picture (clean again after the menu is closed and opened), while the freeze is the game's own text object. `build.PICKER_DETAIL_TEXTS = False` ships them empty; the panes stay. If the freeze comes back with more or longer names, start there.
- The lite build (2026-11-12; the default of `mods\build.py` since then, `--full` builds all 99 as before, byte for byte what was installed that morning). `build.PICKER_ROSTER` lists 21 stored numbers in the picker's order (Link, Zelda (child), Saria, Mido, Malon (child), Ruto (child); Zelda, Sheik, Impa, Ganondorf, Malon, Ruto; Rauru, King Zora, Nabooru, Darunia, Medigoron, Biggoron; Hylia_MM_002, Hylia_MU_005, Hylia_WN_008): two sheets, 18 and 3. Only the picker's list, the portraits and the model files shrink (`build.lite_roster`: our `write` leaves out the 156 model files of the other 78 characters, the NPC session's code is used unchanged); stored numbers, the code patches and their tables are those of the ring of 100, so rotating characters in and out is an edit of that list. Sizes: `NPC characters` 78 MB (255 full), `Characters page` 73 MB (76 full: the texture file is mostly the game's own textures, the portraits are a small part), all folders with the voices about 178 MB (368 full). A page turn from a column the next sheet does not have goes to that sheet's last slot (`Picker.turn_to`; with 99 characters every sheet had every column). Limit: a save whose stored number is outside the roster asks for a model file nobody ships (the NPC session's check refuses exactly that; the lite build tells it which files are left out on purpose); what the game does then is unseen, so pick a roster character and save before switching builds. The build's own time is unchanged (30 s cached); the copy into the mods folder is what gets shorter.
- `UI sandbox` (`mods\ui_sandbox.py`, `install-ui.bat`, separate from `install-mods.bat`): builds every mod of `build.py` into `build-ui\` (the normal `build\` is untouched) and replaces the Characters page's screen and graph with our own, all in the left two thirds (x <= 260; Link stands at x = 640). Two builds: the character picker (default) and the sample pages (`--samples`). **Character picker**, fourth version (passed in game, snaildev 2026-11-10: selector animations perfect, orange highlight in, grid alignment perfect, slot size right; but the Equipment page's "square" is a trapezium, narrower at the bottom with slanted sides. So in game these work: screen animations that are played and run a part's own animation, a looping one, several screen animations driving the same part animation, a 2,378 node graph, one saved-number query and selector per sheet, non-uniform part scale. Fifth version (the slot taken from the inventory, `PaItemMenuBtn_00`, at scale 1, no squash, as a shape test with `ItemPattern` 9 / 4 / 23 per row; the `IsCurrent` hold gone, the orange is the pass's own default): snaildev chose the top row, `ItemPattern` 9 (`MenuBtnBase_13`), "perfect". Sixth version (passed in game, snaildev 2026-11-10: title line and glyphs look right, ZL / ZR turn pages, + opens the + menu and the way back is fine, R / L to and from Key Items work, the left stick moves like the D-pad and "felt great" over a longer try with no issues): all 18 slots `ItemPattern` 9. Over the grid, `T_Title_00` "Character Select" from the grid's left edge and, ending at its right edge, `Pa_KeyZL_00`, the sheet's "page x of y", `Pa_KeyZR_00`; the hint line is gone. The key parts are `PaCharacterKey_00` (= the game's `PaGuideKey_00` with the glyph's text reference `*` and its guide text hidden) at scale 0.7; their messages are the game's own glyph characters, read at build time from `StaticMsg/GuideKeyIcon.msbt` (see the button glyph facts in the layouts section). Controls: ZL / ZR turn the sheet (ZR no longer leaves for Key Items; R does), X / Y do nothing, + opens the + menu (`SeqExecuteCloseAndUnloadScreen` on our screen, display type 4, the page view flag off, command `PouchMenu`, which `characters_page` already routes like Key Items'; the current category stays Key Items, so - from the + menu comes back there). Left stick: folder `Character stick (test)` (sorts before `Characters page`, overrides its graph while switched on; `--no-stick` leaves it out) with a graph whose four directions come from a `SeqQueryControllerReleaseMaskChecker` fed with the trigger mask, and a code patch that makes that class's four direction outputs read D-pad or left stick (see the controller facts under the - menu; 6 small patches, cave 0x83c661d0-0x83c661ec: the padding before the emulator's Dynamic FPS routine, keep other code out of 0x83c661d0-0x83c66200). Offline: the routine run in unicorn stock and patched for 250 masks (stock, every output follows its own sead bit; patched, only the four directions change), the test graph through the same model run. Confirmed in game by that round: the game builds `SeqQueryControllerReleaseMaskChecker` from a graph although no stock graph has one, and the UI controller's trigger mask carries the left stick bits 20-23. For a merge into the normal build (everything is in `mods\ui_sandbox.py`, section "the character picker" and "the left stick"): `make_picker_layout` + `PICK_PARTS` (layout and the two parts), `picker_graph(mod, pk, stick=True)` (the graph; with the stick proven there is no reason to keep the D-pad-only variant or the test folder), `stick_patch()` / `check_stick_patch()` (the code patch and its unicorn check; its cave 0x83c661d0-0x83c66200 is outside `exepatch.CAVE_REGION`, widened for the moment as the NPC build does), `page_graph(picker=True)` (exits: R, +, B / -), `sandbox()` (archive, category list, screen table, messages), and the offline checks `check_picker`, `check_graph`, `check_picker_graph`. The fourth version's request, snaildev: square slots, 3 rows of 6, the grid from the left edge to the screen's centre with equal margins, the texts right of the character, no diamonds: the chosen slot turns from shadow to orange as on the Equipment page, and the cursor pulses and plays its selection animations): 6 sheets of 18 slots of our part `PaCharacterSlot_00` (= `PaEquipmentBtn_00` with initial `ItemPattern` 8, the square, plus an animation `IsChosen`: `P_Base_00` off, `P_Equip_00` on), drawn 264 x 264 (part scale 0.742 x 0.653: the game's box is 356 x 404), pitch 288 x 400, names under the slots at size 30, name and caption at x = 1380. State animations stopped at a frame: `Sheet`, `Det` (detail texts by slot), `Cur` (cursor at once: `Select` at its end on one slot, at its start on the others), `Mark` (the orange slot, frame 18 = none; also holds every slot's `IsCurrent` at 0, which is what makes `P_Equip_00` orange). Played animations that run a part animation from start to end: `In_jj` / `Out_jj` / `Dec_jj` (`Select` / `UnSelect` / `Decide` of slot jj) and the looping `Pulse` (one 40 frame period of every slot's `CursorLoop`). A D-pad move stops the target's `Out` at its end, plays the source's `Out` and the target's `In`; anything that places the cursor at once (entry, after a swap, X / Y) first stops every `Out` at its end and every `In` at its start, so nothing that is still running can change the picture afterwards. X / Y ask for the saved number (one query + `Element_S32Selector` per sheet) to light the orange slot only when the chosen character is on the new sheet. Layout 585 KB, 383 panes, 69 animations; graph 2,378 nodes, 736 KB; `check_picker_graph` now also models the cursor per slot and what is still running, and runs a second press after every first one (32,954 runs). The third version below passed in game (snaildev, 2026-11-10: "working great"; so a 595 KB layout with 422 panes and 318 texts, a 1,715 node graph and a 101-way selector all work). **Third version** (equipment slots of sample page B, 2 rows of 3, X / Y to turn pages): the whole ring of `npc_characters.ring()` (100: Link, Retro Link, 98 NPCs) on 17 sheets of six slots. The build goes through `npc_characters.build_mods()` / `check_build()` and installs the folder `NPC characters` too (about 260 MB in the sandbox emulator). A layout cannot change a text and only our graph drives the screen, so every character has three text panes of its own (name under its slot, big name and a caption in the detail block: number, model file, an actor that uses the model) and the state is three independent animations stopped at a frame: `Sheet` (which `N_Sheet_ss` container shows: title, the sheet's names and detail texts, its marker; also hides the slots the last sheet lacks), `Cur` (slot 0-5: `Select` of the six shared `PaEquipmentBtn_00` slots, the focus line, and the visibility of every character's detail texts by its slot, so "focused character's detail" = sheet container x own visibility), `Chosen` (character 0-99, or 100 = none for a saved number outside the list: each sheet has one green diamond `Pa_Mark_ss` in a container that is shown on the chosen one's sheet and moved onto his slot; six empty diamonds are shared). Layout 595 KB (a text pane costs 1.4 KB: its material has five passes), 422 panes (an animation target names its pane in 9 bits, so stay under 512), 318 messages; the long marker curves keep keys only outside runs of zeros (`sparse_state`). Graph 1,715 nodes, 520 KB: a button loop per character; every way into it ends in that character's gate (a sequence with `IsUpdateNextInFrame` false and two `SeqExecuteGenericWaitFrame` 1, the shape sample page F proved), what a move changes runs before it in the frame of the press; D-pad moves inside the sheet (no wrap), X / Y turn the sheet (wraps, cursor to its first slot), A stops `Chosen` at the character, stores the number a frame later, then runs the swap, which exists once (`build._swap_step_list(g, 0)` without its first step, the store; the slide of the main build's new transition is not used, `slide=False`), and after the swap the saved number decides sheet and cursor through the same 101-way `Element_S32Selector` as on entry. Offline checks in the build: `check_picker` (what every sheet / slot / chosen number shows, from the animations as authored; bounds), `check_graph`, and `check_picker_graph`, which runs the graph for every character x every button and for every saved number on entry (4,574 runs, on the node list and again on the written file) against a small model (`Picker.after`). **Sample pages** (second version; passed in game, snaildev 2026-11-10: page opens, all texts there, the focused part takes the game's own selected look, the list scrolls smoothly and is clipped by the scissor, the green diamond follows the choice and Link swaps, pages B to F fine; so in game these work: text reference `*` with labels by pane path, part layouts of our own under new names, state animations stopped at a frame, driving a part's own animation from a screen animation, visibility / translate / scale curves, scissor panes, 154 panes and a 968 node graph; `--samples` still builds this version byte for byte): layout `CharacterMenu_00` = `build.page_layout()` plus our panes under `N_All_00` -> `N_Sandbox_00`; four part layouts of our own (copies of the game's with small edits, 4 archive entries each, listed in the category table): `PaCharacterIndex_00` (Threads of Time list entry: title from our messages, inner gem green and off by default, new animation `IsChosen`), `PaCharacterDot_00` (only that diamond, as a "current character" marker), `PaCharacterBtn_00` (thin button with our text), `PaCharacterScrollBar_00` (sized for the list, new animation `Pos`); messages `LayoutMsg/CharacterMenu_00.msbt` (Key Items' plus one label per text pane path, all references `*`). Pages: A list like Threads of Time (9 rows, 6 visible, scissor, scroll bar, 5-frame scroll moves), B equipment-style slots 3 x 2, C save-file cards, D buttons, E catalogue of the 12 button parts, F catalogue of panels; a detail block (name, the game's ornament line, caption) and a hint line. State is shown with animations stopped at a frame (`Page`, `Chosen`, `Cur_<page>`, `Scroll`; one key per frame) that drive each part's own `Select` / `IsChosen` / `Pos`, plus a focus line of our own under the focused item that does not depend on the parts. Graph: one state per focused item (per item and scroll position on the list), 968 nodes, 284 KB; D-pad moves, A on Link / Retro Link = `build.character_swap_steps` after marking the diamond, X/Y change page, R/ZR Key Items, B/− close; on entry the saved flag decides the diamond and the row the cursor starts on (`Element_S32Selector` on `SeqQueryGetNonStructGameDataInt`, templates MinusMenu node 8 and ResetGimmickImpl node 8). Every state is entered through a sequence with `IsUpdateNextInFrame` false whose second step is `SeqExecuteGenericWaitFrame` 1, so a button press can never be read twice. Offline checks in the build: every state's values from the animations as authored (`check_states`), nothing right of x = 260 or over the header / footer by the parts' footprints (`check_bounds`), no path from a button loop back to one inside a frame, every state reachable, every loop can leave, every requested animation and frame exists (`check_graph`). First version (5 sampler pages, Link hidden on two): the page opened, any D-pad / X / Y press hung the game (emulator log: forced stop of a busy emulation thread): its `loop -> focus -> loop` path never let a frame pass while the trigger was still set. It also had 81 stale indices inside its picture / text records (see the layouts section) and text references `""`, so what it drew and wrote is not evidence of anything. Installs only into the sandbox emulator.

- `NPC characters` (`mods\npc_characters.py`, `install-npc.bat`, own emulator copy `PLAY-debug-npc.bat`; separate from `install-mods.bat`): builds every mod of `build.py` and its test folder into `build-npc\` exactly as the normal build does (`build\` and the normal mods folder are untouched; the build lists every file that differs from what the normal emulator has installed), then makes every humanoid NPC model a character (98 models; `build-npc\characters.txt` has the list with what the rig did to each).
  - Mod folder `NPC characters`: `Model/Player_<Age>_C<nnn>.Player_<Age>.bfres` for both ages = the NPC's own file with the model renamed, one material renamed to `Mt_Hairs` where the model has none (`HAIR_STAND_INS`: `Mt_Hair` in 87 models, `Mt_Teeth` in 9, `Mt_Item_DekuStick` in 1; the player's code needs the name, NPC section) and the skeleton rebuilt by `tools\npcrig.py` (see the NPC section; 460-700 bones instead of 150-370; the game's own largest skeleton is the Ganon boss with 757; 255 MB in all, cached in `build-npc\_cache`), size table entries = the NPC model's own entry + growth + 64 KB; and `RSDB/ActorInfo` with larger heaps for `Player_Child` / `Player_Adult` / `PouchPlayer_Child` / `PouchPlayer_Adult` (in-place edit: `InstanceHeapSize` x2 / x1.6 / x3 / x3, `PreActorRenderInfoHeapSize` 393,216; largest need by the formula in the NPC section: 251,923; both kept near the game's own largest values, 10.2 MB and 464,504).
  - `Character models`: flags and Retro Link as in the normal build, but its exe patch is built again with our model hook instead of the normal one (cave 0x83c66400-0x83c66c00: ours from 0x83c66400, the normal build's part unused). The stored number v (`CharacterMenu_SelectedCharacter`): `1000 + i` = experiment i; else c = v modulo 100, never negative (the list is a ring, any number is valid): 0 Link, 1 Retro Link (the normal build's name table), else NPC c - 2, formatted with `Model/%s_C%03d.%s.bfres` (the format function is variadic: number in w3, fmdb name moved to x4). Experiments (off the ring; Saria): `lean` = rigged without the added Link bones, `raw` = only the model and the hair material renamed. (`stock` = no file of ours, the hook puts the NPC's own name in for project and fmdb: still in the hook, not built any more, since a stock NPC file has no `Mt_Hairs` and must crash.)
  - Characters page view: `zoom_hook` at 0x8289d1f0 (every path of the 3D UI camera update passes there; the normal build's `view_hook` jumps to it): while `CharacterMenu_IsPageOpen` and display type 0, camera position and aim point are scaled about the character's feet (`ActorBasePos` of the menu Link: child (0, 0.3, 0), adult (0.1, -0.2, 0)) by NPC head height / Link head height (table of u16 x 256 per character and age; 1 within 5 %, at most 8): every character fills the frame as Link does. The + menu's views are not touched.
  - As a library (for the UI sandbox or any other build that wants the whole ring): `npc_characters.build_mods()` in place of the loop over `build.MODS` (builds them into `build.BUILD`, `Character models` with our exe patch, then the folder `NPC characters` = `npc_characters.MOD` with the models and heaps; size tables and install stay with the caller, who installs `MOD` too), `check_build(*result)` = the hook and heap checks, `ring()` = the 100 display names by stored number, `characters()` = the NPCs with model stem, label and the actors that use the model. A stored number is set as in the normal build (`build.character_swap_steps(g, number)`). Tried from a scratch build folder with the normal page buttons: models and exe patch identical to `build-npc\`.
  - Portrait run (debug, 2026-11-10, handed over, not yet seen in game): D-pad down on the Characters page shows all 100 characters in turn for the capture tool: backdrop black, then per character the normal swap WITHOUT the equipment step (`SeqSyncPlayerEquipmentAndGameData` replaced by a dummy: no sword and shield; the new menu character exists without equipment for a frame in every normal swap), 150 frames on black, 120 on white, back to black (about 8 minutes). Unrolled in the graph: one nested one-step-per-frame sequence per character under one sequence (a node can have at most 255 children: the child count is a byte in the file); `CharacterMenu.module.ainb` is 748 KB with 2,737 nodes. So that the black and the white picture of a character match, the test build ships the idle and face clips the menu character plays cut to ONE frame (`FROZEN_CLIPS`: `Wait`, `Wait_Sword` in `Player_<Age>_04_Animation`, `Pouch_Wait_LSword` in `Player_Adult_03_Animation`, `Face_Blink`, `Face_Eye_Random` in `Player_<Age>_01_Animation`; only the frame count in the clip header, FSKA +0x40; all were 600 frames = 10 s loops, the blink 15). The menu character's animation graph is `AS/Player_Pouch.root.asb`. A (black) and L (white) now both use our own screen (pure black pane). `tools\npcshots.py` saves the game's frames meanwhile through the debug server's `screenshot` method (up to 3840 x 2160, saved by the emulator into its dump folder; snaildev allowed it for this, 2026-11-10; nobody looks at them) and lists them with the stored character number in `build-npc\portraits\shots.jsonl`.
  - Portraits, full run (snaildev, 2026-11-10): the run of all 100 went through (about 1,500 frames, 12-15 per character); `tools\npccutout.py --fit` wrote 99 cut-outs to `build-npc\portraits\cut-fit\` (numbers per picture in `cut-fit.jsonl`). What it took after trial 1: (1) gear: neither leaving out the equipment step, nor the Bool `Sequence_IsDisablePouchSyncEquipment`, nor `SeqOneShotPouchTargetRequestDeleteEquipment` takes sword and shield off the menu character; what works is in the models (`GEAR_AWAY`): `Pod_A`, `Pod_B`, `Weapon_Root`, `Weapon_L`, `Weapon_R` (leaves without weights in every model) re-parented under a new bone 2 km down, in the 98 NPC models, Link's own and Retro Link's (his file, made with Switch Toolbox, has a zero-size relocation section AT the relocation table and a pointer to it at header +0x188: `tools\bfres.py` now moves that pointer with the table). (2) the header / footer screen `MenuOverlay_00` is hidden during the run with `SeqOneShotSetScreenVisible`. (3) framing (snaildev: one square does not work, fitted is better): the page zoom makes BODIES equally tall, so faces differ on screen from 0.44 to 3 times child Link's (adults about 0.6-0.7, Gorons 2, King Zora 3); the square is Link's (left 2274, top 304, side 572 of a 3840 x 2160 frame) scaled by the on-screen distance between the eyes (from the skeleton's `Eyeball_L/R`), the eyes placed where Link's sit, then centred on the head's own silhouette and moved down so that no more than Link's 8 % of the side is empty above the head. Squares come out 249-1,737 pixels (median 397). Not done by the numbers: Biggoron (the zoom is capped at 8, he fills the frame and the backdrop sample area: no white frame recognised), 4 models without eye bones (Link's square), 14 with hair over the top edge, a few with something over a side edge.
  - Portraits, second cut (snaildev's notes on the full set, 2026-11-11: 34 of the 99 re-cut, the other 65 files byte for byte as they were). What he judges a portrait by: the space above the head as Link's, the BOTTOM edge at the collarbone or just below the chin, the face (not the hair) in the middle; hair, hats and things carried on the head may be cut. Why the first rule missed that: (1) the distance between the eyes is not the size of the face (Gorons and King Zora have theirs far apart and came out showing the torso, narrow-set eyes came out cut below the nose; the 4 models without eye bones had Link's square); (2) tall hair or a hat pushed the face down; (3) big hair pulled the centre sideways (Great Fairy, `Hylia_WX_001/002`: the face is 80 / 85 / 45 pixels left of the hair's centre). How it is measured now, without looking: the model's eye, `Chin_Point` and `Neck` bone heights put on screen through the head's own silhouette, because the idle pose stands lower than the rest pose (adults 60-100 pixels of a 2160-row frame = 7-9 cm, children 30-50 = 2-3 cm; the page zoom alone is off by that much). In the portraits snaildev did not flag, the chin sits at 0.82 of the side from the top and the base of the neck at 1.07 (adults; children 0.78 / 0.98); the flagged ones are outliers in exactly his words (chin at 0.91 = "starts at the chin", 1.05-1.24 = "below the mouth / nose", 0.58-0.61 = "at the sternum", 0.38-0.47 = "at the belly"). So `tools\npccutout.py` has a table `ADJUST` {character: (zoom, right, down)} applied to the fitted square: flagged portraits with the top right get the unflagged framing (top margin kept, side from chin and neck), shifts are what he described; each entry carries his note. Caveats: the chin bone is not the visible chin everywhere (`Hylia_MM_006`'s sits high, the heavy `Hylia_MF` men's chin hangs lower, so those go by the neck); a few unflagged portraits measure like flagged ones (`Hylia_ML_001` 0.93, `Hylia_MN_007` 0.94, `Zora_ZN_001` 0.92, both Ganondorfs 0.96) and are left alone. The frames are now in `Fritchii-npc\user\log\debug-server` (the emulator copy was renamed; the tool looks there when the listed path is gone), and a character's frames are taken from the newest run that has him (`runs()`), so a run of a few characters does not hide the full one.
  - Giants (2026-11-11). Zoom factors as a child: King Zora 5.49, Medigoron 8.03, Great Fairy 8.44, Biggoron 26.78 (Head bone 24.5 m up, model 30.8 m tall and 42 m wide); with `ZOOM_MAX` 8 the page shows Biggoron up to 11.5 m only (his legs), which is also why he has no portrait. The backdrop `Obj_PlusMenuBackground` is a small cove around the character's feet: floor disc of radius 2.29 m, curving up to radius 3.31 m at 0.58 m, wall to 2.70 m, open towards the camera (161 vertices, positions as four half floats, format 0x1505); one 768 x 768 texture `Tex/PlusMenuBackground_Alb`, dark warm brown (floor about 40 of 255, the wall from 37 at the floor to 11 at the top). The menu camera (`MenuActorParam`: position (0, 0.2, 4.5), aim (0.07, 0.95, 0), 20 degrees) sees the wall just fill the picture at factor 1; at factor f the backdrop is 1/f of that, a patch between the feet for the giants (snaildev). Test build only (`npc_characters.main()`): the cap is `ZOOM_TEST_MAX` 32, the trial run (D-pad down) is Link and Biggoron, and `--display` builds the page as it looks (backdrop there, nothing frozen, gear on) with `backdrop_scale_hook`: at `CAMERA_BLOCK_NEXT` + 4 the backdrop actor (manager +0x208, link +0x10, id +0x18, as the game's wake routine 0x80d2b008 reads a slot) gets the zoom factor as its scale (+0x34c, three floats; the camera's z after the zoom / the camera block's z, 1 in other views). Capture run of Link and Biggoron in that build (snaildev, 2026-11-11; read from the frames, his own answer not in yet): at 26.78 Biggoron stands in the picture exactly as Medigoron does at 8 (covered box of the 2160-row frame: columns 2013-3109, rows 420-2001; Medigoron 2011-3112, 403-2002), so a camera 120 m away clips nothing, and his portrait is cut like the other Gorons' (100 of 100 now). Link's portrait from that run differs from the one in place in 0.4 % of its pixels (no shift), so `npccutout.py` keeps every portrait in place with the frames it was cut from (`frames_used` in `cut-fit.jsonl`) and takes a later run of the same character only with `--newest`. Display round 1 (snaildev, 2026-11-11): Biggoron whole on the page too, Link normal afterwards, but the backdrop still a small patch: a scale written into the actor (+0x34c) alone changes nothing. Why (code): what is drawn is the model part's own copy. An actor's parts hang at +0x270; the model part is [+0x270] +0x68 and holds the actor's 3 x 4 matrix at +0x18 and the scale at +0x48 (three floats). The actor's update 0x83af3580 copies matrix and scale there (0x83af38c8, only if the part's byte +0x88 has bit 2, "follows its actor"; not if actor +0x465 bit 2 or +0x4a8 bit 24) and then calls 0x800384e0(part), which queues the model for its new matrix; the placing routine 0x809f7220 (wake / position request, sets +0x4a8 flag 0x4000) does the same copy. The backdrop is a passive actor (`PreActorSetting` `ConstPassive`), so nothing copies after it is placed. Round 2 (the hook also wrote the part's scale and called 0x800384e0(part); snaildev, 2026-11-11): still a small patch, Link normal, and the zoom-out to a giant "still looks good" as it is. `tools\npcbackdrop.py` (memory reads only, log in `build-npc\backdrop-log.txt`) says why: the factor arrives in the actor (scale 1 -> 26.781 following the glide, back to 1 in other views; the backdrop is state 4 awake on the page, 6 asleep on other pages, flags +0x4a8 0x00130300), but the backdrop has NO part at +0x68, so the second step never ran. What it has is the part at [+0x270] +8, whose virtual function +0xf8 returns the model object; the actor's update (0x83af35e8 .. 0x83af37a0) stores the actor's matrix at model +0x1f8, sets bit 0 of model +0x240 and calls 0x802cf7f0(model), which gives every unit of the model that matrix and the scale (model +0x228, three floats) x (model +0x234, three floats).
