Round 3 (every camera update the hook asks the part for the model and, when model +0x234 is not the factor, writes it there, sets the bit and calls 0x802cf7f0; x0-x11, x18 and the scalar halves of v0-v7 saved around the two calls): milestone reached (snaildev, 2026-11-11: "Perfect, that fixes the background for all tall characters"). The log: the part's getter is `ldr x0, [x0, #0x18]` (the model is [part +0x18]), both scale vectors are 1 as shipped, the second follows the factor frame by frame, the update clears bit 0 of +0x240 again. New with the floor now under the giants (snaildev): as a tall character leaves in a slide, his shadow flashes for one frame and the light on his body shifts. Suspect (code): the zoom keeps the menu scene's depth shadow range scaled through the game's override; the slide creates the next character, "character created" (0x80c36870) requests display mode 0, 2 or 3 (byte +0x2e6), and 0x831dfe10 carries that out later in the frame by 0x80ea3a30, whose 0x80ea3ab4 clears the override flag: one frame with the config's 2.0 .. 8.4 m from a camera up to 120 m away (mode 1, from other callers, sets a far of 10 m after the clear). The fix, milestone reached (snaildev, 2026-11-11: "That fixes the shadow"): `shadow_keep_hook` leaves the flag set there while the manager's display type is 0 and the near in force is neither the config's nor the 1.7 the game sets for a new character (0x80c38680). `tools\npcbackdrop.py --shadow` traces the range as fast as the debug server answers (about 4,000 samples a second; at rest the scaled range does not move). Both hooks are in every build since (`npc_character_models`: the normal build, the field swap build and the NPC test build, whose cave 0x83c66400-0x83c66c00 has 16 bytes left with them); the UI sandbox's own build has no room beside its stick patch and goes without, saying so. `--display` of the test build now only means "the page as it looks" (backdrop there, nothing frozen, gear on). The slide with giants (snaildev, 2026-11-11: going to a giant, the character that just slid away shows again while the camera zooms out; going from a giant, he barely moves for the size of the scene): the slide's distances are metres made for Link's view (`SLIDE_OUT` 2.0, `SLIDE_IN` 1.6; the screen edge is 0.94 m from him) and the view is the zoom factor times as wide. `slide_scale_hook` (built and checked offline, not yet seen in game) replaces the one add in `build.view_hook`'s `vh_move` by a jump to seven instructions in the spare area 0x83c663e0-0x83c66400 that multiply the metres by the zoom factor of the camera update before (read from the backdrop actor's scale through the shared routine `bd_actor`). Seen by snaildev (2026-11-11): the old character stays gone and a giant leaves at a good speed, but Biggoron's hand still hung into the top of the picture after he was out. Cause as read from the code: going from a giant to a small character the factor falls with the camera's glide, so the parked giant's distance shrank with it while he stayed 42 m wide, and he came back towards the picture until he was deleted. Now the character that leaves (`w1` = 0 in `vh_move`) never moves back: his new x is the larger of the scaled place and where he is (actor +0x31c; the game's setter 0x80964e70 only queues a position, +0x31c follows at the actor's next update). Passed in game (snaildev, 2026-11-11: "yup that works"). His next note: going from young Link to Biggoron the giant slides in too fast for the zoom-out and the camera goes through his foot. Cause: the new character's way in was 1.6 m times the camera's factor of that moment, which starts at the old character's, so a giant started 1.6 m from his place with the camera 4.5 m away. Now the new one's distance is times the larger of the camera's factor and the factor he will stand at (the stored character's row of the zoom table, through the zoom hook's own lookup, found in the patch by its instructions): a giant starts 43 m out, a small one after a giant comes in as before. Built and checked offline (1760 runs emulated), not yet seen in game. The whole hook now lives in the one-shot body of the unused node class `SeqOneShotSetMessageToLayout` (0x83bd5390-0x83bd54d4 in the Character models patch; `npc_characters.SLIDE_DEAD`, tested by `check_unused_node`); keep other patches off it. That gives the spare area 0x83c663e0-0x83c66400 and 16 bytes of the models cave back. The factor is kept in the backdrop actor's x scale only, and x10 still holds the manager at that point in `vh_move`. Since round 1 the cap is lifted for every build (`ZOOM_MAX` 32: snaildev saw Biggoron whole twice); the normal build is built with it, not installed. If it does, the change for the normal build is three stores in `zoom_blend_hook` (the factor is in s19 there) and the cap.

The NPC emulator copy now gets its own graphics settings on every install (`tools\npcenv.py`, `CAPTURE_SETTINGS`; the normal emulator's config is only read): resolution scale 1.5 (the game's picture becomes 3840 x 2160 itself, one screenshot pixel per rendered pixel), scaling filter bilinear (the identity at 1 : 1), GPU accuracy high, anisotropic filtering 16x. Emulator post-process anti-aliasing stays off; the game's own is left as it is. - `Character field swap` (`mods\field_swap.py`, `install-field.bat`, own emulator copy `PLAY-debug-field.bat`; separate from `install-mods.bat`; passed in game 2026-11-10: swap, place, facing and camera kept): the picked character becomes the field Link when the menu closes, behind a short fade, without save + return to title + Continue. What it is made of and what it rests on: the last section, "the field player (resident actors) and the field swap".

- `Voice test (<voice>)` (the first voice test; `mods\voices.py --everyone`, run with `venv-voice\Scripts\python.exe mods\voices.py --everyone [voice] [--install]`; passed in game, snaildev, 2026-11-11: sword attacks, jumps, rolls, climbing and damage as Saria all good, loudness right, nothing broken): a test folder that replaces child Link's voice clips with the same clips converted to an NPC's own voice (default `Custom_Saria`). It applies to every character while it is switched on, child Link too: there is no per-character voice yet. Convert (Seed-VC, the NPC's own clips as the reference, Link's pitch kept, rows of up to 7.6 s as in the samples snaildev chose; each clip lined up with the original within 40 ms, set to the original's loudness and checked by loudness curve and pitch; failed clips are tried again with other seeds, `--more <n>` adds tries later; results cached in `build-voice\cache`), pack (the game's ADPCM with the same sample counts, so the archive is patched in place and no size table entry is needed; `Sound/Resource/Player_Child.bars.zs` plus the stream files), check (read back: the other 1767 assets byte for byte, every clip's head equal to its stream file's start, checksums, round trip 37 dB median). Saria: 305 of 325 clips converted; 20 keep Link's original: 10 without a voice in them (breaths, grunts under 0.06 s voiced), 4 two-channel cutscene screams, 6 that failed nine tries (`Damage_B_02`, `Sword_O_01`, `FallingScream_02`, `LavaSwallowedDamage_01`, `RotaryCut_B_03`, `Dm_DeathMtTrail_0025_00092`: the model jumps an octave or loses the shape on some screams). `--install` copies only this folder into the normal mods folder and removes other voice test folders; `--remove` takes them out.

- `Character (voice test)` (`mods\voices.py`, run with `venv-voice\Scripts\python.exe mods\voices.py [--install]`; passed in game with Saria, snaildev, 2026-11-11: "working great"): the picked character speaks with its own voice and Link keeps his. Nothing of Link's is replaced. It holds, per voice in `voices.VOICES` (`Custom_Saria` for Saria, stored number 2; `Custom_Zelda_Child` for young Zelda, number 4, passed in game, snaildev, 2026-11-11: good, clearly different from Saria, Link still Link: 306 clips, list `Player_Child_V04`; `--more <n> <voice>` and `--redo <voice>` act on the named voice only): a sound archive `Sound/Resource/PlayerVoice_Child_<voice>.bars` with the converted clips under Link's own asset names (Saria: 305 assets, 2.4 MB) and their stream files under names of their own (`VSaPlChild_...`, 228 files); the archive list with one more list, `Player_Child_V02` = the voice archive first, then the player's own two archives; a code patch; and the size table. How it works (voices section below): the game searches a list's archives in order and the first that has the clip answers, so a clip the voice has is the voice's and the 20 it does not have are Link's; the patch makes the game ask for `Player_Child_V<number>` first where it asks for the player's list, and for the player's own list when there is no such list. So a character without a voice, Link, and everybody when the folder is switched off sound as before. The patch's code (396 bytes) lies in the body of a node class nothing uses, `SeqOneShotEnableRestoreBG` (0x832f0c80; the padding after `.text` is full), and replaces two calls (0x805d5c24, 0x8088d1b4). Offline checks: the archive and every stream file read back against the game's own files and the converted clips; the game's search modelled on the real hash tables of all three archives (every converted clip found in the voice archive, every other voice clip in Link's); the archive list reloaded (the game's 1060 lists unchanged); the hook run in unicorn (164 runs: both ages, numbers 1 to 255, no list, no flag, no manager, other names); against the installed folders (no byte shared with their four code patches, no file of theirs replaced). The size table: the folder ships the table that was installed when it was built plus its two entries (`Sound/Resource/PlayerVoice_Child_Saria.bars`, the larger archive list) and its name sorts before every other folder so the emulator takes that copy. After another install that copy would be out of date, so `mods\build.py --install` removes the folder (it is in `build.RETIRED` for that reason); `mods\voices.py --install` puts it back. `--remove` takes both voice test folders out. To add a voice: an entry in `voices.VOICES` (voice type, two characters for its stream files' names, the characters' labels from `build-npc\characters.txt`); the conversion runs on the first build (about six minutes). Both ages since 2026-11-13 (passed in game, snaildev, the same day: Saria as adult Link "Same voice", the others "All good", the adult-only shouts "Good", adult Malon "Good" as both Links): every voice has an archive per age, `PlayerVoice_Child_<voice>.bars` and `PlayerVoice_Adult_<voice>.bars`, lists `Player_Child_V<nn>` and `Player_Adult_V<nn>`, stream files `V<code>PlChild_...` / `V<code>PlAdult_...`; the code patch is unchanged (it always handled `Player_Adult`). Each archive is converted from that age's own Link clips (see the voices section, "Both ages"). A voice has a base age (third entry in `voices.VOICES`, child when left out): that Link's clips are converted at his pitch, the other Link's are moved by `voices.APART` = 3 semitones (a child voice as adult Link +3, adult Malon as child Link -3). Seven voices now (Saria 2, Mido 3, young Zelda 4, young Malon 5, young Ruto 6, Fado 7 and `Kokiri_WC_002` 98 with `Kokiri_Girl_Spry`, adult Malon 11), 296 to 306 of 325 child clips and 307 to 315 of 352 adult clips each; the rest stay Link's. The folder is 14 archives. Built since, not yet heard in game: Great Fairy (19) and Darunia's son (24), both on the adult base (18 archives then). How it is built and installed now: `mods\voices.py` without `--install` converts and encodes before it takes the build lock (a full pack is about nine minutes; a conversion about six minutes for a child set, ten for an adult set), `--install-only` copies the folder as last built in seconds after checking it against what is installed, `--age child|adult` and voice names limit `--redo` / `--more`. It installs into Lichii's mods folder (`OOT_MODS_APP=eden` gives Fritchii's) and stamps the install record as `voices`. Beside `Character (customize test)`: that folder sorts before this one and carries a merged size table made when IT was installed, so after a voice build with new or larger files its table lacks their sizes; `voices.py` then refuses to install ("NOT INSTALLABLE beside ...") until `mods\customize.py --remove` has run, and that session's install puts its folder back with the new sizes.

- `Character (customize test)` (`mods\customize.py`, run with `venv\Scripts\python.exe -I mods\customize.py [--install]`; customization track, 2026-11-13; the mode passed its first look in game, see "In game" below). snaildev's design: on the character select page X opens "customize" for the selected character: he moves from the right third to the middle of the screen, the picker's 2D UI goes out and a customization UI comes in, options left and right of him (hair colour, eye colour, clothing colour, outfits, show / hide equipment, accessories). This first step is the mode. The options can be operated and their numbers are kept in unsaved Ints (`CharacterMenu_Custom` + `Hair`, `Eyes`, `Clothes`, `Outfit`, `Gear`, `Accessory`; one set for all characters); nothing reads them yet, so no value changes the character.
  - How it is built: ONE folder laid over what is installed, in about ten seconds (most of it packing the layout archive). It does not run the normal build and writes nothing into `build\` (other sessions change the normal build's sources at the same time: a full build run from here failed inside the NPC session's cloth step, half way through an edit of theirs). It reads the files the installed folders ship, the way the emulator layers them (of a file several folders have, the first folder's by name), makes its own versions and writes them into `build-custom\Character (customize test)`: the page's graph, the layout archive, the category list, the screen table, the two message archives, the GameDataList (seven unsaved Ints appended in place), a code patch, and the size table = every installed table's entries, each at its largest, plus its own seven. The folder's name sorts before every other folder's, the voices folder's too, so its versions and its table are the ones the emulator takes; switched off in the Add-Ons list, everything is what was installed. A normal install removes it (`build.RETIRED`); it has to be built again after one, since it is made from the files that install replaces. With it installed, `tools\voices_install.py` run by itself stops ("a folder sorts before ours"): run the normal install first.
  - The graph, as first built (the controls and sounds changed after the second look, see "Second look" below; the installed picker graph, read and written the way the normal build edits the game's own graphs: unchanged it comes back byte for byte; 2,673 -> 3,106 nodes with 99 characters). Every button loop of the picker asks for X first; the node that was the loop's whole content is its "no" branch, so every other button is as before. X: `CharacterMenu_Customize` + 1, the picker's layout plays `CustomOut`, a sound; our screen is loaded and opened with `SeqExecuteLoadAndOpenScreen` and gets its first requests in the frame it opens (made visible, `Focus` stopped at 0, `In` played, then one selector per option that stops `Val_ff` at the option's number), as the field swap opens its fade screen. Then a button loop per option behind a gate of two waited frames, as in the picker: Up / Down (D-pad or left stick, the picker's patched checker) stop `Focus` at the next option (the six go round), Left / Right ask for the option's number and go to that case's sequence (stop `Val_ff` at the new number, store it, a sound), B or - go back: `Out` on our screen, `CustomIn` on the picker's, `CharacterMenu_Customize` - 1, wait for `Out`, close and unload our screen, then the picker's own entry selector by the saved character. So the cursor comes back on the selected character's slot, wherever it was before. The page's entry sets the Int to 0.
  - The layouts, as first built (the columns' way in and the key glyphs changed after the second look). Ours, `CharacterCustomize_00` (107 panes, 55 messages), is Key Items' layout as the page's own layout starts from it, with Key Items' own content hidden (`N_IsOpen_00`, `P_Overlay_00`, `N_DemoMessage`) and two columns of our panes, hidden until `In` shows them: per option a label, one text pane per value (a graph cannot change a text), the game's ornament line and two key glyphs (the picker's part `PaCharacterKey_00` with the game's D-pad left / right characters) beside the value; a title over the left column, three key hints under the columns. Animations: `In` (14 frames: the columns fade in and slide 160 units in from their sides), `Out` (10), `Focus` (a state: the focused option's row at alpha 1, the others 0.4, its glyphs shown), `Val_ff` (a state: which value text shows). The screen row is the page's with sort key 5001. The picker's layout gets two animations and nothing else changes in it: `CustomOut` (20 frames) moves the light, the window `W_Window_00`, from x = 640 to 0 on the curve the code patch moves the picture by, and takes the picker's own panes away (their container `N_Sandbox_00` fades over 12 frames, slides 160 units left and is hidden at the end, since a hidden pane hides its children whatever alpha does to parts); `CustomIn` is the way back.
  - The code patch (the 3D picture). The 3D UI camera's update stores the picture offset into the camera at 0x8289d4d0 (`stur x20, [x19, #0xcc]`; x in the low half of x20, y in the high half). That instruction is now a call (`bl`, left with `ret`: the function keeps its return address in its frame) of a routine in the one-shot body of the unused node class `SeqOneShotSetDebugBancParam` (0x809d76a0-0x809d77cc, 300 bytes, 20 left; found by the voices session as the fourth largest such body). While the offset is the page view's (the bits of `build.VIEW_OFFSET_X`, which the page view patch writes every update while the page shows the character) the routine reads `CharacterMenu_Customize` = 2 * count + bit, moves the count by one (up to 20 while the bit is set, down to 0 while it is clear) and stores offset = page + (centre - page) * (3t^2 - 2t^3), t = count / 20, centre = -0.0248 (the character's origin on the screen's centre line). The graph only adds 1 or -1 (the bit) and the routine only 2 or -2 (the count), so neither can undo the other whatever order a queued GameData set lands in. Any other offset passes through untouched, so the + menu's views and every other 3D view are as before, and so is everything when `Character models` (the page view patch) is off. Free there: x8-x17 and v16-v18 (each of x8-x13 is written before it is read on every way on from the store; the function never uses x14-x17 or a vector register above v15). The routine has its own inline GameData lookup, so it needs no other folder's code.
  - Offline checks in the build: the layout as built (what every `Focus` and `Val_ff` frame shows, the ends of `In` and `Out`, every pane inside its column, clear of the character's light and of the header and footer, every part a layout the archive has); the picker's two animations (the light's place at every frame against the patch's formula, the ends); the graph run as the game would for X in every loop of the picker and every button in every loop of ours with several stored numbers and option numbers (3,370 presses, on the nodes and again on the file as written: where it ends, what was stored, which animations were asked for in which order, that no number is asked for in the frame it was set, that no press comes back to a button loop inside its frame, that every root node can be reached); the routine in unicorn (708 runs: every value of the Int, a whole visit there and back, other offsets, no manager, no flag, a crowded hash map; registers and stack kept; the way back ends on exactly the page's offset); the unused node body by the NPC session's `check_unused_node`; no byte shared with the six installed patches; the size table against every installed table.
  - Not knowable offline: that a second class-less screen opened over the page shows and takes its texts (its message file is `LayoutMsg/CharacterCustomize_00.msbt`; screen and layout have the same name, as the page's); that alpha on a row's null pane dims its texts (the game does this with `DetailIn`); that the light and the character arrive together (assumes layout animations and camera updates both run 60 times a second); that the sounds play; and how it all looks.
  - In game, first look (snaildev, 2026-11-13, Lichii 160): the picker works as before with the folder on; X glides the character to the middle while the grid goes and the six options come in; the light stays on him all the way (so layout animations and camera updates do run at the same rate); the bright row moves, the values change; B brings everything back with the cursor on the selected character; the screen's look "Good". So these work in game: a second class-less screen of ours opened over the page with `SeqExecuteLoadAndOpenScreen` and its own message file, its first animation requests in the frame it opens, alpha on a row's null pane as a state, a store of the picture offset replaced by a call into an unused node's body. Two faults: the two key glyphs showed on the focused option only ("the buttons aren't visible until I hover over them"; they are on every option with a choice now and dim with their row), and there were no sounds at all.
  - Menu sounds belong to screens (2026-11-13, from the code and the sound link file; why the first build was silent). `SeqOneShotStartSound` (one-shot body 0x80e02110) takes `ScreenName` and `SLinkKey`: it looks the screen up in the screen manager ([0x84c9e098]: count +8, array +0x10; an unknown name gives screen 0), and if that screen's object is there calls its virtual +0x2a8 with the key; the name `GlobalSound` instead goes to 0x837b95b0, the user `UI_GlobalSound`. A screen's sound user is `UI_<screen name>` in `SLink2/slink2.Product.111.bslnk.zs` (XLNK version 0x22: +0x48 number of users, 1216; +0x50 the end of the users' data; +0x58 the name table; from +0x60 the users' crc32 name hashes, sorted, then their 64-bit offsets; a user's data names its keys by offset in the name table; `customize.sound_users()` reads it). 37 `UI_` users exist; our own screens have none. Who has what: `UI_MenuOverlay_00` (the header) `open`, `close_Direct`, `mc_TabToLeft`, `mc_TabToRight` and their `_Plus` / `_Minus` forms; `UI_ImportantMenu_00` (Key Items) `mc_CursorMove`, `mc_OpenDetail`, `mc_ReturnFromDetail`; `UI_PlusMenu_00` `button_on`, `button_decide`, `button_noeffect`, `Slide_L`, `Slide_R`, the equip sounds; `UI_ChallengeMenu_00` `button_on`, `mc_ToChallenge`, `mc_ToTapestry`; `UI_SystemOption_00` `button_on`, `button_decide`, `button_check`, `mc_button_slide_L` / `_R`; `UI_ShortCut_00` `mc_ChangeItem`, `mc_ItemSlide`, `mc_Sort`; `UI_GlobalSound` `ShopBuy_MoveCursor`, `ShopBuy_Focus`, `ShopBuy_Cancel`, `AmiiboMarker_StartUp` / `Cancel` / `OK`, `OcarinaInput_Reset`. A key its user does not have is silent and does no harm. So the first build's keys, asked from the header's user (the template node's `MenuOverlay_00`), could not play, and neither can the PICKER's `mc_CursorMove` and `mc_NormalDecide` on a move and on A (main session: they have been silent all along; `mc_NormalDecide` is in no user at all). Which screens are always loaded (`LifetimeGroup: Resident` in the screen table): `MenuOverlay_00`, `MenuBG_00`, `ImportantMenu_00`, `ChallengeMenu_00`, `MapMenu_00`, `PlusMenu_00`. Not knowable offline: whether a loaded screen that is closed plays. The build now waiting for snaildev's ears is a comparison (`customize.SOUNDS`): X and B and Up from Key Items' user (closed), Down from `GlobalSound`, Left from the header (proven), Right from the + menu's user (closed).
  - Second look and the build since (snaildev, 2026-11-13). What he saw, in his words: "the buttons literally don't show up: I press X, character gets centered, then once I press down or left or right, the first button shows up along with the one I navigated to, then they get drawn in one at a time as I hover over each one"; "wrong sound when selecting options, then a page turn when changing one, but the right button doesn't work, only the left one"; the + menu's equipment page "has a simple sound for navigation over slots (our character page itself should also have this)". And he asked for a prompt for X on the picker, and for the D-pad or left stick to select an option and ZL / ZR to cycle its values. (1) THE ROWS. The title and the hints, which hang on a column pane directly, showed; the option rows, which hang on a row pane under it, did not, and a row appeared exactly when `Focus` changed its row pane's alpha (the row left and the row arrived at; a row whose alpha stayed 0.4 stayed undrawn). The first build brought the columns in with alpha 0 -> 1 and a visibility switch on the column panes. So: WHAT AN ANIMATION DOES TO A PANE'S ALPHA OR VISIBILITY DOES NOT REACH THE CHILDREN OF A CHILD PANE THAT HAS AN ALPHA ANIMATION OF ITS OWN; they keep what they had when that child's own alpha last changed (here: the column's alpha 0 of the opening frame). Through a child without an alpha animation it does reach them: the picker's `CustomOut` / `CustomIn` fade and hide `N_Sandbox_00`, and the texts under its sheet panes went and came back. (A count over the game's layouts gave no flag bit for this: bit 1 of the pane flags, set on every container, is on our panes too.) Now nothing fades or hides the columns: they stand off the screen at rest (x -1100 / +1100) and `In` / `Out` only move them (14 / 10 frames), so the only alpha in the chain is the rows' own. (2) THE SOUNDS, by the comparison: the header's tab sound played (Left), the + menu's page slide asked from the + menu's own closed screen did not (Right), the shop's cursor from `GlobalSound` played and was "wrong"; whether Key Items' closed screen played (X, B, Up) he did not say. So the build asks nothing from a closed screen any more. Changing a value plays the header's tab sounds (ZL `mc_TabToLeft`, ZR `mc_TabToRight`), everything else comes from `GlobalSound`, whose user gets three sounds of other users through the sound link file: our folder ships `SLink2/slink2.Product.111.bslnk.zs` with 9 bytes changed, the parameter block of three of `UI_GlobalSound`'s entries (`customize.BORROWED`): `AmiiboMarker_OK` = `mc_button_on_EquipmentMenu` of `UI_PlusMenu_00` (the cursor over the equipment page's slots: a move here and in the picker), `AmiiboMarker_StartUp` = Key Items' `mc_OpenDetail` (X), `AmiiboMarker_Cancel` = Key Items' `mc_ReturnFromDetail` (B). Why that is sound: an entry is {key, asset number, parent, key hash, parameter block, condition} (48 bytes, layout in `customize.slink_users`), the blocks are one table for the whole file and 4,144 of its 14,960 blocks are used by several users in the game's own file, and every `SE_Sys_` sound is in one archive that is always loaded (`Sound/Resource/UI_Static.Product.111.bars.zs`; the archive lists have no UI users). The price: the three keys are the amiibo marker's sounds, so with this folder on a scanned amiibo sounds like the menus. In the + menu's user `button_on` is a group: `button_on_` under it has `button_on_CollectMenu`, `mc_button_on_EquipmentMenu`, `mc_button_on_ItemMenu`, `mc_button_on_SysMenu`, each with a condition (the page that is open); `Slide_L` / `Slide_R` are plain sounds without parent or condition, so that Right was silent says a closed screen does not play (or not this one). (3) The picker's own cursor sound: in our copy of the graph the one node that asked for `mc_CursorMove` from the header now asks for the borrowed sound. Its A sound (`mc_NormalDecide`) is as it was, silent. (4) THE PROMPT: two panes more in the picker's layout, last in its own container `N_Sandbox_00` (copies of its ZL glyph part and its title): the X glyph and "Customize" under the grid from its left edge (y -740) at first, and since snaildev's word of the same day ("underneath the divider under the character's name on the right, so it's roughly wide enough to fit the divider") centred under the line of the detail block right of the character (centre (1380, -50); the head line's glyph and text enlarged from size 46 to the name's 84, about 572 wide by estimate under the line's 700; the detail texts that would be drawn there are empty in every build, which this build checks; the name over the line is the CURSOR's character while X customizes the one shown, a question put to snaildev); their two messages go into the picker's message file. (5) THE CONTROLS: D-pad or left stick go to another option (up / down inside a column, left / right to the same row of the other column, no going round), ZR / ZL the next / previous value (going round), B or - back; the glyphs beside a value are ZL and ZR, the hints the four-way D-pad glyph "Select", ZL ZR "Change", B "Back". 108 panes, 56 messages, 3,108 nodes, 3,766 presses run offline. Built, checked offline, waiting for his look.
  - Third look and the build since (snaildev, 2026-11-13). Seen: the sounds are right ("All right": the equipment page's cursor sound in the picker and in the mode, the tab sounds on ZL / ZR, open and back), the prompt is there (first under the grid, then at the name's size under the detail block's line: "Too big. Let's just go back to the normal size"; now the head line's size, centred under that line), and X is to customize the character SHOWN, not the cursor's (his answer). Not right: with the columns only moved in from off the screen NO option showed at all, "even when moving the cursor now". THE LIKELY CAUSE, found in our own file and not yet proven in game: the panes of a layout are stored in PRE-ORDER (a pane, then its whole subtree, then its next sibling; each pane records its parent and where its subtree ends). All 157 layouts of the stock archive and the five the mod ships are so; `uiedit.Editor.insert` asks for a block in that order and does not check it, and `Editor.validate` passes a block that is not. The first three builds of this layout made both columns first and their contents after, so `N_Col_01` lay inside `N_Col_00`'s recorded subtree and every option group inside both; that fits what was seen in both looks (what was done to a column arrived at the panes directly behind it and not at the groups), which the second look's rule above ("does not reach the children of a child pane that has an alpha animation of its own") only described: read that rule as unproven. `customize.preorder` checks the order now, on our layout and on the picker's before and after our two panes. The layout since is also built from what has been seen working, so that it holds whichever it was: no group panes (every text and glyph hangs on its column directly), the columns at rest where they are shown ("In" / "Out" move them in from / out to x -1100 / +1100; the mode is whole even if "In" did nothing), and no alpha animation: the focused option is marked by the only ornament line and by the ZL / ZR glyphs beside its value, one marker pane `N_Mark_00` (the line and the two glyphs directly on it) that `Focus` moves to the option as the sandbox's focus line is moved; the glyphs are hidden on an option with one value; "In" shows the marker when the columns have arrived, "Out" hides it. Unfocused options are no longer dimmed (dimming through a group's alpha can come back once the order is proven to be the cause). The graph is unchanged (`Focus` keeps its name and its six frames). 82 panes, 46 messages. Built, checked offline, waiting for his look.
  - Fourth look and the colours (snaildev, 2026-11-13). Seen: all six options are there at once (so the pane order was it, or the layout without groups: both changed together), the columns slide in, the marker follows, ZL / ZR change values, B goes back, no dimming is "Good", the prompt's place and size are right. Two things to fix, both done: (1) the columns slid in TWICE, first with the layout's own values, then, about a second later, with the stored ones: THE GAME PLAYS A LAYOUT'S ANIMATION NAMED `In` BY ITSELF WHEN A SCREEN OPENS (125 of its layouts have one, 0 have an `Open`), and `SeqExecuteLoadAndOpenScreen` does not return until well after that. Ours are now `ColsIn` / `ColsOut`, the whole of our screen (`N_Custom_00`) is hidden at rest, and the graph asks for `ColsIn` (whose first act is to show it) last, after the marker and every value are set. (2) The hint line is gone ("select, change, and back button prompts are redundant"). His word then: "let's move on to actually implementing all those pickers as best you can". COLOURS, built and checked offline, not seen in game yet. Hair Color and Eye Color now change the character (how the game colours hair and eyes, and how a value is set on a living model: see "Material colours" under player models). Two routines of ours call one that sets the options' colours on every unit of an actor's model: `cz_sync` in place of the equipment sync's call of the hair routine (0x810ccee4: every player and menu character passes it when it is made; it calls the hair routine as before, then sets a colour only where an option is not 0, so a character nobody customized is never written to), and `cz_frame` at the 3D UI camera update's way out (0x8289d600, in place of its first reload, `ldp d9, d8, [sp, #0x50]`; while our FLAG is not 0 it sets the menu character's colours on every update, and there an option at 0 puts the model's own values back from the model file, so "Default" after another colour shows at once). The field character is not written to while the menu is open: a change of a colour sets the field swap's `CharacterMenu_FieldSwapPending`, and the swap makes him anew when the menu closes. The code is in the enter and update routines of `SeqExecuteLoadFile` (0x832e1620-0x832e19f0, 72 bytes left), the GameData lookup and the tables in those of `SeqExecuteSaveFile` (0x832e39d0-0x832e3c00): no file of the game names either class, not even the node definition file (128,851 files read), and `customize.check_dead` checks at every build that nothing reaches them. The tables: nine hair colours (colour 0 and 1, most of them the game's own: Zelda's, Ingo's, Nabooru's, a Kokiri boy's, Saria's), seven eye colours as (hue wanted, saturation, brightness), and per stored number whether `Mt_Hairs` is hair (a character without hair has a stand-in under that name, which keeps its colours) and the hue of its iris texture (`customize.model_facts`, from the installed model files and `Tex`; 102 numbers, 91 with hair, 74 with an iris material, 20 with an iris texture of their own). Hair is set on `Mt_Hairs`, `Mt_HairLong`, `Mt_Hair_Front`, `Mt_Hair1`, `Mt_Hair3`, `Mt_Ponytail`, `Mt_BodyHair` and `Mt_Eyebrow` (12 models have brows as a material, a grey mask coloured the same way). Offline: 2,486 runs of the routines in an ARM64 emulator on a model laid out as the game's code reads one, the unit's three virtual functions and the hair routine answered from the check (every hair and eye colour, characters with and without hair, with their own iris texture, numbers out of range, units with and without the materials, both ways a unit keeps its material objects, no GameData, a menu character that is missing or going away; callee-saved registers and the stack as found). NOT KNOWN UNTIL SEEN: that the colours look as meant (the eye shift is read off eight of the game's own values); that setting a value from the camera update is safe (the game sets such values from its actors' updates); that a slot 0 sync is passed by every new character (the hair routine's crash on a model without `Mt_Hairs` in the menu says the menu character passes it). The options are still one set for every character and not saved. NOT DONE, and why: Clothing Color (clothes are textures; nothing in the 1,193 materials colours plain cloth; it needs recoloured textures or the game's own tunics), Outfit and Accessory (the game's `Armor_*` actors: three adult tunics, two boots, six child masks, a gauntlet; they hang on the equipment component's slots, see "Material colours"), Equipment shown / hidden (the equipment actors are in those slots and an actor has a "shown" bit, `build.ACTOR_SHOWN`; which slot is which is not read yet).

Wrong: (1) HAIR: "it doesn't actually change the hair color, just some of the tint on the hair". Measured since: the hair's colour is in its TEXTURE (Saria's is green, mean (0.18, 0.22, 0.01) linear; Zelda's gold; the Gerudos' red; only the two townswomen that share one have a near black one, which is why their colour 0 differs); colours 0 and 1 colour the shine. Nothing among the 129 parameters is a known tint of the texture; seven candidates (the ones that are white, or black, on every hair material: places 104, 105, 107, 113, 114, 118, 116 of the shader's list) are in this build as a test: the Clothing Color row is "Hair Test", each value makes one of them pure red on `Mt_Hairs`. If one colours the whole hair, a hair colour is that parameter set to (colour wanted) / (the texture's mean colour) per character; if none does, hair colours need recoloured textures. (2) EYES work but the names were off ("gold turned out bright red, brown is black, green is hazel, grey is light grey, violet is red to pink depending on the character"): the three numbers are not a wrapped hue and two factors but each ADDED, twice its distance from a half, and clamped (see `customize.EYE_COLOURS`; it fits his seven sightings and the game's own values: Impa and Sheik run into the red end, the brown-eyed men have 0.08 / 0.94). The table is redone on that reading. (3) THE FIELD CHARACTER kept his own colours. Read from his running game (debug server, read only): his model's values were the file's own while the menu character's were ours, so `cz_sync` (the equipment sync's call of the hair routine) is not passed by a field character that is made. It is gone. Instead `cz_head` stands in place of the call of the head gear update (0x804d756c) in the routine every player-like actor's update runs each frame (0x804d74b0; the field player's update 0x82c3d740 reaches it through 0x804d7190, two more actor updates call it directly): the head gear update as before, then the options for that actor where one is not 0. And `cz_frame` (the menu's camera, while customizing) now also does the pouch's target, the field character asleep under the menu, with the putting back. No field swap is asked for any more. (4) He wants the options TRACKED PER CHARACTER (not done yet: one set for everybody, not saved). (5) EQUIPMENT shown / hidden is in this build: "Hidden" clears the "shown" bit of the actors in the equipment slots 1 to 8 and 12 (everything but the head and 9 to 11; from the same read: a child has a mask in slot 0, the sword's sheath in 4, the ocarina in 8, a gauntlet in 9, the ocarina's holder in 12), on every update of the actor; "Shown" sets it again while customizing. The code: the routines of `SeqExecuteLoadFile` (0x832e1620-0x832e19f0, 68 bytes left) and the enter routine of `SeqQueryCreate3DLayer` (0x8103ae80-0x8103b160, 192 left), the tables in `SeqExecuteSaveFile`'s (behind sixteen bytes of `ret`). Offline: 2,633 emulated runs (the gear's bits with the emulator's ARMv8.1 model, every hair test, both callers). NOT KNOWN UNTIL SEEN: that the per-frame place is passed by the field character and by the menu character (the code says the first; the second only matters for a character picked while options are set); that a cleared "shown" bit stays cleared on equipment (the game may set it again on a draw or a sheathing); how the eye colours look on the new reading.

Seen: the field character has the eye colour and the hidden gear at once when the menu closes, and a character picked afterwards has them in the field too (so the players' per-frame place, `cz_head`, is passed by the field character, also by one that was just made); the eye colours are "pretty good, just a bit bright / light for natural eye colors" (the brightnesses are 0.04 to 0.06 lower now). THE HAIR TEST: "none of them change the base color. Test 2 was the only one that really had any noticeable change" (colour 3). So no parameter colours the hair: its colour is its texture, and a hair colour needs recoloured textures (79 hair textures among the player models; the game finds a texture by `Tex/%s.bntx`, 0x83d9fc6e, 11 references: a path hook as the model hook's would do, not built). The test row is gone, Clothing Color is its old self (it does nothing), Hair Color still colours the shine only. GEAR: "it only hides the ocarina and sheath of the sword, not the full sword and shield". Read from his running game: the equipment component has 21 handles of 0x18 bytes from +0x20, and the game's thirteen "slots" are the last thirteen (from +0xe0); the first eight: 0 `Weapon_Sword_Kokiri`, 1 the item in hand (`Item_Bottle`, asleep), 4 `Weapon_Shield_Deku`. Hidden now clears the "shown" bit of handles 0, 2 to 7, 9 to 16 and 20: everything but the item in hand, the head (8) and the armour (17 to 19). THE MENU CHARACTER did not get the options of a character that was picked ("only pressing customize or going to the field updates the color"): the menu character does not pass the per-frame place. `cz_frame` (the menu's camera) now does the menu character on EVERY update, the putting back of a model's own values only while customizing, and the field character while customizing as before. PER CHARACTER (his word in the fifth look): the six Ints the graph reads and writes are the options of the character whose stored number an Int `CharacterMenu_CustomOwner` holds; an Int a stored number (128 of them; GameData knows an Int by a hash only, theirs are 0x1337C000 + the number, no names) keeps a character's six options, four bits each; `cz_keep`, called first by `cz_frame`: the stored number is the owner's -> his Int gets the six; it is another's -> the six are loaded from that one's Int and he is the owner. So picking a character brings his options up within an update, in the menu and on the field character that the swap makes. Not saved: they last until the game is closed. The code: `SeqExecuteLoadFile` (224 bytes left), the enter routine of `SeqQueryCreate3DLayer` (132 left), `SeqExecuteRequestSaveGameData` (0x83bcdc10-0x83bcdde0, the keeping, 244 left); the tables in `SeqExecuteSaveFile`. Offline: 2,671 emulated runs (the keeping: 60 random cases, numbers beyond the kept ones, missing Ints, a visit Link -> Saria -> a change -> Link -> Saria, and through `cz_frame` a picked character's options on the menu character in the same update). NOT KNOWN UNTIL SEEN: that clearing "shown" hides a sword and a shield (it hides the sheath and the ocarina) and what the game does to the bit when they are drawn; the eye colours' new brightness. SEVENTH LOOK (snaildev, 2026-11-13): "All good" to every step: sword and shield go and come back in the menu and are gone in the field, each character keeps his own options and the menu character shows a picked character's at once, the field character has his own, the eye colours are dark enough. His word on the hair: "let's go ahead and try hair. Might as well do clothing too, while we're at it" (recoloured textures).
  - Hair by textures, the proof (2026-11-13, built and checked offline, NOT seen in game). What the model files say: a material record (FMAT, 0xB0) has its texture names at +0x20 (pointers, relocated), the views the game binds at +0x18 and their descriptor slots at +0x90 (both filled at run time: zeros and 0xFFFF... in the file, not relocated), the sampler slots at +0x98, and two counts at +0xa2 (samplers) and +0xa3 (textures), equal in every material of the game. 98 of the 2,172 texture names of the character models have no file `Tex/<name>.bntx.zs` (child Link's hair colour texture `Player_Child_Hairs_Alb` among them; what the game shows for those is not known). No model file holds textures. THE PROOF: `customize.add_textures` gives Link's two models (the game's own files, shipped in our folder) nine more textures on `Mt_Hairs` behind its seven samplers' own, a palette the game should load with the model and draw nowhere; `cz_tex` (called for the hair material by `cz_actor`) puts the palette entry of the Hair Color option where the material's first texture, its colour, is on the living material: by what nn::g3d is known to do, a material object keeps a {view, descriptor slot} pair a texture at its +0x50, and the routine leaves a material alone unless its second pair is the file's second texture as bound (so a wrong guess of that place does nothing), and unless the material has more textures than samplers (so every other model is untouched). For the proof the nine are other characters' hair textures (Zelda's, Talon's, the black one, Nabooru's, Malon's, Saria's, Impa's, young Malon's, Rauru's): no texture is made yet. TWO THINGS ONLY THE GAME CAN SAY: whether a model with more textures than samplers loads at all, and whether the drawing follows the living material's pairs. If it does: recoloured textures of each character's own hair (79 textures x 9) and of the clothes, a writer for model textures (mip chains; `toolsntxwrite.py` writes single-mip layout textures), the palette on every player model. If the model does not load: the palette as copies of the material instead. If it loads and nothing changes: a read of the running game for where the pairs are. FIRST TRY (snaildev, 2026-11-14): Link appears as usual with the palette on his models (a model with more textures than samplers loads), and the hair changed "only the shine, as before". A read of his running game said why: a living material keeps its textures as TWO lists, not pairs: the material object's +0x50 a list of the views (the same pointers as the file's +0x18 list as bound) and its +0x58 a list of their descriptor slots (eight bytes each; a slot belongs to its texture: the menu's and the field's character have the same numbers; the file's +0x90 list is something else, the same number for most textures, a sampler's by the look of it). So the first routine's own check held it back and nothing was written. SECOND CUT, built and checked offline, not seen: `cz_tex` exchanges the first place with the colour's palette place in both lists (so the material's own texture is never lost: it is wherever the file's first view is found), and does nothing unless the living second view is the file's second. Whether the palette's textures are bound in the living lists and whether the drawing follows them is what the next look says. (Also since: the per-character tables allow for a roster with gaps in the stored numbers: the installed build had become a 37 character one.) SECOND TRY (snaildev, 2026-11-14): "only the shine, as before", and a read with Link on the customize screen said why, twice over. (1) THE PALETTE WAS NEVER LOADED: Link's hair material had 7 samplers and 16 textures, and the nine palette places were empty (view 0) in the file's list and in the living one. The game counts a material's textures by the byte +0xa2 wherever it loads or binds them: the model loader's loop over a material's texture names (0x803819c0, in 0x803818e0, which looks each name up by its crc32 in a table of textures asked for and otherwise formats `Tex/%s.bntx`), its binder (0x803802d4, in 0x80380230: the texture object's +0x88 / +0x90, view and slot, into the file's lists +0x18 / +0x98), a second binder by name (0x80457404), and nn::g3d's own bind and release (0x814ebe98, 0x814ebf40); only the living material's lists are as long as +0xa3 says (0x811fb5e0). The two bytes are equal in every material looked at (770 model files, 2,341 materials). (2) THE DRAWING DOES NOT FOLLOW A WRITE TO THE LISTS: the engine's own routine for setting a texture on a living material is 0x834c5910 (material object, place, &{view, slot}): it writes both lists and then, if the view or the slot changed, jumps to what the material object's +0x78 points at (the listener; a texture's view is in no other place in memory than the file's list, the living lists and the texture's own record, found by searching the running game). The game uses it through 0x834c58e0 (material object, place, texture object) in 0x82eb5100, which walks an actor's units and materials and sets a reloaded texture by its name, and through 0x834c5970 (unit vtable +0x1b0: a material's textures again from the file's lists; a texture object is `[view + 0x40]`, its slot `[+0x90]`). Also: the file's +0x90 list is the samplers' slots, +0x98 the textures' (the first cut had lengthened the wrong one); texture names that have no file of their own (child Link's hair: `Player_Child_Hairs_Alb`) are loaded all the same, from somewhere not found yet (they are in a long sorted list of names in memory). THIRD CUT, built and checked offline, not seen: the five count reads are patched to +0xa3 (`customize.COUNT_PATCHES`: nothing changes for the game's own models), the palette's slot list is the textures' (+0x98), and `cz_tex` calls the engine's routine with the palette entry's view and slot from the file's lists (or the model's own first), when that texture is bound and is not the one there. The routine is in `SeqExecuteLoadStartupMap` (0x83bcce70-0x83bcd000). THIRD TRY (snaildev, 2026-11-14): "Yes it works for Link": the palette loads with the five count reads patched and the hair's colour texture changes through the engine's routine, in the menu at once. Two faults, both from the borrowed textures: the hair is "thicker, like it's missing a layer that trims away to form proper wisps" (another character's cut-out: a hair texture's alpha shapes the strands) and "the colors are messed up". THE REAL PALETTE, built and checked offline, not seen: `tools	exrecolor.py` recolours a texture file IN PLACE by changing the colours of every compressed block's end points (BC1, BC3, BC7: the places between the end points, the alpha, the console's block order, every mip and the file's size stay; the name inside is changed to one as long, the last `Alb` to `Ah1`..`Ah9`); a recoloured file decodes to the colour function of the old one with a mean error of 0.003 and the same alpha (checked for every file made). `customize.write_palettes` gives every hair material of every player model the installed folders ship (49 model files, 57 hair materials: `Mt_Hairs` where it is hair, `Mt_HairLong`, `Mt_Hair_Front`, `Mt_Hair1`, `Mt_Hair3`, `Mt_Ponytail`, `Mt_BodyHair`) the nine hair colours of its own colour texture (21 textures, 189 files, 51 MB; the models 118 MB: our folder ships its own copy of each); a colour moves the texture's mean hue, saturation and brightness to the colour's (`customize.HAIR_TEXTURE`) and keeps the strands' differences. `cz_tex` is called for every hair material. Child Link's hair, whose colour texture `Player_Child_Hairs_Alb` has no file, was left out at first and is in since: `System/Gfx/UnifyTex.Product.100.byml.zs` is the game's table of UNIFIED TEXTURES (5,186 names, each with the name of the texture whose file it shares; found by searching all 128,851 files for the name): `Player_Child_Hairs_Alb` -> `Mt_Hairs_Player_Adult_Alb`, and so for his other four hair textures; that is where the 98 texture names of the character models without a file of their own come from. A unified name gets the palette of the texture it shares (52 model files, 60 hair materials, 189 texture files). The hair textures of the installed characters are BC7, BC1 and BC3; of their 36 clothing textures many are ASTC, which this way of recolouring does not do (decode, repaint and encode again, mip by mip, is the way for those: not built).
  - Clothing by textures (customize session, 2026-11-14; built and checked offline, NOT seen in game; in the build only with `--clothes` until it has passed, so a release build is as before). Clothing Color (Default and seven colours) changes the clothes the way Hair Color changes the hair: recoloured copies of a material's own colour texture as a palette behind its samplers' own, put in the first place with the engine's routine. What is new. (1) WHICH COLOUR. A character's clothes are several materials and a clothing texture holds several colours (Link's vest: the green, the belt, the trim), so a clothing colour replaces ONE colour of a character, his clothes' main one, wherever his clothing textures have it, and leaves the others: Link's green becomes red, his belt, boots and undershirt stay. The main colour is measured from the model, not looked at: every triangle of the clothing materials by its area and the texel at its UVs (`customize.cloth_facts`, kept in `build-custom\cloth-facts.json`), then `cloth_main`: a hue where one hue covers 35% of the clothes; else the largest clothing material's own hue where that is a fifth of it (a pale outfit with a coloured vest: the game's own colour variants of such models differ in exactly that accent); else the unsaturated part, greys, whites and blacks (Malon's cream dress, an Iron Knuckle's armour). Clothing materials are all but skin, face, eyes, hair, teeth, hands, weapons, metal, footwear, stones (`customize.CLOTH_NOT`; `Mt_Body` only beside a skin material); a creature has none and the option does nothing for it. (2) WHICH TEXELS. A clothing material's texture is taken only where the material's SURFACE shows the main colour (a twentieth of it near the hue, or a tenth of it grey: `cloth_shows`; a quarter of adult Link's glove texture is a yellow green that no triangle of his gloves draws, and by the texture alone his gloves got a palette in the first build). Per such texture, the part within 0.115 of a turn of the texture's own hue peak nearest the character's hue (wholly within 0.065), or the unsaturated part (`cloth_weight`); `cloth_fn` gives that part the colour's hue and saturation (the part's own differences kept), moves its brightness towards the colour's (darker: scaled; brighter: by a power, so black stays black; measured on the copies: Link's tunic as Red is hue 0.00, saturation 0.75, brightness 0.4; the texels outside the part move by less than 0.02 of full scale, which is the ASTC encoder's) and blends by the weight; a texture with less than 2% (5% for greys) of such texels gets no copies. Two characters that share a texture share its copies while they agree on its colour (the copies' names: the last `Alb` becomes `A`, a letter for the kind and the colour's digit; `h` is the hair's). (3) WHICH MATERIALS: every material of the model that draws a recoloured texture as its first (the hat's band draws the vest's). (4) THE CODE: `cz_cloth` goes through a unit's materials by number (their count is the unit's virtual +0xc8, as in the game's own loop at 0x80232e2c) and sets the option's entry on every material whose palette is as long as there are clothing colours (seven; the hair's is nine: that is how the two are told apart, and the build refuses equal lengths), through `cz_texo`, the part of `cz_tex` that works on a material object; `cz_actor` calls it for every unit before the hair. The three are in the unused `SeqExecuteLoadStartupMap` (32 bytes left there, 164 in `SeqExecuteLoadFile`), the option's hash in the tables' head (+0x18; 464 bytes of tables). 2708 runs emulated. (5) NUMBERS of the full build: 212 model files with a palette on 609 materials, 819 recoloured textures (7 colours each of 117 textures' kinds; 819 made in this run), 232 MB; the main colour is a hue for 196 model files, the greys for 16, none for 22 (build-custom\cloth-plan.txt). `build-custom\cloth-plan.txt` lists what was decided for every model file and every recoloured texture; the rules are a first cut from numbers, and only the game shows where they are wrong for a character (`customize.CLOTH_TEXTURE` has the colours, `cloth_main` the rule). (6) Equipment hidden no longer hides handles 9 to 11 (player models, "The equipment's 21 handles"). (7) For building while the normal emulator holds something else (it held the release's one folder): the build reads `%APPDATA%\lichii\load\...`, so a folder of junctions to `build\<the working folders>` and `build-voice\mod\Character (voice test)` under another `APPDATA` gives it the working folders without an install. `--install` now copies the folder as last built when its switches, the project's scripts it loaded and every file of the installed folders are what that build was made from (`build-custom\built-over.json`), and builds otherwise; `--install-only` never builds.
  - Clothing colours, the look, and a colour a part (2026-11-14, late). SEEN (snaildev, the build with `--clothes`): Link's tunic and hat change and his belt, boots and skin stay; the seven colours match their names; Saria, young Zelda and young Malon change in the main colour of their clothes; the field character has the colour when the menu closes; the hair's colours still work. So Clothing Color is in the build WITHOUT a switch since (`--clothes` is still taken and means nothing; the default build's files are byte for byte the ones he saw but for the code patch, which has the routine below and passes the emulated check). His words on it: "most NPCs seem to have at least 1 alternate color (links vest is slightly darker green, but when I change colors, everything changes except his belt/belt buckle, which it would also be able to choose different options for those)". BUILT for that, checked offline, NOT seen (`--parts`): three options, Clothing Color, Clothing Color 2 and Clothing Color 3 (the right column; Hair Color, Eye Color and Equipment the left), a PART of the clothes each. A part is whole materials: every clothing material has a main colour of its own (`cloth_own`: the hue a fifth of its surface shows, else its greys); the materials of one texture and one colour are a piece; pieces of one hue within 0.02 of a turn are one part (Link's tunic and shorts are one weave in two textures). The part with the character's main colour is the first colour, the next two by area (4% of the clothes or more) the second and third; a small part (a tenth of the clothes or less) goes with the part nearest its hue (within 0.05) and a larger one keeps its colour (`cloth_parts`). Link as a child: 1 the tunic's weave with the shorts and the collar, 2 the vest and the hat's band, 3 the leather (belt and shoulder belt); as an adult: 1 the vest, 2 the weave and its trim, 3 the leather upper and the gloves, and the pale undershirt keeps its colour. Each material gets the copies of its texture for ITS colour, so two materials of one texture can differ (a villager's shirt and trousers painted in one texture). A material that is not clothing but draws a part's texture gets that part's palette (the belt's buckle), footwear excepted (child Link's boots are in his leather's texture and keep their colour). How the code knows which colour a palette is for: its LENGTH, 7, 8 or 10 textures behind the samplers' own (the copies and, to fill, the last one again; 9 is the hair's; a texture name twice in one material's list is in 462 of the game's own materials), read by `cz_cloth`, which is now given the three colours and is in the unused `SeqExecuteRequestSaveGameData` (72 bytes left there, 124 in `SeqExecuteLoadFile`); the three options' hashes are in the tables' head (+0x18). 2710 runs emulated. Numbers: 212 model files with a palette on 648 materials, 1043 recoloured textures (7 colours each of 124 textures' kinds; 0 made in this run), 308 MB; the main colour is a hue for 196 model files, the greys for 16, none for 22; a colour a part of the clothes: two colours for 62 model files, three for 36 (build-custom\cloth-plan.txt). WHAT IT CANNOT DO: two colours inside ONE material. 41 of the 107 characters with clothes have one clothing material (most villagers: the whole outfit is one piece of the model with its colours painted in one texture), and for them, and for a second colour inside any material, the model's triangles have to be divided between two materials by what they draw (a second shape and material in the model file; `tools\bfres.py` writes no shapes yet): not built. All combinations of two colours in one texture as copies would be 63 a texture where there are 7 now. EQUIPMENT: his word the same evening, "I haven't progressed far enough to unlock those items yet, don't bother with equipment for now": the map under the player models stands as written and the live read is not made.
  - Three clothing colours, the look (snaildev, 2026-11-14, night: "those clothing colors work"; the field character has all three). So they are in the build without a switch (`--parts` and `--clothes` are taken and mean nothing; a release from here has Hair Color, Eye Color, Equipment | Clothing Color, 2, 3 and 308 MB of clothing textures). What he saw, and what was changed for it (built and checked offline, not seen): (1) ORDER. Link: 1 the tunic, 2 the vest, 3 the leather, "as expected". Saria: 1 was the vest, 2 the boots and belt, 3 "the base"; young Zelda: 2 "does nothing" (it was her petticoat, which is under the skirt) and 3 "should be the secondary". Now the parts of the character's main colour come first, a garment before what is worn over it (`CLOTH_OVER`: vest, cape, mantle), then the others by area with what is worn under the clothes last (`CLOTH_UNDER`): Saria 1 weave, 2 vest, 3 the brown parts; young Zelda 1 pink, 2 the blue satin, 3 the petticoat; adult Link 1 weave, 2 vest, 3 leather as the child. (2) EYEBROWS ("we still don't have a separate eyebrow color ... it should match the hair if we can"). The eyebrow material (`Mt_Eyebrow`; 16 of the characters have one: Link, Saria, Mido, Sheik, Dark Link, Twinrova and others; everybody else's eyebrows are painted in the face's texture) draws a GREY texture (saturation 0.002) in its colour 0 and colour 1, and in the game's own models those are the hair texture's colour made linear (Link's colour 0 is 0.309 0.151 0.052, his hair texture's mean made linear 0.307 0.158 0.033). Hair Color had been setting the hair's SHINE colours on the eyebrows too, which are brighter and redder than the hair looks; now the eyebrows have a table of their own after the hair's, the hair colour's texture colour made linear (`brow_colour`; the tables are 536 of the 544 bytes they have room for). Eyebrows painted in a face texture are not changed: that would be nine copies of every face texture and finding the eyebrows in each (by the triangles above the eyes); not built, told to him. (3) BOOTS ("the boots change with clothing 3, but we also want custom boots. If it really uses the same color and there isn't an easy way to fix it, currently it's okay. Then, his boots trim, a flap just on top of the boots, is still a different light-brown color"). Child Link's boots ARE his leather material (one shape of 8,008 triangles: belt, straps and boots), which is why they go with the belt; the trim is `Mt_Fur_Boots`, which draws the leather's texture and had been left out as footwear for one build: it has the leather's palette again. (4) Young Zelda: "the base cream material color on her dress doesn't change": the cream is the second colour INSIDE her skirt, bodice and satin materials (40%, 66% and 64% of them). WHAT BOTH NEED, and what most villagers need for a second colour: a material divided in two. Read from the models for that: every clothing material is ONE shape (its name is only its first piece's), so nothing can be divided by shape; 85 of the 107 clothed characters have a second colour region of 8% or more inside a material; of those materials' surface 12% is triangles whose three sample points are not in one region (the rest divide cleanly by triangle). A shape has three meshes (levels of detail), each with its own index range at a multiple of 256 bytes in the buffer, its own record with the place for the run-time buffer object, and a first-vertex number; so a second shape can be given the first one's level 1 and 2 records and index space (its triangles copied there, both shapes then having one level), with a copy of the material, without growing or moving any buffer. Boots are not a colour but a place (the triangles weighted to the lower leg and foot). NOT built: this is the next step.
  - The clothing colours are placeholders: snaildev's word after the next look (2026-11-14, night). SEEN: the trim on Link's boots follows the boots with Clothing Color 3; Saria is 1 base, 2 vest, 3 boots and belt; young Zelda's 2 is the blue secondary. NOT so: Link's eyebrows "do not change" with Hair Color. So what the bullet above says of the eyebrow material is not borne out: its colour 0 and colour 1 are set (the emulated check sees the sets) and nothing shows; where the eyebrows' colour comes from is not known (the face's texture, or another parameter; the game's own values of colour 0 / 1 do go with the hair's colour, which is what misled). Not looked into further on his word; the eyebrows' table stays in the code and does nothing seen. HIS DECISION: "getting true recolors would realistically just require a tool, it's getting too artistic with different elements (and different number of elements) in different places for each character. For now these 3 options can be placeholders that just cover most base colors. Only thing to fix is adult link's boots don't change with color 3 (the boots and flaps both stay default)". So the division of a material in two (the end of the bullet above) is NOT being built, and the rules that pick the parts are not tuned further; true recolours a character would be made with a tool, by a person. Built for the one fix, checked offline, not seen: FOOTWEAR (materials named Shoes, Boots, Sandal) is still no clothing (no say in the main colour, no part of its own), but where it is the very hue of a part (within 0.02 of a turn, as pieces are one part) it goes with that part (`cloth_footwear`, the end of `cloth_parts`): adult Link's `Mt_Boots` (0.069) with his leather (0.065), which is Clothing Color 3, and the flaps (`Mt_Boots_Far`, lighter, 0.102) because they draw the boots' texture; the same rule gives 12 other characters' shoes to the part of their hue. Child Link needed none of it: his boots are his leather material.
  - The footwear passed, and v0.2.1 (snaildev, 2026-11-15, after the round for adult Link's boots: "that's good, we can push these color updates and actually release this as 0.2.1 on github"; no answer by step, taken as the boots and their flaps changing with Clothing Color 3). v0.2.1 is v0.2.0 with this folder as it is now. Against v0.2.0's zip, by the sums: 1,099 files are new (the recoloured clothing textures, 311 MB) and 221 differ (212 player model files, which carry the palettes; this folder's page graph, layout archive, two message archives, GameDataList and size table; the code patch, 8,532 bytes patched where v0.2.0 had 8,172, and its listing; the install text); every file that comes from the other eight folders is byte for byte v0.2.0's. The package was made from the working folders as installed and played (`tools\package.py v0.2.1 --full` checks them byte for byte against the emulator's mods folder; 6,904 files in the one folder, 1,023.2 MB); the merged one folder itself was not played. In the release and not seen in game: Equipment hidden leaving the adult's tunic, boots and gauntlets on (`GEAR_HANDLES`), and the 12 other characters' shoes that go with a part of their hue.
  - Found on the way. (1) `mc_NormalDecide`, which the picker asks for on A, is in the executable's strings and in no sound user. (2) Key Items' in / out animations, which the page uses for its own entry: `InFromChallenge` / `InChallenge` slide `N_All_00` by 120 units and fade the full-screen near-black picture `P_Overlay_00` (root level, over everything of the layout); `DetailIn` and `GuideIn` are alpha on null panes with text and picture children. (3) keystone does not take `adr` with an absolute address (`adr x8, #0x809d77a8`: "Unknown error"); a label inside the same text works, with the data as `.byte` lines after the last instruction (as the cloth hook has its strings). (4) `SeqOneShotStartSound` in our graphs plays on the header screen's sound user (`ScreenName: MenuOverlay_00`, from the template).

## What we know about the − menu

- Flow: `Sequence/*.module.ainb` (AINB v0x408). `MinusMenu.module` picks the start page by `SeqQueryGetCurrentMinusMenuCategory` (Important 6, Challenge 1, Map 2); pages read their own buttons (`SeqQueryControllerTrigMaskChecker` outputs) and leave with StateEnd `Next`/`Prev`/`Main`/`PouchMenu`/`Event`. Page order Key Items -> Threads of Time -> Map; Key Items had no `Prev`. Module files are loaded by path (no list; `Sequence/FileEntry` only swaps in `.110` versions); a new module needs a size-table entry.
- AINB input flag 0x1000000 (the `ainb` package calls it "Is Output"): in vanilla it is set only when the value equals the node type's declared default (`Sequence/NodeDefinition/Node.Product.111.aidefn.byml.zs`, which also names the enum types), and the game then uses the declared default. When changing a copied node's value, drop the flag (`vanilla()` in `build.py` does). Before this fix, `IsDirect=true` and `IsVisible=true` on copied nodes acted as false.
- Size table: AINB = `align32(size) + 376`; RSDB tables = `align32(size) + 264` (except ActorInfo). Text archive slack +336 bytes.
- 3D view: `SeqOneShotSetPouchActorDisplayType`: 0 = 3D Link (animated, right stick rotates), 1 and 4 = nothing, 2 = tapestry, 3 = Key Items stones, 5 = menu closed. Switching is instant. The 3D UI camera actor (`Pack/Actor/3DUICamera.pack.zs`) runs code-side `ExecuteUpdate3DUICamera` while the display type is 0-3. Env sets (`SeqExecuteApplyEnvSet`, ModelSceneType 1; files `gsys/EnvSetParam/*.gsys__EnvSetParam.bgyml`): "ImportantMenu", "Tapestry", "Pouch" (+ menu; brighter, preferred for Link). Applying an env set blanks the 3D until the display type is set again (about a frame).
- Camera states (`SeqOneShotSetUICameraState`): vanilla pages set 1 (enter from left), 2 (enter from right), 3 (settled), 4 (leave right), 5 (leave left). With Link shown, our page setting 0 or 2 turned the view black for good; 3 is harmless; the states left by Key Items / Threads of Time do no harm. Our page sets none.
- Screens (`RSDB/UIScreen.Product.111.rstbl.byml.zs`: Name, BodyLayoutFile, Class, LifetimeGroup, DrawUnitId, SortKey, ...): menu pages are draw unit 1, sort 5000; header `MenuOverlay_00` 8749; `EventFade_01` (menu fade, loaded on demand) 9000 = above the header, so fading with it also fades the header (looks wrong for tab changes); `EventFade_00` (draw unit 0) is not visible in menus. Nothing sits between the pages and the header. Several screens may share one layout (EventFade_00/01).
- **The UI layout archives store every layout 4 times** (628 SARC entries, 157 names, identical copies, same name and hash), and the game uses more than the first entry. Edit all entries (`nxpack.sarc_patch_all`); `nxfmt.sarc_files` now refuses differing duplicates and `nxfmt.sarc_entries` lists every entry. (Before this was found, edits went to the first entry only and looked as if the emulator ignored them. Useful runtime path if ever needed: the header screen's PlusToMinus constants are at `[[[[screen+0x310]+0x20]+0]+0]+0x38`.) Screen object fields seen live: preset +0x354, category +0x358, mode byte +0x350, slot count +0x328, slot array +0x330. In memory the layouts are relocated in place inside the decompressed archive (file offsets become pointers).
- Layouts: `UI/Layout2Archive/<Name>.Product.100.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.blarc.zs` (SARC of `.buil` files; `Common` holds the menus), no public editor. `tools\buil.py` reads animations: targets (pane index = key byte 1 >> 3, correct only below 16; full rule in the layout section, `uiedit.key_pane`), property group, property) and constant tables; enough for same-size edits. `MenuOverlay_00` panes 8-11 = `Pa_Category_00-03` (base x -690/-230/230/690, 400x224). The − / + header layouts are the end states of `PlusToMinus` / `MinusToPlus`; `OpenImportant` has a constant -520 (old Key Items tab x, probably a glow position; not adjusted). `ImportantMenu_01` anims: InChallenge, InFromChallenge, InFromPlus, DetailIn, GuideIn, DemoMessageIn, IsOpen, IsMedal, IsDemo, RubyOn. `EventFade_00`: Color, VeryShort/Short/Middle/Long In/Out.
- Header/footer: class `UIMenuOverlayScreen` (layout `MenuOverlay_00`, 4 slots `Pa_Category_00-03`, each a `PaMenuCategory_00` part with anims Type / CurrentIn / CurrentOut). `SeqOneShotOpenAppMenuOverlay` -> `setPreset` (0x80a88a40): stores the preset at +0x354 and maps it to the current category at +0x358 (0,1 -> 1 Threads; 2,3,16-19 -> 2 Map; 4 -> 3 Equipment; 5,6 -> 4 Items; 7 -> 0 Collection; 8,9,12-15 -> 5 System; 10,11 -> 6 Key Items; others: unchanged), then builds the footer (0x80a897b0, per-preset button hints) and the header (0x80a89220). Header: + presets fill slots with categories 0,3,4,5; − presets with 6,1,2 and hide slot 3. Per slot: `0x80a8ab50(slot, category, enabled)` sets the text (category enum name as a `LayoutMsg/MenuOverlay_00` label, "Unopened" when not enabled; categories > 6 use one default label) and the − style (`Type` anim) for categories in mask 0x46; `0x802ecf60(slot, is_current, animate)` highlights; `0x80228350(slot, 0)` shows. Category enum names (0-6): CollectMenu, ChallengeLog, MapMenu, EquipmentMenu, ItemMenu, SystemMenu, ImportantMenu. Node definitions name the enums (`game::ui::MenuActorMgr::CameraState`, `DisplayType`, `UIMenuOverlayScreen::Preset`, ...) but the executable keeps no value names for them.
- Text: − header tab names are `Mals/<lang>.Product.110.sarc.zs` -> `LayoutMsg/MenuOverlay_00.msbt` (label `ImportantMenu` = "Key Items"). The emulator runs US English.

- Controller input in sequence graphs (2026-11-10): `SeqQueryControllerGetMask` (inputs `ControllerIndex` 0, `ControllerUserName` "UI") gives pointers to five masks (hold, release, repeat, trig, trig with repeat); `SeqQueryControllerTrigMaskChecker` / `HoldMaskChecker` / `ReleaseMaskChecker` (the last in no stock graph) turn one mask into 16 bools, found by name (`TrigA` ... in the file's own order). The bits are sead's: A 0, B 1, ZL 2, X 3, Y 4, ZR 5, RS click 6, LS click 7, - 9, + 10, L 13, R 14, Up / Down / Left / Right 16-19; sead also has left stick up / down / left / right at 20-23 (present in the "UI" user's trigger mask: confirmed in game, 2026-11-10), which no checker output reads (calc routines: trig 0x807750b0, hold 0x8077d3c0, release 0x8330f390; the release one calls 0x80854960(slot, value) per output, slots at node + 0x28 + 8 * i in the order A B X Y L R ZL ZR Plus Minus Up Down Left Right LSPush RSPush). The menus read the stick in their screens' code (analog value against a threshold, input names like `TrigLSLeft`), so a graph-only screen has no stick without code. `SeqQueryGetButtonInputDirection` reads a field of the + menu's screen object and `SeqQueryControllerGetStick` (inputs as GetMask, outputs `LeftStick`, `RightSitck`) is in no stock graph. Key Items leaves for the + menu with `CloseAllMinusMenuContentScreen.module` (default flags: its screen closed at once), display type 4, command `PouchMenu`.

## What we know about player models and game data

- Model mods (GameBanana tutorial 20479) replace `romfs/Model/<Project>.<Fmdb>.bfres.zs` (made with Switch Toolbox) and whatever textures they need in `romfs/Tex/<name>.bntx.zs`, plus a size table when a file grows. The N64 Young Link mod: `Player_Child.Player_Child.bfres` (216 KB vs 5.7 MB) + `Mt_Vest_Player_Child_{Alb,AO,Nrm,Rgh}`; N64 Link: `Player_Adult.Player_Adult.bfres` + `Mt_Angin_Acs_Player_Adult_*`. Their models still name other stock textures (face, hair) too. Legend of Leon (`legend_of_leon_v10.zip`, not used yet) also replaces both player models, `Mt_Vest_Player_*` textures, the slingshot, both voice banks (`Sound/Resource/Player_*.bars.zs`, 145 stream `.bwav`s) and `ELink2/elink2.Product.110.belnk.zs`; it ships an installer exe we never run.
- Textures are separate files found by name: a model's materials list texture names (arrays of string pointers in the bfres, no dictionary), the game loads `Tex/<name>.bntx`; a `.bntx` holds its own name once. Size table rule for textures: `align32(size) + 264`; models take far more than their size (stock Player_Child 5.7 MB -> 6.0 MB entry), so new models get the entry of the model they stand in for.
- Actors: `Pack/Actor/<Name>.pack.zs` (SARC). `Component/ModelInfo/<x>.engine__component__ModelInfo.bgyml` = `ModelProjectName` + `FmdbName` -> file `Model/<Project>.<Fmdb>.bfres`. `Player_Child` and `PouchPlayer_Child` (the menu's 3D Link) use the same ModelInfo `Player_Child`; `Dm_Player_Child_Op` (opening) uses `Player_Child_Op`, Dark Link `Player_Adult_Dark`. The game has no runtime model switch for the player: alternative models are separate actors.
- Path format `"Model/%s.%s.bfres"` (0x83cafff3): 12 references, 11 calls to the string printf 0x80062b70 (`Character models` hooks them). 0x809727c0 is a model-load task run from a queue (0x8068d9b0) with a once-only flag (bit 0x800 at +0x3c). Not known yet whether the model cache is keyed by the formatted path or by the name (decides whether the menu's Link can change while the field Link still holds the old model).
- Field Link is a "resident actor": `LoadBancImpl` creates it with `SeqExecuteSceneRequestCreateResidentActors(IsCreateChildPlayer = Sequence_IsChildhood)`. Normal area changes only put it to sleep (`SleepResidentActor.module`); it is deleted only by `ChangeBanc` with `IsDeleteResidentActorsBeforeDestroyBanc` (only the Opening sets it), by return to title (`PlusMenuToTitle`) and on scene destroy. So a new model shows on the field after return to title + Continue (or an age change), not at the next door.
- Menu Link (`PouchPlayer_Child/Adult`): created by `LoadBancImpl` (`SeqOneShotCreatePouchPlayer`, actor path is a node input), deleted and re-created there when its age does not match; also deleted when loading a save from the + menu (`_LocalModule_bde133631675`) and on return to title.
- Menu actor manager = `[[0x84c9e2a8] + 0x4e8]` (the menu nodes' code, e.g. 0x80c384a0). Handles of 0x20 bytes {pending request, handle (vtable, link, id)}: 3D UI camera at +0x08, pouch Link +0x28, then +0x48 (unused here), tapestry +0x68, collection background +0x88; the actor is `[link + 0x40]` (live check: id at `link + 0x48` matches). `SeqOneShotCreatePouchPlayer` (0x80c38510) creates at the origin with scale 1 and only if the slot is empty; `SeqOneShotCreateUICamera` (0x80c38350) does nothing while a camera exists. **`SeqOneShotDeletePouchPlayer` (0x80c35c30) also deletes the 3D UI camera**, and only `LoadBancImpl` creates one (after the pouch Link, if the banc parameter `IsUse3DUICameraActor`), so re-creating the menu Link mid-area needs `SeqOneShotCreateUICamera` afterwards; without it the menu used a default camera (Link's boots at the top of the screen) until the next area load. Seen live (2026-11-09): menu Link at (0, 0.3, 0) turned -30° (MenuActorParam), camera at (0.07, 1.15, 4.49); actor fields: base position +0x2ec, base rotation +0x2f8 (3x3), current position +0x31c, rotation +0x328, scale +0x34c.
