- **Actors created while the − menu is open are not fully set up until the next area load** (2026-11-09, live diff of the same menu Link and camera actors before / after walking through a door, with `SeqOneShotCreateUICamera` added to the swap): position, rotation and camera spot were already right (Link (0, 0.3, 0), camera (0.07, 1.15, 4.49)), but both actors had empty pointers at +0x148/+0x150 (an embedded object at +0x140, vtable image 0x84c79420) and +0x3f8/+0x400 (object at +0x3f0, vtable 0x84c93958), and the menu Link a different id list at +0x1d8 (count + ids; after the door 3 of 6 ids changed). The door filled them in without re-creating either actor, and the view became right. Closing and reopening the menu does not. The collection background (created during an area load and shown) has them; the tapestry (not shown) has them empty. These turned out to be red herrings: +0x140 and +0x3f0 are nodes of actor lookup trees (their neighbours are random actors), +0x458 is a counter. The real cause: the camera's numbers (menu manager +0x22c + 0x38 * display type: position, aim point, up, near, far, fovy; display type at +0x228, camera state at +0x30c) and the actors were all right in the broken state, but **a 3D UI camera created while the menu is open only becomes the active view after the menu pause ends** (closing and reopening the menu fixed it). Fix: keep the old camera when the page swaps the menu Link (`CharacterMenu_KeepUICamera` + the patch at 0x80c35c64).
- Layers (live 2026-11-09): manager `[0x84ea1cf0]` (+0x20 count, +0x28 array of 32); a layer's viewport is floats at +0x30 (min x, y) / +0x38 (max x, y) in 1280 x 720 units, render info at +0x80 (aspect at +0xb0). Tags -> layer index via `[[0x84eb50a8]+0x38]` (groups at +8, mask +0x108; each a sorted tag list and the layer index at +0x38). The menu 3D layer (tag 0xb9f65e9a = -1175036262, also the 3D UI camera's LayerTag) was layer 6; the main layer tag 0x1b6ce343 (460120899) layer 0.
- Debug server: the game's `main` runs 0xacb000 above the image addresses (live 0x80aeb000; find it by searching memory for code bytes from `image.bin`), so a global at image address A is read at A + 0xacb000. `tools\live.py` finds the shift itself and prints our character flag and the menu actors (handle ids, names, positions); its GameData lookup is the same as the model hook's.
- GameData: `GameData/GameDataList.Product.110.byml.zs` (big-endian BYML): per type an array of {Hash, DefaultValue, ResetTypeValue, SaveFileIndex (-1 not saved, 0 progress.sav per slot, 1 option.sav, 2 caption.sav, 3 account.sav)}; arrays are not sorted. Hash = murmur3_32(full name), e.g. `Sequence_IsChildhood` 0xcea220d3. Sequence nodes: `SeqOneShotSetNonStructGameDataInt` / `SeqQueryGetNonStructGameData*` (by `GameDataFullName`).
- GameData in memory: manager = `[[0x84c9e180]]` (GOT entry). Bool: hash map +0x20 (capacity +0x2c), records +0x18 (count +0x10, 0x18 bytes, value byte +0x16). Int: hash map +0xe0 (capacity +0xec), records +0xd8 (count +0xd0, 0x28 bytes, value +0x1c). Hash maps: {u32 hash, u32 record index}, open addressing from hash % capacity, empty slot = hash 0.
- GameData sets from sequence nodes are queued, not written at once: `SeqOneShotSetNonStructGameDataInt` by name ends in 0x80183b70(manager, value, hash), which appends (value, hash) to a fixed-size queue (entries at +0xf8, capacity +0xf0, counter +0x100) applied later, and jumps to an abort stub (0x8351c280) when the queue is full. So a flag set is not visible to code in the same step (why set / delete / clear of the keep-camera flag in one frame did nothing), and a sequence that sets a flag and returns to a button loop in the same frame, while the button trigger is still set, loops until the game aborts ("Userspace PANIC" on D-pad Up, 2026-11-09). Put a `SeqExecuteGenericWaitFrame` (template: FastLoadOn node 4) after such a set, or spread the steps over frames (`IsUpdateNextInFrame` false).
- Emulator log (`%APPDATA%\eden\log\eden_log.txt`): `Module rtld loaded at X (size 0x20000)` gives main = X + 0x20000, so crash backtrace addresses map to image addresses by subtracting (X + 0x20000 - 0x80020000).
- Reset: `SeqExecuteResetGameData ResetType=N` resets flags whose ResetTypeValue has bit N (most saved Ints: 8 = bit 3, reset by `NormalTitle` ResetType 3). Scene destroy (`DestroySceneCommon`, e.g. return to title) resets ALL game data (`SeqExecuteResetGameDataAllForDebug`, runs in retail unless the debug flag `IsSkipForceResetGameData` is set), so a flag that should survive return to title must be saved.
- Save files (`%APPDATA%\eden\nand\user\save\0000000000000000\<user>\0400486024DEC000\slot_NN\progress.sav`, `option.sav`, ...): header 0x20 (magic 0x01020304, version 0x002d80eb, offset of the variable data), then per type a marker (0, type index) and (hash, value) pairs, then the variable data (Bool64bitKey flags, arrays, strings), then the file's SaveTypeHash. The loader (0x80bc26c0) looks each hash up and keeps unknown ones aside (0x8378c050 / 0x8378ce10), so new flags don't break old saves and saves with extra flags still load in the stock game. Bookkeeping per extra scalar flag: `AllDataSaveOffset` / `AllDataSaveSize` +8 (AllDataSaveOffset = 0x20 + 8 * (all flags except Struct, BoolExp, Bool64bitKey + 34)), and `SaveDataOffsetPos[f]` / `SaveDataSize[f]` +8 for its save file f (SaveDataOffsetPos[0] = 0x20 + 8 * (file-0 flags except Bool64bitKey + 34) = 95704, matches the save header).
- Save backup before the first test with our saved flag: `%USERPROFILE%\Games\OoT-save-backups\2026-11-09 before Character models`.
- **The equipment's 21 handles** (customization session, 2026-11-14: read from the game's code offline, for a later option that swaps single pieces; "seen" = also read from snaildev's running game as a child on 2026-11-13; the rest was never seen in game, `tools\equiplive.py` reads all of it from a running game). The equipment component is `game::component::EquipmentUser`, `[[actor + 0x270] + 0x200]` (its +0x18 the actor), parameters in `Component/EquipmentUserParam/*.bgyml`. A handle is 0x18 bytes (+8 link, +0x10 id; the actor is `[link + 0x40]` while `[link + 0x48]` is the id). The game numbers things three ways: a SLOT (18, what every "equip this" routine takes: 0 Weapon, 1 SubWeapon, 2 ThirdWeapon, 3 Sheath, 4 Quiver, 5 Bow, 6 Shield, 7 ArmorHead, 8 ArmorUpper, 9 ArmorLower, 10 ArmorHand, 11 Tool, 12 SubTool, 13 to 16 Accessory1 to 4, 17 Extra; names in the table at 0x83f19540; per slot a pending request at component + 0x10b0 + 8 * slot and a state at + 0x12d4 + 8 * slot: 1 asked, 2 made, 3 failed), eight DYNAMIC handles from + 0x20 (drawn and put away) and thirteen STATIC handles from + 0xe0 (worn). One routine, 0x80398360 (run from the field player's update when the requests are done), puts a finished piece into its handle and says which slot and which KIND of actor each handle takes (the kind is the index of a component the piece must have, else it is thrown away): handle 0 Weapon (slot 0, kind 0x8b weapon: the sword, seen), 1 SubWeapon (slot 1, weapon: the C-button item in hand, a bottle, Deku stick, hammer or rod; seen with a bottle), 2 ThirdWeapon (slot 2, weapon; nothing known), 3 Bow (slot 5, kind 0x30: bow, slingshot), 4 Shield (slot 6, kind 0x79; seen), 5 and 6 Tool and SubTool (slots 11 and 12, kind 0x3b: NPCs' things, the player's bone names for them are empty), 7 Helmet (never filled by that routine); 8 Head (slot 7, kind 0x2a armour: a mask, the Lens of Truth, else `Armor_<Age>_Default_Head`; seen with `Armor_Child_Mask_Skull`), 9 upper body (slot 8, armour: the Goron / Zora tunic; the name `Armor_Adult_Normal` means "empty this handle"), 10 Lower (slot 9, armour: Iron / Hover boots), 11 Hand (slot 10, armour: Silver / Golden gauntlets), 12 Sheath (slot 3, kind 0x78; seen), 13 and 14 SubSheath and ThirdSheath (never filled), 15 Quiver (slot 4, kind 0x78), 16 Accessory1 (slot 13, kind 0x20: the ocarina; seen), 17 Accessory2 (slot 14: the child's Goron bracelet, `Armor_Child_Gauntlet_Goron`; seen), 18 Accessory3 (slot 15: the Stone of Agony, by the names; a strong guess), 19 Extra (slot 17, armour; nothing known), 20 Accessory4 (slot 16: the ocarina's holder, the ocarina's name + `_Holder`; seen). So a substitute for a handle must be an actor of that handle's kind. Per-handle bytes: component + 0x13bc + d (dynamic) and + 0x13c4 + k (static; bits 0-1 are set to 1 or 2 by 0x802e49a0): the bytes + 0x13c4 and + 0x13c6 that the pouch player's node writes (0x80a9abc4) are therefore the HEAD's and the BOOTS', not a weapon's or shield's drawn state as an earlier note here has it; what 1 and 2 mean is not read (it may be the game's own switch for showing a piece).
- **The equipment actors** (same source; every one is `Pack/Actor/<name>.pack.zs`, its model `Model/<ModelProjectName>.<FmdbName>.bfres.zs` from its ModelInfo). Swords (handle 0): `Weapon_Sword_Kokiri` (child), `Weapon_Sword_Master`, `Weapon_Sword_Biggoron`, `_Giant`, `_BrokenGiant` (adult; the last three in the model project `Weapon_Sword_Biggoron`), `Weapon_Sword_DarkLinkSword`. Sheaths (12): `Weapon_Sheath_Kokiri`, `Weapon_Sheath_070` (the Master Sword's), `Weapon_Lsheath_059` (the big swords'), `Weapon_Sheath_DarkLinkSword`. Shields (4): `Weapon_Shield_Deku`, `_Hylian`, `_Mirror`, `_DarkLinkShield`. Bows (3): `Weapon_Range_Bow` (adult), `Weapon_Range_SlingShot` (child); quiver (15) `Weapon_Quiver_001`. In hand (1): `Weapon_Bottle` and twelve `Weapon_Bottle_*`, `Weapon_DekuStick`, `Weapon_Hammer`, `Weapon_FishingRod`. Head (8): `Armor_Child_Default_Head` / `Armor_Adult_Default_Head` (the default: a hair piece of 6 bones), ten `Armor_Child_Mask_*` (Keaton, Skull, Spooky, Bunny, Truth, Goron, Zora, Gerudo, Amiibo0, Amiibo1), `Armor_Child_GlassTruth` / `Armor_Adult_GlassTruth`. Tunics (9): `Armor_Adult_Goron`, `Armor_Adult_Zora` (whole-body models of 168 / 178 bones with cloth bones of their own; `Armor_Adult_Normal` is never made). Boots (10): `Armor_Adult_HeavyBoot`, `Armor_Adult_HoverBoot`. Gauntlets (11): `Armor_Adult_Gauntlet_Silver`, `_Gold`. Ocarinas (16): `Item_Ocarina_Fairy`, `Item_Ocarina_Time`, their holders (20) `Item_Ocarina_Fairy_Holder`, `Item_Ocarina_Time_Holder`. Bracelet (17): `Armor_Child_Gauntlet_Goron`. Stone of Agony (18): `Armor_Child_AgonyStone`, `Armor_Adult_AgonyStone`. NOT pieces of the component: the hookshot, the longshot (held actors of the ActorHolder component) and the boomerang (a "shootable"). There are also 31 packs `Npc_Equipment_001..028` and `Npc_Equipment_Mask_*` (18 model files), what NPCs carry through the same component from blackboard strings (`EquipmentUser_Weapon`, `_Shield`, `_Head`, `_Upper`, ...; 0x80d8c0b0): which handles could take them is not looked at.
- **How the game picks a piece, and where another could be put in** (same source). (1) The pouch writes a NAME: 0x80a9acd0(pouch, &item name, ...) takes the item's row of `PouchActorInfo` and stores its `ChildEquipment` if the player is a child (the byte `[[0x84e9cfa8] + 0x5c0]`), else its `AdultEquipment`, in the GameData member its category asks for. (2) The names are saved: GameData struct `PlayerStatus` (hash 0x060890b0), String64 members `EquipWeapon`, `EquipShield`, `EquipArrow`, `EquipHeadArmor`, `EquipArmor`, `EquipBoot`, `EquipOcarina`, `EquipHandArmor` and the Bool `IsEquipAgonyStone`; they are used as actor names as they are. (3) 0x82e16c80(component, slot) reads the member of a slot and asks for that actor; 0x80f401b0 / 0x80f402c0 run it for the field character and the menu character. (4) Every request, the player's, an NPC's blackboard string's or a node's, goes through one of two routines: dynamic 0x82e17b20(component x0, slot w1, pointer to the name pointer x2, state w3, w4, w5, w6), which also asks for the weapon's sheath (slot 3) and the bow's quiver (slot 4); static 0x81033f20(component x0, slot w1, pointer to the name pointer x2, fade w3, by the pouch w4), with 0x81033ea0 before it for the tunic. (5) The C-button items are not slots: the ActorHolder component makes them (`ActorHoldSettings` in the player's `ActorHolderParam`) and 0x82e1b440 puts one into a dynamic handle. Where to substitute, best first: per character in code, at the entry of 0x82e17b20 and 0x81033f20 (they have the component, whose +0x18 is the actor, the slot and the name before anything is made; the substitute must be an actor the game can load and of the handle's kind; whether an actor outside the player's pack loads without a hitch is NOT known, only a patched build says); for everybody in data, `PouchActorInfo` (`ChildEquipment`, `AdultEquipment`, `WeaponSheathName`, `BowQuiverName`: the Kokiri sword as an adult is one missing `AdultEquipment`), `EquipmentUserParam.HeadArmorDefaultActorName`, `ActorHoldSettings`, or a piece's own ModelInfo; NOT the `PlayerStatus` strings (saved, and one set for every character) and not a write to a handle (the actor would not be bound).
- **How a piece is attached** (same source). One-bone pieces hang on a bone the USER's `EquipmentUserParam` names: sword and item in hand `Weapon_L`, shield `Weapon_R` (drawn) / `Pod_A` (on the back), bow `Weapon_R`, sheath `Pod_A`, quiver `Pod_B`, the ocarina and its holder `Pod_B`, the bracelet `Ph_Bangle` (child), the Stone of Agony `Spine_2`, each with rotate / translate offsets per age. All 200 NPC player models of the full build have `Weapon_L`, `Weapon_R`, `Pod_A`, `Pod_B`, `Spine_2`, `Neck` and `Head` (`Ph_Bangle` the child models only, as Link), so such a piece sits at the character's own hand, back and hip. ARMOURS are bound bone for bone to the user's skeleton and are Link's body parts: a tunic, boots or gauntlets are drawn at Link's size on whoever wears them, and they HIDE materials of the user's model by name (`ArmorParam.HideMaterialGroupNameList` through the user's `HiddenMaterialGroupList`: the tunics hide `Mt_Inner`, `Mt_Angin`, `Mt_Angin_Acs`, `Mt_Vest`, `Mt_Leater_Upper`, `Mt_Feather`; boots `Mt_Boots...`; gauntlets `Mt_Glove...`), so a character whose own model has a material of such a name loses it while an adult wears one. For the customize folder: "Equipment hidden" hid handles 9 to 11 with the gear until 2026-11-14 (an adult in a tunic would have had no body there; never seen, snaildev's looks were as a child); they are out of its mask since. And a clothing colour's palette on the player model does not reach a Goron / Zora tunic, which is the armour actor's own model.

## What we know about UI layouts (.buil)

- File: `BFFH` header (u32 size at +8, section table at +0x10: `BFSI` entries {u32 offset, u32 size, u32 align, u64 offset, name[16]}, root at 0xf8, type name `Nintendo.Ui.ResModels.LayoutResData` at +0x28), section `Default` (objects) then `StringPool` (C strings and 6-byte animation keys). Pointers are u64 file offsets; the game relocates them in place by walking every reference by type (`LayoutResData` relocator 0x8153e804; material 0x8153e5e0, picture data 0x8153e720, part 0x8153f900, slide-list part 0x81540000, pass 0x8153fb40, animation 0x8153fec0 -> 0x815723e0 -> curves 0x815722e0, whose two keys objects are relocated by type-word handlers; every vanilla curve has type 0). Objects are written depth first in field order; arrays are {u64 ptr, u32 count, u32 stride}; dictionaries are {keys, values, hashes, count, stride} written as values, keys, hashes, 32 zero bytes, then the objects; hash = CityHash64 of the name. Strings enter the pool in visit order (deduplicated).
- Root (0x228): name +0x18; dictionaries in pane order: panes +0x20 (record 0x44), materials +0x40, pictures +0x60 (P_ and W_), texts +0x80 (0xa8: font, size, text, message ref), alignments +0xa0, parts +0xc0, captures +0xe0, slide lists +0x100, animations +0x140, window extensions +0x160, capture textures +0x180; passes (shader setup with texture names, archive "ui") +0x130, colour palette +0x1c8 (RGBA floats; materials index it), float palette +0x1d8, pane alpha table +0x1e8, name table +0x1f8 (names of things inside the layout's parts that are referred to by index: part animations driven from here, part panes whose properties an instance overrides), per-material u32s +0x208, screen size 3840x2160 at +0x200.
- **Typed records repeat their pane's indices** (100 % on the 164 layouts): picture +0x10 = material index; text +0x88 = material, +0x8c = pane index; capture +0x0 = material, +0x4 = pane; slide list +0x28 = pane; part +0x40 = pane. After any insert these must be rewritten for every later pane (`uiedit` does since 2026-11-09; the first sandbox layout had 81 stale ones, e.g. the fade overlay pointing at another pane's material).
- Pane: +0x10 scale, +0x18 rotation (radians), +0x1c translate (y up, centre of screen = 0,0), +0x24 alpha table index, +0x28 parent, +0x2c size, +0x34 end of subtree, +0x38 flags (bit 0 visible, (flags >> 7) & 7 = type: 0 null, 1 picture/window, 2 text, 3 alignment, 4 scissor, 5 capture, 6 part, 7 slide list; bits 12+ two-bit anchor fields), +0x3c material | window-extension << 16, +0x40 type index << 16. Each material owns a run of passes (1 for pictures, 5 for texts) from material +0x14.
- Part instance record: layout name, overrides, property paths (start with the part's own pane index as a string), initial frames of the part's own animations ({name, f32 frame}; e.g. `ItemPattern` picks an equipment button's icon, the header's tab shapes), text overrides, own pane index at +0x40.
- Texts (from the code: text resolver 0x837a0cd0 -> label builder 0x80791bb0, message file `LayoutMsg/<name the screen returns>.msbt` built at 0x80459950, in `Mals/<lang>.Product.110.sarc.zs`): the reference string (text record +0x18) decides. `*` or a null pointer: the label is the pane's path, the names of the part panes it sits in and its own name joined with `-` (`Pa_Parts_00-T_Icon_00`; 0x80791d40). `%Label`: that label. `=path`: the label of another pane (`=T_Btn_00`, `=../../Pa_HeadEquip_00/Pa_Guide_00/T_Guide_00`). `&`: a path with `/`. Anything else, `@...`, `-` and also a non-null empty string as the builder is written, gives no lookup: the game's code sets the text, or it stays empty. Vanilla panes with an empty reference do have path labels in the message files (`Pa_CheckButton_01-T_Btn_00`), so the empty case reaches the builder as a null pointer somewhere we have not read; use `*` for our own panes. A part instance can replace the reference of a text pane inside the part (strings in its record at +0x38, picked by 24-byte override entries at +0x8 whose key names the pane through the name table and whose type is 0x19; e.g. `@T_Btn_00`, `-`); not decoded far enough to write. Text record: +0x08 font size x/y, +0x70 and +0x78 horizontal alignment (0 left, 1 centre, 2 right), +0x74 vertical (0 top, 1 centre, 2 bottom), +0x7c / +0x80 spacing floats, +0x38 / +0x54 / +0x58 colour palette indices.
- Setting a text pane's string from code (read in the stock lift, `build-hookshot/work-stock/sa/gen`, 2026-11-14; nothing built on it yet). The core is `0x80459680(textbox, const char16_t *str, int len, -1)` (a ui2d TextBox's set string), reached through `0x80459520(pane holder, msg)` where `msg` is `{u16 *ptr @0, i32 len @8, @16 stored at holder+16}`. `0x8045b970(screen part, key, msg, 0, 0)` walks the panes of a screen part and sets every text pane whose reference string is `@<key>` (so `@...` references are the panes the game fills by code). The header uses it per slot (`0x80a8ab50`): `0x8045c4a0("T_Text_00", layout at screen+0x20, &out)`, the message `{ptr,len}` from the label lookup `0x80a8b060` (message file `LayoutMsg/MenuOverlay_00`), then `0x8045b970`. The graph node that does this, `SeqOneShotSetMessageToLayout` (stock body 0x83bd5380-0x83bd54dc: inputs at node +136 / +152, label lookup, then an asynchronous request through `0x83be9670` / `0x81006110`), is NOT usable: its body holds the slide hook. `SeqExecuteUIPrototypingText` is no route either: its enter and update are empty in this build (only its inputs FontScale, Message, VisibleSeconds are bound). The label test (`mods\label_test.py`, folder `Character (a label test)`, 2026-11-14) does it with our own routine: screen id = `0x801939e0(name, 0)` (murmur3), screen = `0x83be9670([[GOT 0x84ca0968] + 0x38], id, 0)`, holder = `screen - 0xe8` (`0x8045b970` reads the layout at `[holder + 0x80]`: it adds 0x40 and `0x8045c040` reads `[+0x40]`; a second try with `screen - 0xa8` crashed on a null layout) (for a screen of the default class, as our page's, the holder lies INSIDE the object the lookup returns; read from the running game with `tools\labelprobe.py`, 2026-11-14: the first try took the header class's pointer at +0x20, read garbage and crashed), then `0x8045b970(holder, "T_Title_00", &{text, bytes, 0}, 0, 0)`. Texts are UTF-8 in this build, the length in bytes (the title's text box held "Character Select" with 16) on the picker's title, whose reference the test folder sets to `@T_Title_00`. The TextBox keeps the text's pointer (no copy), so the text must live for good (ours is in the code). The routine is the one-shot body of the unused `SeqOneShotClearLODJudgeLayer` (0x832edb10-0x832edbd4, 178 bytes used; a node of that class, made from `SeqOneShotSetLayerVisible`'s with its one input `LayerTag`, runs one frame after the page's entry): keep other patches off it. Seen in game (third build, snaildev, 2026-11-14): the title read TEST, so the route works; but the game hung, because a one-shot body must end as the stock ones do, `[node + 24] = 0x80000000 | (low 30 bits)` (done), or its sequence waits for ever. The fourth build does that on every way out: PASSED (snaildev, 2026-11-14: TEST, page turns, the game carries on). LIVE TEXT PASSED (snaildev, 2026-11-14: "text changes and game kept running fine"): the routine reads its text and length from a buffer at the end of the padding after .rodata (0x8486cf00-0x8486d000: 16-byte marker `label_test.MARKER`, u32 length, the UTF-8 text; keep other patches off it), and `tools\labelset.py "<text>"` writes a new one into the RUNNING game from outside: it opens lichii.exe, finds the marker in the process's memory (ReadProcessMemory, about a minute) and writes there (WriteProcessMemory); the debug server reads it back at the game's own address. No emulator change. The text shows the next time the page opens, since the text box lays its letters out only when the setter runs.
- Colours: a material's +0x18..+0x24 are four colour palette indices (corners), +0x28 the white colour and +0x2c the black colour: the texture's grey is mapped black -> white. Threads of Time's entry diamond (`PaChallengeIndex_00`): `P_DotBase_00` (texture `ChallengeIndexDot_00`, 80 x 80: opaque frame, dark middle; white = palette 5, dark brown) is the empty diamond; `P_Dot_00` (`ChallengeIndexDot_01`: a smaller grey ramp; black -> white = palette 7 -> 6, (0.738, 0.074, 0.014) -> (1, 0.212, 0.09), deep red to orange red) is the gem inside. No animation shows or hides `P_Dot_00`: the game's code does. `ChallengeState` 0 / 1 / 2 = diamond shown / hidden / hidden with the title dimmed; `ChallengeType` 2 = indented entry; `Select` 0 -> 4 = cursor in.
- Pane flag bits 12-23 are two-bit fields: 12-13 base position horizontal (0 left, 1 centre, 2 right), 14-15 vertical (0 top, 1 centre, 2 bottom), 18-19 / 20-21 which edge of the parent the position is measured from (same values; read off vanilla data, e.g. gauges that grow from the bottom; not confirmed in game), 16-17 and 22-23 unknown (1, sometimes 3). A part's footprint: its `B_Hit_00` when it has one, but some hit boxes cover only part of what is drawn (`PaOptionBtn_00`: 816 of 1880 wide) and some base pictures are far larger than the visible shape (equipment / item slots: 700 x 700 picture, 220 x 540 hit box). Part instance pane sizes look like editor bounding boxes (fractional values) and nothing is known to use them.
- Scissor panes (`S_`, type 4, flags 0x0255522b: a null pane's + type + bit 5) clip their children: Ocarina's melody strip is scissor -> null pane that scrolls -> parts, the pattern the sandbox list copies.
- Animations: Anim (0x30) -> data (name, targets, curves, per-curve component bytes, constants, per-constant component bytes, frame range), part-animation events, frame count +0x28. Target key: b0 sub element (0x7f = the pane), pane = ((b1 >> 3) & 15) | (b2 & 31) << 4, b3 property (0 scale, 1 translate, 2 visibility, 4 alpha, 27+ material colours etc., 57+ text, 0x43/0x44 with b4 = 1 = frame / enable of a part's animation). Target: first curve, first constant, count (curves | constants << 8), value type (0 bool, 12 alpha/normalised, 14 float, 15 colour). Component bytes: offset in the value (0 x, 4 y, 8 z, 12 w). Curves are Hermite keys (times; value, slope pairs; doubled keys for slope changes). A layout animation can drive a part's own animation: b0 = index in the name table (root +0x1f8), the animation lists an event (name, pane index, 1) per driven part, the frame is normalised (0 = start, 1 = end; vanilla uses 0.5 for frame 1 of a 2-frame state animation), usually a curve, a constant 1.0 in 8 places. Verified on all 3,661 vanilla targets. The key is a 7-bit-per-byte bit stream (decoded at 0x81505fd8): sub element 10 bits (b0 | (b1 & 7) << 7, all ones = the pane), pane 10 bits ((b1 >> 3) & 15 | (b2 & 63) << 4; bit 9 is always set, so the index is the low 9: a pane with an animation target needs an index below 512, and inserting panes moves the ones after them, so keep a layout with animations under 512 panes; `uiedit` asserts it), property 9 bits ((b2 >> 6) & 1 | b3 << 1 | (b4 & 1) << 8): "b3 = 2" with b2 = 0x60 is visibility, with b2 = 0x20 it is the pane size (value type 13). Curve type word (+0x10): 0 clamp, 0x0400 repeat (the curves of looping animations), 0x0480 a second repeat kind; animation data +0x58 = 1 marks a looping animation (59 of 781). The two 40-byte objects at animation +0x8 / +0x10 are zero in every file.
- Animation runtime (0x802f1380 = a screen's update; requests from `SeqOneShotRequestPlay / StopAnimationScreen` are objects inside the graph node, queued on the screen and run in the order they were issued, at most once per node per frame): play (0x802ee030) and stop at a frame (0x802f2d90; ignored when the frame is beyond the animation's length) both set the frame, enable the animation and move it to the END of the screen's list of active animations. Each update walks that list in order: apply the animation at its frame (0x802f2c20: its own targets, then the part animations it drives, recursively), then advance it if it is playing. A stopped animation is applied once, then disabled and taken off the list; a played one stays until a frame or two after its end. So what an animation wrote stays until another one writes the same property, a later request wins inside a frame, and state can be kept in many independent animations ("stop at frame n" = show state n). Animations are found by name with a linear scan of the CityHash64 values, then a string compare (0x8045c870): order and position in the dictionary do not matter. More from the code (2026-11-10): a play request plays with the loop mode of the animation's own flag (0x80bcce80 reads animation data +0x58 bit 0 and passes it to 0x802ee030, which stores it in the entry at +0x16: 0 once, 1 wrap, 2 back and forth); at the end a wrapping one has its length subtracted from the frame and goes on. Applying an animation (0x802f2c20) does nothing unless its enable bit (animator +0x1c bit 0) is set, applies its own targets, then calls itself for each part animator it drives whose enable bit is set: a part animation driven by a screen animation is applied exactly when that screen animation is applied, at the frame its targets just wrote. So two screen animations can drive the same part animation (or two part animations that write the same panes), and while both are active the one requested later is applied later and wins.
- The equipment slot `PaEquipmentBtn_00` (23 panes): `ItemPattern` (25 frames, set as the part's initial frame) picks the shape through texture pattern lists in the materials (`MenuBtnBase_14 / _15 / _16 / _17 / _26` = patterns 0 to 4, the same suffixes for `Equip`, `Line`, `Era`, `CursorLine`, `CursorGlow`, `DecideGlow`): frames 0-4 pattern 0, tall (drawn 263 x 550, hit box 220 x 540); 5-7 pattern 1, hexagon (343 x 470, hit 320); 8-11 pattern 4, in game a trapezium, narrower at the bottom with slanted sides (356 x 404, widths 328 down to 302, hit 320; the four frames are identical); 12-24 pattern 2, square top with a pointed bottom (hit 340; 23 and 24 mirrored); 25 pattern 3, wide (hit 800 x 320). The Equipment page uses 2, 1, 0 / 7, 6, 5 / 11, 10, 8 for its grid (pitch 360 x 560 / 480) and 12, 20, 22, 23, 24, 25 for the head and foot slots. `State` (4 frames): 0 shadow background `P_Base_00` and no icon, 1 the same with the icon, 2 the orange background `P_Equip_00` with the icon (equipped), 3 / 4 `P_Era_00` with / without the icon. `IsCurrent` (1 frame): 0 = the orange colours `P_Equip_00`'s pass already has by default (shader parameters in the pass, not the material's white / black colours), 1 blue ones and `P_ActiveDeco_00`. Cursor: `Select` (8 frames, the cursor fades in), `UnSelect` (14, fades out), `Decide` (16, the glow of a choice), `CursorLoop` (looping, 1000 frames, its curves repeat every 40: the idle pulse); the game's code plays these on the part. `Select`, `UnSelect` and `Decide` write the same panes; `CursorLoop` writes other components of the cursor materials. Button glyphs are text: a text pane in the font `External_00` (font archive entry `External_00.buif` -> `Font2/Scalable/External.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.bfotf.zs`) showing private-use characters, U+E040 A, E041 B, E042 X, E043 Y, E044 L, E045 R, E046 ZL, E047 ZR, E04B +, E04C -, E050-E05F and E060-E06A sticks and D-pad (the full table is `StaticMsg/GuideKeyIcon.msbt`, label = button name). The - header's L / R are `T_BtnL_00` / `T_BtnR_00` of `MenuOverlay_00` with those characters as their messages (size 90, alpha 0.78); `PaGuideKey_00` (glyph `T_Icon_00` size 102 + right-aligned `T_Guide_00`) and `PaControllerKey*` are filled in by code. A mod that changes button prompts replaces that font file: checked on five (snaildev's downloads, 2026-11-10: keyboard / mouse, two DualSense, Xbox, PlayStation): each ships `Font2/Scalable/External.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.bfotf.zs` and the texture file `UI/Layout2Archive/Common.Product.100.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.bntx.zs` (the PlayStation one as an .ips of it); none ships the layout archive (.blarc) or messages, so they do not collide with our folders and our glyph panes show their glyphs. Only the Xbox one also ships a size table, which loses to ours by folder order (not tested whether its texture file needs its entry). The inventory slot `PaItemMenuBtn_00` (22 panes) is built the same way (same pane names, same `Select` / `UnSelect` / `Decide` / `CursorLoop` lengths and curves, `State` with 3 frames: 0 empty, 1 item, 2 orange, 3 era; no `IsCurrent`; plus an item count `N_Num_00` / `T_ItemNum_00` filled in by code and an amiibo icon) with 14 shapes `MenuBtnBase_00` to `_13` behind an `ItemPattern` of 35 frames: frame -> pattern 0:5, 1:4, 2:7, 3:6, 4-5:8, 6-7:9, 8:10, 9-22:13, 23:11, 24-27:2, 28-31:3, 32:0, 33-34:1, 35:12. Drawn sizes (texture alpha over 0.6, 1.5 units per texel): 0 487 x 644, 1 427 x 356, 2 472 x 487, 3 455 x 157, 4 310 x 326 (narrower at the bottom), 5 263 x 493, 6 292 x 359, 7 307 x 215, 8 259 x 263 (top and bottom edges cut short at one end), 9 280 x 215, 10 311 x 358 (pointed), 11 278 x 263 (wider at the bottom), 12 259 x 259 (round), 13 259 x 263 (straight sides: the squarest, hit box 200 x 220). The inventory page uses frames 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 4, 6, 7, 8 x 3, 9, 23 x 5, 24, 29, 30, 31, 32, 33, 35.
- Archives: a layout must be listed in `UI/LayoutArchiveCategory.Product.100.byml.zs` under its archive (Common); screens name it in `RSDB/UIScreen` (`BodyLayoutFile`). Size table rules: layout archive `align32(size) + 4144`, category table `align32(size) + 264`, Mals archive `align32(size) + 312`. Nodes in SARCs are sorted by name hash (multiplier 0x65), data aligned to 16.
- The Characters page's "light circle" (placeholder layout `ImportantMenu_01`) is Key Items' vignette, not a 3D light: window pane `W_Window_00` (4080 x 2160, material colour = palette 4, near black (0.013, 0.007, 0.004)) with two textures: `DecoNoise_01` (tiled 2 x 2, repeat) and the alpha mask `ImportantFade_00` (BC4, 1360 x 720 = one quadrant at 1.5 layout units per texel; component selector one/one/one/red = white with alpha from the mask). The window draws the mask as four mirrored quadrants: clear at the screen centre, 5 % dark 912 units out sideways / 482 up and down, half dark 1080 / 648, 98 % at 1344 / 904. It stays centred whatever the 3D camera does, and its clear middle went black during a character swap apparently because the 3D backdrop behind it is only drawn while the menu Link exists. The page's 2D layout is drawn over the menu's 3D Link: the vignette darkens his boots (snaildev, 2026-11-09; computed 96 % at his soles with the centred window), so page UI placed where Link stands covers him. (An earlier note here claimed the opposite from the fact that nobody had mentioned his feet.)
- Texture maps (material +0x0 array, 0x30 each): +0x10 scale x/y, +0x18 translate x/y, +0x20 rotation (radians), +0x24 mode (0 normal; 1, 2, 5 seen on icon/button parts). u' = sx * (u - 0.5) + 0.5 + tx (centre pivot, translate added after; same for v). Confirmed in game for x (the Characters page's narrowed vignette). Evidence beforehand: 35 vanilla maps flip with scale -1 and zero translate on clamped textures; `ChallengeMenuMask_00` (scale 2, translate -0.5) then clamps on its uniform left edge (the other sign would smear its non-uniform right edge). Pass texture bindings: bytes 0/1 = wrap u/v (0 repeat, 1 mirror, 2 clamp to edge, as in nn::gfx). Window record (picture +0x8): kind at +8 (1 x164, 3 x17), elements of 24 bytes (four floats and flags; not decoded).
- Item / equipment icons are stored pictures, not 3D renders: `UI/Tex/Icon/<actor name>.bntx.zs` (129 files: 84 `Item_*`, 19 `Armor_*`, 12 `Weapon_*`, ...; one texture each, format 0x2d06, square, 341 or 683 pixels, no mips) and `UI/Tex/IconSh/` (128). A button shows one through its part `PaReplaceIcon_00` (`PaEquipmentBtn_00`: `Pa_ReplaceIcon_00`), whose texture the code replaces by name. No character portraits exist among them. All are ASTC 4x4 with a real alpha channel (selectors r, g, b, a): transparent background (corners near 0), the object opaque with a hard edge (about 1 % partly transparent pixels) covering 3-62 % of the square, median 32 %. `IconSh/<name>_Sh` is the same silhouette smaller (205 or 411 pixels) with a soft edge (17 % partly transparent): the shadow. Measured with `toolsntx.py` (numbers only).
- Texture data layout on this platform (found by the NPC session on `Tex/*.bntx`, 2026-11-10; note for whoever decodes textures): block-linear as on the first Switch at the level of the 64-byte x 8-row groups, but the byte order inside a 512-byte group is `(x >> 5) << 8 | (y >> 2) << 7 | ((x >> 4) & 1) << 6 | (y & 3) << 4 | (x & 15)` (x in bytes 0-63, y 0-7), not the first Switch's. Proof: decoded with it, every tested texture matches its own next mip (mean error 0.2-1.3 of 255; 5-25 with the old order), for BC1 / BC4 / BC7 / ASTC. `tools\bntx.py` still uses the old order, which mixes up neighbouring blocks (right colours, scrambled detail: the likely reason small UI textures "did not decode cleanly"); `tools\npcportrait.py` has `ounce_deswizzle()` with the same interface. Not checked on the UI archives themselves (their textures have one mip).
- Layout texture files (`UI/Layout2Archive/<archive>...bntx`, version 4.1.1, platform tag `Ounc`; `tools\bntxwrite.py` reads and writes them, 2026-11-10): header (+0x10 file name, +0x16 first block, +0x18 relocation table, +0x1c file size), container at 0x20 (count, pointers to the record pointer array at 0x198, the data block, the name dictionary and a 0x140-byte memory pool at 0x58), `_STR` (strings as {u16 length, characters, NUL}, pointers point at the length), `_DIC` (Patricia tree over the names read as big-endian numbers; entry i + 1 = texture i), one record per texture (`BRTI` 0xa0 bytes: +0x1c format, +0x24 width, +0x28 height, +0x34 block height log2, +0x50 data size, +0x54 alignment, +0x58 component selectors, +0x60 name; then two 0x100-byte runtime areas, the mip pointer at +0x2a0 and, in all but four textures, a user data entry `TextureOriginalSize` = the size in layout units, 1.5 per texel: 0x318 bytes, 0x2a8 without it; the last record's size field runs to the data block), `BRTD` (16-byte header ending on a 0x1000 boundary, data at each record's alignment), `_RLT` (two sections: everything before the data, the data; per section the places of the pointers into it as {place, structures, pointers each, words skipped}). The stored block height: 16, halved while the texture's rows of blocks fill no more than two thirds of it (rows up to 10 -> 0, 21 -> 1, 42 -> 2, 85 -> 3, more -> 4; all 921 UI textures), and the data layout halves it further while the texture is no taller than half a block. Size table entry: align32(size) + 8264 (all three stock files). Proof of the writer: each of the three stock files rebuilt from its parts is byte for byte the original up to the relocation table, which lists the same pointers; a texture taken out, encoded again from its decoded pixels and added decodes the same (mean error under 0.2 of 255). The game's icons (`UI/Tex/Icon`) store straight alpha, not premultiplied (edge pixels at alpha 0.5 are 0.7 to 0.9 as bright as their opaque neighbours), with colour carried on into the transparent part.
- Common textures: `UI/Layout2Archive/Common.Product.100.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.bntx.zs` (878 `BRTI` records: format +0x1c, width +0x24, height +0x28, block height log2 +0x34, data size +0x50, component selectors +0x58, name +0x60, mip table +0x70; block-linear swizzle as on Switch for the large textures; small ones (under about 100 px) did not decode cleanly with the header's block height). `tools\bntx.py` decodes all of them: the block height has to be halved while the texture is no taller than half a block (yuzu's rule), whatever the header says. Formats in Common: BC4 x526 (masks: selectors one/one/one/red), ASTC 4x4 sRGB x350, ASTC 4x4 x2.
- Notes for the UI sandbox (`mods\ui_sandbox.py`, which uses `mods\build.py` as a library): (1) a sequence that returns to a button loop in the same frame as the button press runs again and again, because the trigger is still set (the main build's D-pad test aborted on this; the sandbox's `loop -> focus -> loop` on a D-pad press never yields, which is a frozen game). End such a sequence with `build.t_wait_frames(1)` or give it `same_frame=False` and more than one step. (2) `build.character_swap_steps(g, value)` is now one nested node that is safe in any sequence. (3) The page view is `build.t_set_flag(build.PAGE_VIEW_FLAG, True)` on entry and `False` on the R/ZR exit; closing the menu clears it (`close_page_with_menu`). (4) The test folder has passed in game: the page's layout comes from `build.page_layout()` (a `layout.Layout`): build the sandbox content on that instead of `ImportantMenu_01`.
- Pane order (customization session, 2026-11-13; a note for whoever writes layouts): the pane list is in pre-order, a pane, then its whole subtree, then its next sibling, with each pane's parent index and subtree end recorded (all 157 stock layouts and ours). `Editor.insert` takes a block in that order without checking it; a block with a pane's children after the pane's next sibling is written without complaint and passes `Editor.validate`, and in game panes then do not follow what an animation does to their parent (README, mods list, `Character (customize test)`, third look; `customize.preorder` is the check, a candidate for `Editor.validate`).

## What we know about NPC models, skeletons and animations

- Model files: `Model/<project>.<fmdb>.bfres.zs` hold one model named `<fmdb>` (FRES v0.10.4; layout in the head of `tools\bfres.py`: header, relocation table `_RLT` whose entries are grouped by the section the pointer points into, length-prefixed strings, dictionaries = Patricia tries over the name read as a big-endian number, FMDL 0x78, FSKL, bones 0x58, shapes 0x60). The header's "first block" u16 is the string pool offset cut to 16 bits, so nothing can be using the block chain. 5 models also exist as `.bfres.mc` next to the `.zs`.
- Bones: Euler XYZ rest rotations (skeleton flags 0x1200, scale mode Maya), world = parent x T x Rz x Ry x Rx x S; the inverse bind matrices (one per smooth matrix) are exactly the inverse of that. Flag bits 26 / 27 ("rotation / translation is zero") are hints that are sometimes clear although true, never the other way round. Rigid matrices (eyeballs) and weightless shapes use the bone's own frame for vertices, so such a bone's rest frame cannot change without touching vertices. Three models have a scaled bone (adult Link `Ph_Knee_L/R`, child Ruto `Ph_Neck_Assist`), always a leaf.
- The humanoid rig is shared: 98 `Npc_*` models have Link's names for the 60 body joints (Skl_Root, Spine_1/2, Neck, Head, Waist, per side Clavicle, Arm_1/2, Wrist, the 19 hand bones, Leg_1/2, Ankle, Toe) with Link's local frames (child joint on +x and so on). Exceptions found by comparing rest rotations with Link's: the upper arms (Link is modelled arms down 45 degrees as a child, 35 as an adult; 77 models arms level, the Gorons 10), child Malon's waist (120 degrees: another frame, compensated in Leg_1), `Npc_Hylia_MM_003`'s legs (90-139), Dampe's and `MU_004`'s bent neck and head (54-88: a pose, not a convention). Bones of the same name outside those joints are NOT the same thing: `Hair_Root` (120 / 180 degrees in 23 models), the `Ph_` cloth, sleeve, skirt, collar and hair bones (90-180), child Link's eyelid bones against everybody's (71-110), face bone positions (child Link against Saria: median 9 mm, eyelids 48 mm).
- NPC skeletons lack what the player's data and code name: `Head_2`, `Pod_A`, `Weapon_Root`, mostly `Pod_B`, sometimes `Ear_*` / `Weapon_L/R`, and Link's own cloth / helper bones. Names used: `EquipmentUserParam` (Weapon_R, Weapon_L, Pod_A, Pod_B, Ph_Bangle, Spine_2; its material group lists name materials Link does not have, e.g. the typo `Mt_Earrinig`, so missing materials are tolerated), the player's `.asb` (Skl_Root, Spine_1/2, Head, Head_2, Neck, Face_Root, Mouth_Root, Waist, Leg_1_L, Arm_1_L, Weapon_L, Pod_A, Eyeball_L, the partials Lower / Upper / Face), the Phive files in the actor pack (cloth, `HelperBoneParam` with 119 bone names, foot / hand / look IK, eye bone kinematics), and about 30 bone names as strings in the executable (defaults of parameter structs).
- Animations: `Model/<project>.anim.bfres` (skeletal animations FSKA 0x50 each at header +0x58, count +0xe2; bone animations 0x38: name, curves, base values, flags with base S/R/T bits 3-5 and curve bits 6-15). They are stored unbound (bind index 0xFFFF for every bone, no bind skeleton) and bind by bone name at run time; packs are shared between models with different skeletons (the Kokiri girls use Saria's set with 151 of 249 bones in common). A bone animation writes absolute local scale, rotation AND translation: every bone animation of Link's packs (child 1,843 animations / 277 bones, adult 3,102 / 295) has base values for all three, so a bone of the same name takes Link's offsets. Curves on translation: Skl_Root (hips), the face bones (half of the animations), Pod_A, Weapon_Root, a few helper bones; the body joints' offsets are constant apart from Leg_1 / Waist / Toe in a handful.
- The engine can retarget: `AnimationParam.AnimationResources[i]` has `IsRetarget`, `RetargetModel` (default "Skeleton" = the file `Model/<animation project>.Skeleton.bfres`, a model with only the skeleton of the set's main character; 55 exist, e.g. `Player_Child_Demo_Animation.Skeleton` = Link's skeleton) and `IgnoreRetargetRate` (struct 0x50: project +0x38, RetargetModel +0x40, IgnoreRetargetRate +0x48, IsRetarget +0x49; read at 0x83b18fa8), and a component `RetargetScale` (per animation name a Scale vector). 15 AnimationParams use it: child Epona on the adult's set (bone lengths 0.32-0.76), Nabooru on the generic Gerudo set, Biggoron (15x), Medigoron (4.5x), Darunia's son (0.6x), the big Stalchild (1.6x) and Leever (1.44x), Ingo. In all of them the rest rotations are equal (Nabooru's arms differ by the 45 degrees of the modelling pose, and she is animated right), so it deals with lengths, not frames. The player does not use it; our rig does the same job in the model file instead (below), which leaves the actor parameters alone.
- What `tools\npcrig.py` makes of an NPC skeleton (per Link age, verified per file by `check_rig`): (1) the joints stay bound: each takes Link's rest rotation as its own (the frame turns, the inverse bind matrix follows, the mesh stays), so Link at rest = NPC at rest and Link's turn of a joint is the NPC's; the upper arms keep their own frame (so they hang as Link's do), and so does a joint a rigid shape hangs on. Each joint gets a static parent `Fx_<name>` holding (NPC offset - Link offset), which the animation's own offset completes to the NPC's: limb lengths, shoulder and hip width and standing height stay the NPC's. (2) every other bone that shares a name with a Link bone becomes `Nx_<name>` and keeps its rest pose (face, hair, cloth static). (3) every Link bone still missing is added without weights under Link's parent with Link's values (attach points, face, cloth and helper bones). World rest positions of all NPC bones are unchanged. Hips: Link's hip height + a constant, not scaled by leg length (a taller character floats in crouches and rolls; scaling needs scaled bones around Skl_Root and is not tried).
- Helper bones (`Phive/HelperBoneParam/<model>.phive__HelperBoneParam.bgyml` in the actor pack; 150 files, Link's has 119 bone names, 33 drivers, 73 driven bones): the game moves the `Ph_` assist bones itself, after the animation. A driver is a joint read against its rest pose (`BaseBoneNameId`, `BaseRotate` quaternion, `BaseTranslate`, aim and up axis) and split into three angles (`DriverAttribute` 1843776221 = roll about the aim axis; 652179061 and 1920147722 = the two bends); a driven bone gets `BaseTranslate` / `BaseRotate` plus, per channel (`Roll`, `BendH`, `BendV`, `TranslateX/Y/Z`, `ScaleX/Y/Z`), a static value or curves (`Hermit2DSplit`) of driver angles. The base bone is NOT always the skeleton parent (165 driven bones in the stock files, e.g. `Ph_Hip_L`: parent `Leg_1_L`, base `Waist`, and its `BaseTranslate` is the offset from the waist), so a driven bone is placed relative to its base bone: a static bone put between the two in the model file would be ignored. Bones are found by name, so on our characters Link's parameters drive Link's helper bones (all present, without weights) and nothing drives the NPC's own (renamed `Nx_`).
- The ankles (2026-11-11; snaildev: "disjointed ankles" on most characters, not on Link): Link's foot yaw is not in the shin. `Ph_Ankle_Assist_L/R` (child of `Leg_2`, just above the ankle) takes the ankle's roll one to one, and the lower shin / boot shaft is skinned to it. All 98 humanoid NPC models have the same bone under the same parent, skinned, and their own parameters drive it the same way (Saria: 0.83 of the ankle's sideways bend). In the first rig that bone was static, so the whole foot yaw sat on the ankle seam. Fix in `tools\npcrig.py` (step 4, `FOLLOW`): Link's helper bone is added before it and the NPC's bone hangs under Link's (same rest place, same weights, same bone count). The turn is then about Link's axis, which runs from the NPC's knee in Link's shin direction and misses the NPC's own bone by a little: as child Link 0.1 mm for Saria, about 10 mm for a typical adult, at most 0.045 of the shin length; as adult Link 0.09 of the shin length for everybody (17-42 mm; adult Link's own legs are splayed 5 degrees). A 30 degree foot yaw shifts the shaft against the foot by half that distance. Two models stay static (`FOLLOW_MAX_SLOPE`): the sitting `Npc_Hylia_MM_003` and Nabooru (legs 15 degrees off Link's). Checked per file by `check_rig` (a roll written into Link's helper turns exactly the NPC's bone and its children, nothing else moves). In game (snaildev, 2026-11-11): no visible change on Saria or a tall character, nothing else changed either; so this is not what shows as the disjointed ankle (the step stays in: correct on paper, harmless in game, but whether the game really drives Link's helper on our models is not confirmed). Next suspect: the foot IK (`Phive/FootIKParam/Player*`: two-bone solver with `IKBone` = `Ankle_L/R`, the other two bones are not named, so presumably the ankle's parent and grandparent). In our rig those are `Fx_Ankle` and `Leg_2`, not the knee and the hip: the solver would then bend a 2 cm stub below the knee, put the foot on its target and leave the shin behind. snaildev's look (2026-11-11) fits: fine on the Characters page (no foot IK there), a little off standing, off while walking, very off on slopes, stairs and with one foot on a ledge; foot and knee in the right place, the lower leg stretched over to the foot and its angle at the knee backwards. Experiment built for it (`npcrig` step 5, `scaled_legs`; `npc_characters.SCALED_LEGS` = Saria and Zelda only, the others keep the first rig for comparison): `Leg_1 -> Leg_2 -> Ankle` are direct parents with Link's own offsets, and one static bone above the hip (`Sx_Leg_1_L/R`, uniform scale, `Leg_1`'s segment-scale-compensate flag cleared) gives the leg its size. The ankle stays exactly where it is; hip and knee pivots move by what one scale cannot fit (as child Link: Saria 2 mm, Zelda 11 / 16 mm, median 13 mm; as adult Link 4-8 cm, because the adult's thigh to shin ratio and leg splay differ). Offline: the mesh does not move at rest or in Link's rest pose, and a bend written into the knee turns shin, foot and their helpers as one piece about the knee (`check_rig` 4); it works for 96 of 98 models (Dampe and child-era Ganondorf have a rigid shape on a leg bone, which cannot be scaled). In game (snaildev, 2026-11-11): with Saria and Zelda built this way the whole game world was lit bright cyan; the experiment is switched off again (`SCALED_LEGS` empty, the build is the one before it). snaildev: cyan always, whoever was picked (so it sticks for the session once one of the two has been loaded); he did not get to look at the legs. A scale inside the skeleton is therefore out (`legs_mode="scale"` stays in `npcrig` only as a record). Second experiment (built, waiting for snaildev's look): the engine's own retargeting. Evidence that it does what we need: Biggoron's model has bone offsets 15 times those of `Npc_Goron_GON_Animation.Skeleton` (`Leg_2` 4.1 against 0.273, `Skl_Root` height 12.03 against 0.802, same rotations) and plays the normal Goron animations with nothing but `IsRetarget: true` on that set in his AnimationParam, so with the flag a bone's offset comes from the model, not from the animation. The player's AnimationParam (`Component/AnimationParam/Player_Child...bgyml`, the same file in the packs `Player_Child` and `PouchPlayer_Child`) lists six sets without the flag; only `Player_Child_Demo_Animation` has a `.Skeleton` file (309 bones, exactly Link's). Test folder `Character leg retarget (test)` (`npc_characters.leg_test`, an override folder that sorts before the others): both packs with `IsRetarget` on all six sets, that skeleton file copied under the other five sets' names, and Saria and Zelda as child Link with `legs_mode="retarget"`: no offset bone between hip, knee and ankle, `Leg_2` and `Ankle` carry Link's offsets times a factor (Saria 1.10 / 1.09, Zelda 1.88 / 1.74), the ankle exactly in place, hip and knee pivots 2 mm (Saria) and 1-2 cm (Zelda) off. Every other joint of every character keeps Link's offset in the file, so retargeting should leave it as it is. Works offline for all 98 models. PASSED IN GAME (snaildev, 2026-11-11): lighting normal, Link himself normal, Saria and Zelda fixed on slopes and ledges, Mido (first rig) as before. So with `IsRetarget` the game keeps the model's own bone offsets and leaves bones that carry Link's offsets alone, and the foot IK does take the ankle's parent and grandparent. Folded into the normal build the same day: every character and both ages get the offset-free legs (`npc_characters.LEGS`; `Npc_Hylia_MM_003` sits and keeps the first rig), and the retargeted packs (`Player_Child`, `PouchPlayer_Child`, `Player_Adult`, `PouchPlayer_Adult`; the adult's parameter file is `Player.engine__component__AnimationParam`, which `Player_Base` and Dark Link also carry in their own packs, untouched) and the copied reference skeletons are in the folder `NPC characters`; the test folder is retired. Passed in game (snaildev, 2026-11-11: lighting and Link normal, every character tried fixed on slopes, Nabooru fine although her pivots are 22 cm off, Characters page as before). The adult side is built the same way but, like everything adult, not seen in game. Still on offset bones, so open to the same fault wherever an IK walks a chain: the arms (hand IK: climbing, pushing, ledges), the toes, the spine; hips of tall characters could now also come from the model (`Skl_Root` height) instead of Link's. Open points for a second try: the scale bone's flags had bit 24 set (one of the two "scale is one" hint bits; the game's own scaled bones have both clear), and a scale inside a skeleton may give the renderer something it cannot use (bounding volumes, normals). Other helpers of the same kind that are still static on NPCs: `Ph_Wrist_Assist_A/B` (wrist roll), `Ph_Arm_1_Assist`, `Ph_Clavicle_Assist`, `Ph_Elbow`, `Ph_Knee`, `Ph_Calf`; the same step can take them (`FOLLOW`), the bending ones need a look at the pivot first.
- Files inside an actor pack and their heaps (2026-11-12, from snaildev's running game: he played the story as Saria, went through the Door of Time and the loading screen never ended, the game still drawing). A packed file that has a size table entry of its own gets a heap of that entry + 40 bytes, the file itself is loaded into that heap (the adult's AnimationParam at +0xc0) and what is parsed out of it follows. The stock entries fit to the byte, so a packed file that grows needs its entry raised by the growth. `retarget()` adds `IsRetarget` to every animation set: `Component/AnimationParam/Player...bgyml` (the adult's, in `Player_Adult` and `PouchPlayer_Adult`) grew from 472 to 552 bytes with its entry left at 6280. Of its 8 animation sets (0x50 bytes each) seven were made, the eighth was 24 bytes short of the heap's end (heap 0x18b0 = 6280 + 40), the allocation returned null, the game built the object at address 0 and called through it: a loading thread died ("Cannot execute instruction at unmapped address 0" in the emulator's log, image address 0x801b056c, the loop over the array's `$type` elements in 0x801b03e0-0x801b06c4) and everything else waited for it for ever. This is the adult's first load with the retargeting, with any character, Link included: the adult side had never been loaded with it. The child's file grew from 380 to 444 bytes (6 sets, entry 6008) and happens to fit. Fix in `build.write()` (`packed_growth`): every actor pack written is compared with the stock pack, and a packed file that grew gets its entry raised by the growth rounded up to 32 (adult 6280 -> 6376, child 6008 -> 6072); nothing else in the build changes. On Lichii build 160 the game's code is loaded 0xa4b000 above the addresses of `image.bin` (live 0x80bfb56c = image 0x801b056c); the emulator's log is only on screen and through the debug server's `log_tail` (its log file stays empty while the game runs).
- Faces (2026-11-11). How Link's face moves: by clips, like the body. `Player_Child_01_Animation` has 36 face clips (`Face_Blink`, `Face_Default`, `Face_Talk_*`, `Face_Damage_L`, `Face_Battle`, `Face_Eye_Random`, `EyeScroll_Front` / `EyeScroll_Around` ...), and about half of the body clips in every set carry face tracks too: rotation and translation curves (a few scale curves on the lips) on every bone under `Face_Root`: lids, brows, cheeks, lips, smile lines, chin, nose, teeth, tongue, eyeballs (rotation only). `Face_Root` and `Mouth_Root` never have curves, `Head_2` in 2 clips, the ears in about 30. The eyes also get a controller (`Phive/EyeBoneKinematicsControllerParam`: units `Eyeball_Root_L/R`, base bone `Head` with a position offset, the two `EyeScroll` clips as its range) and the look IK lowers `Ph_Eyelid_Up_L/R` (`Player_LookIK_Eyelid` bone group). The NPCs have the same face bones under the same names (Saria 66, Zelda 66, Mido 64 of Link's; all but one of the 98 models have a set) in adult Link's convention, within about a centimetre of child Link's (Mido's eyes 23 mm); child Link's lid bones are turned 71-79 degrees against everybody else's, and the NPCs hang everything straight under `Face_Root` under `Head` where Link has `Head_2` and `Mouth_Root` between. In the first rig all of that was renamed `Nx_` and static, so the face never moved. The experiment (`tools\npcrig.py` step 6, `face=True`): the face bones are bound like the body joints (Link's rest rotation as the frame, an `Fx_` offset bone for the place, Link's `Head_2` / `Mouth_Root` added first and the NPC's bones hung under them), so a turn or shift a clip writes is, seen from the head, the same on the NPC. Eyeballs carry rigid shapes and keep their frame; they are bound only if that frame is Link's within 3 degrees (Ingo's two models are not: static eyes). `Npc_Hylia_MU_005` has no face bones, Phantom Ganon 28. Checked per file by `check_rig` (check 5: Link's rest pose leaves the face as modelled; a turn and shift written into a face bone moves it and what hangs on it as one piece, nothing else). A face-bound model has 9 bones fewer than the static one (the NPC's bones take the names instead of 66 added copies). What it cannot know offline: how far a lid must turn to close on another face, whether the eye controller places the eyeballs by its own offset, and whether Link's expressions suit another face. Passed in game as the test folder `Character faces (test)` with Saria, Mido and adult Zelda as child Link (snaildev, 2026-11-11: "everything is as expected", they have facial animations). Folded into the normal build the same day for every character and both ages (`npc_characters.FACE`; 194 model files change, the test folder is retired); passed in game on the full set (snaildev, 2026-11-11: lighting and Link normal, the three still good, a mix of other characters all good, Ingo with still eyes "looks fine", legs on slopes still fixed). The adult side is not seen in game yet.
- Dresses (2026-11-11; experiment, built, not seen in game yet). What snaildev sees on young Zelda as child Link: fine standing, wrong as soon as the sword is out. Link's stance is wide with one foot forward and bent knees, and the dress is not wide enough for both lower legs; her legs are only modelled from just above the knee, so in a lunge a lower leg floats in front of the dress; and the dress stays stiff with the upper body, which leans forward, so its lower part points backwards. What the data says: skirts are cloth simulations. Link's tunic skirt is `SimulationMesh_Skirt` in `Phive/ClothParam/Player_Child` (cloth file `Work/Phive/Cloth/Player_Child.phcl`, named in the ControllerSetParam's `ClothList`; 55 collidables, among them capsules on `Leg_1` and `Leg_2`); it moves twelve columns of four bones, `Ph_Skirt_1..4_<column>` (columns `Front`, `Front_A_L/R`, `Front_B_L/R`, `L`, `R`, `Back`, `Back_A_L/R`, `Back_B_L/R`; Link's third bone is called `Ph_Skirt_3_<column>_1`), under `Waist -> Ph_WaistBelt_1`; the bone group `Player_Skirt_Dm` is the twelve top bones. Young Zelda's dress is built the same way: the same twelve columns under the same names, seven bones each from the waist to the floor, her own cloth file (`Npc_Hylia_WU_Zelda_Child.phcl`, `SimulationMesh_Skirt` with base bone `Waist`, preset satin, collidables on her legs), and her HelperBoneParam also drives the twelve top bones from the legs (`Ph_Hip_L/R`, `Ph_Leg_1_Assist_L/R`). The player loads the player's cloth, not hers, and the first rig renamed her skirt bones `Nx_` or left them without a driver: a stiff shell on the waist. As child Link her columns sit close to his: bone 3 within 14 mm, bone 1 96 mm higher (her waistline), bone 4 67 mm lower. The experiment (`tools\npcrig.py` step 7, `skirt=True`, `skirt_map`): each dress bone hangs under Link's simulated bone of the same place in the column (Link's bones added first, without weights, as in the ankle step); bones 5 to 7 stay a chain under her bone 4. So the upper dress should move as Link's tunic skirt does (hang by gravity, pushed out by the legs) and the lower dress goes on rigidly from the tunic's hem. Checked per file by `check_rig` (check 6: a move of Link's bone takes the dress bone and what is below along as one piece, nothing else moves; the rest pose is unchanged). Not known offline: whether Link's cloth runs at all on a model without his tunic mesh (the ankle step never proved that the game drives Link's physics bones on our models), how wild the lower dress swings on a short tunic's hem, and whether the missing upper legs still show. Test folder `Character dress (test)` (override folder; young Zelda as child Link; `npc_characters.DRESS_TEST_*`). IN GAME (snaildev, 2026-11-11): "Looks great". So Link's cloth does run on our models and moves his (weightless) skirt bones, and bones hung under them follow. What is left, in his words: the legs just barely poke through in some animations, and the feet show through the back while running; the front centre column falls inward as Link's tunic does, which is too much on a long dress (the bottom ring is round except for the front point, far inward; her dress has a crease there anyway, under the hanging banner); the sides stretch with the animation and move in the idle but do not float or wave as Link's hat does (the lower three bones of a column are rigid on the tunic's hem); and her veil (`Ph_Veil_*`, 15 bones on `Head`) is still, where it could wave. All four point at the same thing: her own cloth (`SimulationMesh_Skirt` over all seven bones, `SimulationMesh_Veil`, `_Ribbon`, `_Sleeve`, her own leg collidables). How actors get their cloth: the ControllerSetParam's `ClothList` (one entry `Default` with a `Work/Phive/Cloth/<name>.phcl` path) and `Cloth` (the ClothParam: collidables and per-mesh settings); 826 files have a list, no actor uses two different cloths (Talon lists his twice), 167 cloth files exist. `Player_Child.phcl` is used by `Player_Child` and `PouchPlayer_Child`; the packs of `Player_Child`, `Player_Base`, `PouchPlayer_Adult`, Dark Link and `Dm_Player_Child_Op` also carry a parameter file that names `Player_Adult.phcl`. The executable builds the file name with the format `.%s.bphcl` (string at image offset 0x3d151fa), so a per-character cloth file is a question of a hook like the model path's, plus her mesh names in the player's ClothParam. Second experiment (snaildev chose "her own cloth"; built, not seen in game yet): first without any hook, for everybody. Test folder `Character cloth (test)` (sorts before the dress test; `npc_characters.cloth_test`, `CLOTH_TEST_*`): her cloth file under the player's name (`Phive/Cloth/Player_Child.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.bphcl`, not compressed, magic `Phive`), her ClothParam (4 meshes: Veil, Ribbon, Skirt, Sleeve; 26 collidables) in place of Link's in the packs `Player_Child` and `PouchPlayer_Child` (built on the retargeted packs), and their ControllerSetParam's `ClothReaction` / `ClothAdvandecOption` lists set to her four meshes (her veil's reaction file `Mant` is not in the player's packs, so the veil gets Link's hat's, `Linl_Hat`). What stands in the way of simply keeping her bone names: Link's animations have curves on `Ph_Skirt_1_*` and the sleeve bones in 451 of 487 clips of the main set, and his HelperBoneParam drives the twelve skirt tops and `Ph_Sleeve_*` too, all by name; her cloth file names 145 of her bones, 73 of them names Link also has. So the model is rigged another way (`npcrig` step 8, `cloth=`): the 19 joints among them keep their names (body joints are turned by at most 1.5 degrees against her own frames, the upper arms not at all, so her collidables sit right); every other cloth bone whose name starts with a word Link uses gets that word's last letter replaced by `z` (`Ph_Skirz_*`, `Ph_Sleevz_*`: 102 bones; same length, same sort order) and the same bytes are replaced in the cloth file and in the ClothParam's base bone; the twelve skirt tops still hang under Link's (animated, helper-driven) tops as in step 7, the rest of each column is her own chain again. While the folder is on, every character has her cloth, so the folder also ships her model as the plain Link (stored number 0); a model without her cloth bones (any other character) with this cloth is untested and may crash. Not known offline: whether the cloth finds bones by these names (a hash somewhere would not match), whether a cloth made for an NPC actor runs on the player, how the sleeves behave on static tops. Result (snaildev, 2026-11-11): "those cloth physics are working great"; so a cloth made for an NPC actor runs on the player, the renamed bones are found by name, and the only fault was the test's own (Link was her). Third version of the folder (built, not seen in game yet): her cloth for her alone. How a parameter file reference becomes a file: two routines of the same build, 0x803fbc80 (136 callers) and 0x8071f0a0 (reached through a pointer), take (out string, pointer to the path's address); a path starting with `?` is already a file path; otherwise they first ask 0x8071fa60, which knows the physics file types that are named another way (`Work/Phive/Cloth/X.phcl` -> `Phive/Cloth/X.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.bphcl`, the `.%s.bphcl` format) and says no to all others, then read the path again, drop `Work/` and turn the extension into `.b<extension>` (emulated offline on our names with the game's own code). The player's cloth is named in one place, the ControllerSetParam file its PhysicsParam points at (`ControllerSetPath`): `Cloth` (the ClothParam file), `ClothList` (the cloth file) and the reaction lists are all in it. So the folder ships, beside Link's files and under names of their own (`<pack>_C<two letters A-P>`, the stored number's two hex digits; young Zelda, stored number 4, is `Player_Child_CAE` / `PouchPlayer_Child_CAE`): a ControllerSetParam file and her ClothParam file added to the packs `Player_Child` and `PouchPlayer_Child` (`npc_characters.sarc_add`; files inside a pack have size table entries of their own, so the new ones get entries too), her cloth file as `Phive/Cloth/Player_Child_CAE.Nin_Ounce_NVN2.bphcl`, her model with the cloth rig as her own model file only, and a code patch (`npc_characters.cloth_hook`): both routines start with a jump to a hook that, for the reference `Work/Phive/ControllerSetParam/[Pouch]Player_Child.` ... while the stored character has its bit in a small table, calls the routine with the reference to the `_C..` file instead (built on its own stack; the game's string is not written to). Everything else, Link and every character without a cloth of its own, passes through unchanged. The hook's 446 bytes sit in the one-shot body of the sequence node class `SeqOneShotLuaScript` (0x832f7090-0x832f725c; no graph, pack or parameter file of the game names it, its body is referenced by its vtable alone; checked at every build, as the field swap and the voices do with other classes; keep other patches off it and off the first instruction of the two routines). First install (snaildev, 2026-11-11): the game hung while starting ("Almost ready..."). Found offline: `sarc_add` padded the archive's head to 4, so in the rebuilt `PouchPlayer_Child` pack every file started 4 bytes off a multiple of 8 (the game's packs start every file on a multiple of 8); fixed and checked at every build. Second install: still hung; the emulator's log (`%APPDATA%\eden\log\eden_log.txt`, worth reading first after any hang) said "Cannot execute instruction at unmapped address 0x8071f0a4": the hook formed the address to go on at as an absolute number, and the game is not loaded at `image.bin`'s addresses. Every address in a patch must be relative (b, bl, adr, adrp); the offline emulation cannot see this, since it runs at the image's addresses. Third install (snaildev, 2026-11-11): the game starts, Link and Saria as before, but her dress is stiff on the page, in the field and after save + restart with her stored. A read of the running game's memory (her in the field) showed why: none of the `_CAE` settings names and none of her settings' own strings were in memory, while Link's ControllerSetParam reference was there in both forms (`Work/...gyml` and the converted `Phive/...bgyml`) and his cloth file's converted name five times. So the settings file is found once, before a save's character is known or by another route, and kept; redirecting the settings reference does nothing. Fourth version (passed in game, snaildev, 2026-11-11: "she comes back as normal. this should be good for now"; he gave no per-step answers, so read as: no fault seen on Link, Saria or her, her cloth also after a restart): ONE set of settings for both, and the switch where the cloth file is named. A ClothParam is settings by name only (meshes: base bone and coefficients; collidables: name and a flag; the shapes are in the cloth file), so Link's ClothParam gets her four meshes after his twenty and her ten collidables he lacks (the sixteen both have are identical entries), and his two ControllerSetParam files get her reaction and option entries; her two mesh names he also has are changed like the bone names (`SimulationMesh_Skirz`, `SimulationMesh_Sleevz`, also in her cloth file). The hook (`npc_characters.cloth_hook`, 397 bytes, same unused node body) now sits on 0x8071fa60 alone and turns the reference `Work/Phive/Cloth/Player_Child.phcl` into `Work/Phive/Cloth/Player_Child_CAE.phcl` while her number is stored. So both unknowns came out well: the cloth file is asked for when a character is created, and settings entries for meshes the loaded cloth file lacks do no harm. They were: settings entries for meshes the loaded cloth file lacks (the game's own data has that only in reaction lists: two Gerudo actors; never in a ClothParam, of 399 looked at), and whether the cloth file is asked for at each creation. Files inside a pack have size table entries that follow their content closely (about 1000 per mesh), so the changed files get larger entries (`build.PACKED_FILES` tells `write_rstb` that such a name is not a new file). Offline: 57 emulated runs of the hook.
- Cloth for every character (2026-11-13; snaildev: cloth for every character that has the bones, and hair, "since it probably works in a similar way"; PASSED IN GAME, snaildev, 2026-11-13: "Tested with both child and adult. Looking good for hair and cloth physics."). Hair does work the same way: an actor has ONE cloth file, and its skirt, sleeves, hair, beard, earrings, ribbons and other trinkets are meshes of it. 96 of the 98 humanoid NPC models have one (not `Npc_Hylia_MN_004` and `Npc_Hylia_MX_003`), with 600 meshes between them (1 to 16 each; named `SimulationMesh_<part>`, on a few models `SimulMesh_` or `Sim_`), and as players all of it was stiff. A model is used by several actors (cutscene actors `Dm_...`, shop and "through ground" variants); they name the same cloth file and ClothParam and differ at most in the reaction list (5 characters); adult Zelda and adult Ruto have a second cloth file under another name. `npc_characters.cloth_source` takes the cloth file named as the model and, of the settings that name it, those of the same name, else the first. What a cloth file is (`npc_characters.cloth_strings`): a container `Phive` (five numbers at 0x0C: where the Havok tag file starts, where a parameter archive starts, the file's end, the two parts' sizes), the Havok tag file (`TAG0`: `SDKV` 20250200, `DATA`, `TYPE`, `INDX` = `ITEM` + `PTCH`; an `ITEM` entry is 12 bytes: kind and type, offset in `DATA`, count; a string is an item of characters that ends with a zero), and after it a parameter archive (`AAMP`, version 2) that lists the meshes once more (name, base bone, axis order `xyz`, twist axis, preset: what the ClothParam file also says), its strings in one section at its end, each on a multiple of 4. A mesh's name stands three times: as a string of the tag file, at the end of a string `cloth_skeleton_<mesh>`, and in the archive. A search for names cannot tell where a string starts: the archive's first string follows a number directly (Mido's: 180.0, whose last byte reads as the letter C), so names are changed through the file's own tables. No table in the file is sorted by name and nothing in it is a hash of a name (a file with changed names passed in game). The settings: a ClothParam is `HktPath` (a source file's name), `MeshList` (per mesh `Name`, `BaseBone`, `Preset`, `ParamCoefDict`, bone correction and twist) and `CollidableList` (`Name` and `ForReplace`, which no NPC sets; no shape name stands for two different entries anywhere among the NPCs and the two Links); the game's 171 files have at most 38 meshes and 64 shapes. Its size table entry is 4653 + 1.9 a byte + 289 a mesh + 31 a shape (least squares over 170 files, at most 784 off). The ControllerSetParam has `ClothReaction` (mesh name and a `Phive/ClothReaction` file; 25 such files in the game's actor packs, each the same bytes in every pack that has it; `String` is named by Link's own settings and by many NPCs' and is in no actor pack, so a reference to a missing file is tolerated) and `ClothAdvandecOption` (a file only for three meshes of adult Ruto and for the menu Link's hood). Per actor, not per mesh, and therefore NOT carried over: `ClothReactionDefault` and `ClothGroundType` (adult Zelda has one). Who names the players' cloth files (every actor pack looked at): `Player_Child.phcl`: the settings `Player_Child` and `PouchPlayer_Child`; `Player_Adult.phcl`: `Player` (the adult's settings, also the `$parent` of the child's, so it is in the packs `Player_Adult`, `Player_Child`, `Player_Base`, Dark Link's and `Dm_Player_Child_Op`), `PouchPlayer_Adult`, and `Enemy_MiddleBoss_DarkLink`: Dark Link's own settings for his own model `Player_Adult_Dark`. `Player_Base` is the parent ActorParam of both players (in both their packs), not an actor that is created. `Player_Child_Op.phcl` is the opening cutscene's Link. What the build does (`npc_characters.cloth_all`; the folder `Character cloth (test)` is still an override folder: switched off, everything is the build without it): for all 96 and both ages what it did for young Zelda as child Link. Per character and age: the model rigged for its cloth (in place of `NPC characters`' model) and its cloth file under a name of its own (`Phive/Cloth/Player_<age>_C<two letters>`; 12.5 MB an age). The rig (`tools\npcrig.py` step 8) needed four things beyond her: a cloth bone with a name of Link's but no shared word gets one letter replaced (`Hair_Root`, the base bone of most hair, which Link also has: `Haiz_Root`; `Weapon_R`); the ankle helper that the ankle fix hangs under Link's is named by the Ganondorfs' and Phantom Ganon's cloth files (their hems) and keeps hanging there under its cloth name; adult Zelda's skirt hangs from `Ph_Belt` on `Spine_1`, before the waist, and stays there instead of under Link's skirt tops; and the joints. A cloth file's collision shapes and pinned points ride on the bones it names, and the rig turns a joint's frame to Link's (as child Link at most 1.5 degrees for most; as adult Link the clavicles by 10 and the legs by 5; Dampe's `Spine_2` by 34, `Npc_Hylia_MU_004`'s neck by 58, the Gerudos' ears by 14) and the leg fix moves hip and knee pivots (Nabooru's by 22 cm). Beyond 2 degrees or 3 cm a static bone with the NPC's own frame and place hangs under the joint and the cloth file names that bone (`Lez_1_L`, `Claviclz_L`, `Spinz_2`; 75 such bones as child Link, 498 as adult Link); below the limits the joint is used as it is, which is her as child Link as she passed. The settings are one set for everybody in Link's files, as before: Link's 20 meshes (adult 22) + 464, his 41 collision shapes (64) + 157 (151), and 440 reactions and options in each of the four ControllerSetParam files (`Player_Child`, `PouchPlayer_Child`, `Player`, `PouchPlayer_Adult`; the pack `Player_Child` carries the adult's two files too and gets the same). A mesh keeps its name where nothing else stands under it or the same settings do (136 of the 600 share an entry), else its last letter is changed in the cloth file and the settings (305; `SimulationMesh_Skirt` alone has 32 different settings); a base bone follows the bone's name. The ClothParam grows from 6 to 91 KB and its size table entry from 23096 to 707160 (8 for every byte it grows by, + 4096; the game's rule would give about 323000). The reaction and option files the player's packs lack come from the NPCs' packs (`Cloth_Light`, `Lantern`, `Link_Hair_3`, `Mant`, `Muffler`, `Muffler_Heavy`, `Zelda_Adult_Skirt_Escaping`, Ruto's option file, and `HairLight`, `Link_Hair_1`, `Link_Hair_2`, `Earrings` where a pack lacks them); young Zelda's veil now has its own `Mant` (it had Link's hat's). The hook (same place, 413 of 460 bytes) knows both players' references (`Work/Phive/Cloth/Player_` + five characters + `.phcl`; no other cloth file of the game is named so) and has one bit a character. Dark Link would have got the stored character's cloth on Link's bones (the hook cannot tell who asks), so the folder also ships his pack with his settings naming `Player_Adult_Dark.phcl`, a copy of Link's adult cloth file; nobody has fought him with it. The folder is 270 MB in development (192 models that replace `NPC characters`' own in a merged package, 25 MB of cloth files, five packs). A full build with every model cached takes 41 s on this PC (about 28 s before); the first build after a change to the rig makes 196 + 192 models, about 14 minutes (7 before). Offline (2026-11-13): `NPC characters` is byte for byte the installed folder, young Zelda's model and cloth file as child Link are byte for byte the ones that passed, and a read-back of the folder from disk that does not use the build's tables finds for every character and age: the cloth file differs from the NPC's own in names only, every bone it names sits in the shipped model where the NPC's bone sat (2.6 cm and 1.5 degrees at most, exact for every bone under a new name), every mesh has the NPC's settings under the name the file has, in every settings file, and every reaction file is in the pack. What the game answered (his run): one ClothParam with 484 meshes is taken, the bones that stand in for joints work, the adult side works (the hook on the adult's reference, the adult's settings). Still unseen: Dark Link, and nobody named a cloth file larger than Link's on the player.
