Before his run this read: NOT known until seen in game: whether one ClothParam with 484 meshes is taken (the game's largest has 38; a ClothParam entry for a mesh the loaded cloth file lacks passed with 24), the bones that stand in for joints (a cloth file naming a leaf bone under the joint it meant), the whole adult side (the hook on the adult's reference, the adult's settings), a cloth file larger than Link's on the player (adult Zelda's as child Link), and Dark Link. If the game does not start or a character crashes it: the emulator's log first, then `cloth_all` on fewer characters. `tools\byml.py` does not read node type 0x20, which some ControllerSetParam files have; `nxfmt.Byml` passes over it (enough for reading names).
- Dresses and the legs (2026-11-13; SOLVED FOR YOUNG ZELDA AS CHILD LINK on 2026-11-13, see "MILESTONE" at the end; what stands before it is the way there, with three ideas that were wrong. snaildev, after the cloth passed: "the legs poking through while running for the female characters. Their dress is still mainly aligned with their upper body, but also not wide enough to cover the wide stance of having a sword drawn. Running is somewhat close, but the dress isn't quite big enough, the knees mainly come through at the front"; and "for young zelda, when you select her, her right leg ends up with the cloth flung around it so after she's selected, our preview shows her with part of her leg sticking thru the dress". What a mesh's settings can say (all 171 ClothParam files): `Preset` (burlap 471, hair 175, satin 165, old_leather 133, leather 68, silk 55, velvet 17 and a few special ones; no file named for them was found in the game's data) and `ParamCoefDict`, numbers over the preset: `localRange_maximumDistance` (how far the cloth may leave the shape the bones give it; 0 to 500, median 10), `localRange_stiffness`, `simulation_particleMass`, `simulation_particleRadius` (how far it keeps from the collision shapes; 0 to 100, median 3), `standardLink_stiffness`, and rarely `bendStiffness_bendStiffness`, `simulation_particleFriction`, `standardLink_allowedCompression` / `allowedStretching` / `ignoreHiddenEdges`, `stretchLink_stiffness`, `volume_stiffness`. Room and distance kept of some skirts: Link's tunic 6.5 and 2.5 (adult 3), Saria 5 and 2, young Zelda 15 and 12, adult Malon 15 and 8, child Malon 31 and 12.1, a townswoman (`WF_001`) 25 and 10, adult Zelda 30 and 8. Adult Zelda has a SECOND skirt mesh for running, `SimulationMesh_Skirt_Run` (45 and 10, preset silk where the other is burlap), switched by a node of her own in her AI (`ExecuteNpcHyliaZeldaAdultControlSkirtEnableCloth`), and a reaction file `Zelda_Adult_Skirt_Escaping`: the game's makers needed more room for a dress that runs. A reaction file is wind and water response (`WindReactionParam`, `EnableWaterReaction`); an option file (`ClothAdvancedOption`) has `DampingOverride` (the menu Link's hood: 0.9999), `GravityFactorOffset`, `TimeFactorOffset` (adult Ruto: -0.3). The game's own means against cloth that is flung by a sudden move: nodes for an actor's AI graph, `OneShotPhysicsRequestClothWarp` (331 graphs; a module `NpcLongHair.ExecuteRequestClothWarp` in the global AI pack), `OneShotClothControllerReset`, `OneShotClothFreeze` / `Unfreeze`, `ExecuteClothSetEnableCollidable` (King Zora), `ExecuteClothOverrideGravity`; `ClothWarpMode` (default `Auto`) on the event nodes that play an animation (474 event flows); `EnableClothWarpWhenSpAction` in the NpcSpecialParam of both Malons and adult Zelda. There is no sequence node for it. In the code (image addresses): an AI node class has the sequence node classes' layout (name at vtable + 0x98, one-shot body at + 0x138); an actor's physics is `0x809944c0(actor)` = [[actor + 0x270] + 0x50] + 0x20, its cloth part [physics + 0x1c8], the warp request the byte at cloth part + 0x1f2 (bit 7 asked, bits 0-5 one a mode, with bit 6 set a request keeps the modes it has); `0x80993d30(physics)` asks for a warp, `0x80993b60(physics, mode)` with a mode; the cloth controllers are [cloth part + 0xd8] (count at + 0xd0), `0x80994320(physics, i)` the i-th, `0x80b2f860(controller, bool)` resets one, bit 21 of [controller + 0x48] freezes it (the node bodies: warp 0x82924c70, reset 0x828e2290, freeze 0x828e2430, unfreeze 0x828e2910). THE SETTER THE SLIDE USES, `0x80964e70(actor, &position, flag)` (`build.ACTOR_SET_POS`): bit 0 of the flag CLEAR means warp. It passes `1 & ~flag` to `0x804e6630`, which on a warp marks the physics ([physics + 0x228] |= 3), keeps the new transform at physics + 0x1d0 and asks for the cloth warp; with the flag 1 none of that happens and the cloth feels the move. 29 of the game's 32 calls pass 0; 1 is passed by `ExecuteMenuActorRotate` (the call at 0x805917a8, which the slide copied), `ExecuteAIScheduleShowItem` (0x8263e358) and one more (0x826c8a6c): smooth moves that cloth is meant to follow. (The flag also sets or clears bit 0x8000 of [actor + 0x4a8].) The slide brings the new character 1.6 m in a third of a second: a dress stays behind as far as its settings allow, the leg on the leading side passes through it, the collision shape then pushes the cloth out on the far side, and it stays caught around that leg after the slide: young Zelda's right leg. Adult Link's tunic skirt has the child's twelve columns under another name, `Ph_SkirtAdult_1..4_<column>` (the child: `Ph_Skirt_1..4_<column>`; both under `Ph_WaistBelt_1` on `Waist`), so as adult Link no dress top found a bone of Link's to hang from (npcrig step 8 looked for the child's name): 44 characters have such tops, and as adult Link their dresses hung from the waist alone. The three experiments, an override folder each that sorts before the cloth folder (`npc_characters.CLOTH_FIT_MOD`, `CLOTH_SKIRT_MOD`, `CLOTH_WARP_MOD`), so each can be switched off alone: (1) `Character cloth (fit test)`: the cloth folder's four packs with more room in the settings of the skirt and dress meshes of the Hylian women and the Kokiri girls: room times 3 but at most 60 and never less than the NPC's, distance kept times 1.5 (young Zelda 15 -> 45 and 12 -> 18, adult Zelda 30 -> 60 and 8 -> 12, her running mesh 45 -> 60 and 10 -> 15, child Malon 31 -> 60 and 12.1 -> 18.15, adult Malon 15 -> 45 and 8 -> 12); the files keep their sizes, nothing else differs. (2) `Character cloth (adult skirt test)`: the adult models of those 44 characters with the dress tops under adult Link's tops of the same column (`npcrig.player_rig(tops=)`); bones, names, cloth files and settings are the cloth folder's. (3) `Character cloth (slide test)`: a code patch alone: in the setter (the instruction at 0x80964e98), a call with flag bit 0 set for an actor that has a cloth part, while `CharacterMenu_Slide` is not 0, sets the warp request as the game does on a warp; outside a slide and for the game's own callers nothing changes. Its 188 bytes are in the one-shot body of the unused sequence node class `SeqOneShotSetLODJudgeLayer` and the padding after it (0x83304c20-0x83304cf0; keep other patches off it and off 0x80964e98; from there a conditional branch does not reach the setter, 43 MB away, so the hook leaves with plain jumps). Offline: 400 emulated runs of the patched setter. Not known until seen in game: all three; what a warp does to cloth that is asked every frame of a slide (the game asks once after a move); whether more room makes a dress flap too much. Sequence node classes no graph of the game uses, with a one-shot body of their own, for later patches (2026-11-13; 37 found, the larger ones): `SeqOneShotClearLODJudgeLayer` (0x832edb10, 196 bytes), `SeqOneShotUpdateProjectionSetting` (0x83bd84d0, 160), `SeqOneShotUISoundManualCall` (0x83bd7910, 160), `SeqOneShotReleaseDucking` (0x832fa2c0, 156), `SeqOneShotUpdateLayerSetting` (0x83bd81d0, 136), `SeqOneShotSetAutoShowControllerSupport` (0x83bd4810, 132). Taken: `SeqOneShotSetGameDataAppearScarecrowFalseAll` (field swap), `SeqOneShotEnableRestoreBG` (voices), `SeqOneShotSetMessageToLayout` (slide), `SeqOneShotLuaScript` (cloth), `SeqOneShotSetDebugBancParam` (0x809d76a0, 300 bytes: the customization session; this patch sat there for an hour before that was seen: read CLAUDE.md again before taking one), `SeqOneShotSetLODJudgeLayer` (this). FIRST ROUND (snaildev, 2026-11-13, all three folders on). The slide test: "That fixes young zelda's leg in the model view, but the character's cloth animation freezes (and resets) on exit, and the game lags really bad on entry." The fit and the adult skirt: "For adults: feet just barely show through the back as they run, and the knees in front are still broken in roughly the same way. For child zelda: knees still poke through in front a little bit (but it's improved), and when running/walking, you can sometimes just barely see the heel poke through. For the sword stance, the dress still falls behind her front leg, and her back knee still pokes through slightly." Why the warp froze the cloth and lagged, read in the cloth update (0x801666f8 on: `tbnz` bit 7 of the request byte, then its low bits): a request has a MODE. Bit 0 (mode 0) goes on without a reset: the cloth is carried along with the actor (0x80168320). Bits 1 and 2 call 0x80e64e80(controller, 0 / 1) first. Bit 3 (mode 3) resets the controller WITH its settling steps (0x80b2f860(controller, 1); the player's GameClothParam says `IterationIfEventMember: 25`, presumably how many). With no mode bit, which is what the game's setter asks for and what the first cut asked for, the game chooses between mode 0 and mode 3 (0x80de6320(controller)) and marks that it chose (bit 5); for a move like the slide it evidently chose the reset, on every frame, for both characters. The update clears bit 7 when it is done; the helper of the game's node (0x80993b60) clears the old mode bits unless bit 6 is set, sets the mode's bit, then bit 7. SECOND CUT (built and checked offline, waiting for his look). The slide test asks for mode 0 as that helper does (the request byte becomes 0x81): the cloth is carried along, neither reset nor settled, so it should stay alive and cost nothing; 200 bytes, the unused node's body is full; 600 emulated runs. The fit test: why more room alone did little: a dress is moved by twelve columns of bones, about a hand apart at knee height, and a leg's collision shape that passes between two columns pushes neither unless the shape and the distance the cloth keeps together reach them; and a leg that moves further in one frame than shape and distance are thick goes through and has the dress behind it afterwards (the sword stance: the front leg steps through, the dress stays behind it). Every dress character has collision shapes on thighs and shins (young Zelda 12 on the legs and the ankles, adult Zelda 6, adult Malon 8), so the lever is the distance kept: for a long skirt (room 12 or more before: the dresses) it is now times 3.5 and at least 40 (young Zelda 12 -> 42, adult Zelda 8 -> 40 and her running mesh 10 -> 40, child Malon 12.1 -> 42.35, adult Malon 8 -> 40), for a short one times 1.5 as before; the room as in the first cut (`npc_characters.CLOTH_FIT_LONG`). Not known: whether a dress that keeps 4 cm from the legs looks too full. SECOND ROUND (snaildev, 2026-11-13). The slide test with mode 0: the leg is "through again", the slides "smooth and alive": so mode 0 does not free a caught dress, the reset of the first cut did. The fit test: young Zelda walking and running "better"; the sword stance "still behind the leg. A bit better, but the knee still pokes through at the front, and after doing a different animation, the dress can fall behind the leg again. She has no upper leg, so the dress falls behind the lower leg which is like a separate piece. Can we just make that invisible femur bone solid?"; her dress "looks right"; the adults "worse". WHAT A MESH REALLY DOES IS BAKED INTO THE CLOTH FILE, and the ClothParam's `ParamCoefDict` and `Preset` are what it was baked from, not read when the game runs: the game's code has none of those names (`localRange_maximumDistance`, `simulation_particleRadius`, `satin` ...) and none of their hashes (murmur3, crc32); the numbers in the files are exactly the ClothParam's (below); and a distance kept of 42 would be 21 cm from every leg, which no dress showed ("looks right"). So the first two cuts of the fit test changed nothing in the game, and his "improved", "better" and "worse" for them have no cause in what was changed. How to read a cloth file's objects (`npc_characters.ClothTag`): the tag file's `TST1` section is the type names, `TNA1` a count and then a type a record (name, number of template arguments, the arguments in pairs), all in Havok's packed integers (one byte below 0x80; two with 0x80-0xBF first, 14 bits; three with 0xC0-0xDF, 21 bits; four with 0xE0-0xE7, 27 bits; five with 0xE8-0xEF; eight with 0xF0-0xF7); an `ITEM` entry's low 24 bits are its type's number, its top byte 0x10 for one object and 0x20 for an array; a pointer in `DATA` is an item's number. One `hclClothData` a mesh (its name is the mesh's), with one `hclSimClothData` and its operators. In `hclSimClothData`: + 0x40 the particles (`ParticleData`, 16 bytes: mass, 1 / mass, size, friction; a pinned one has mass 0), + 0x88 the constraint sets, + 0xb0 the bone numbers its collision shapes ride on, + 0xc0 their offsets, + 0xd0 the shapes (`hclCollidable`), + 0xe8 a mask a particle of the shapes it collides with. `hclCollidable` (0xB0 bytes): + 0x20 its rest transform (three columns and the place), + 0x98 its shape, + 0xa0 its name, + 0xa8 the pinch radius, then four bytes of which the last is "enabled". `hclCapsuleShape`: + 0x20 start, + 0x30 end (in the bone's frame, along the bone), + 0x50 the radius; `hclSphereShape`: + 0x2c the radius. `hclStandardLinkConstraintSet::Link` (12 bytes): two particles, the rest length, the stiffness, which is the share of a stretch given back in one solver pass DIVIDED BY the sum of the two particles' 1 / mass. `hclStretchLinkConstraintSet::Link` the same shape (the dress's length from its pinned row). `hclLocalRangeConstraintSet::LocalConstraint` (16 bytes) or `::LocalStiffnessConstraint` (20): particle, reference vertex, how far it may leave the place its bones give it, two limits along the normal (not used: +- the largest number), [its stiffness]. `hclSimulateOperator::Config` (48 bytes): name, two arrays, then a byte each: steps a frame, solver passes a step, two flags. Young Zelda's dress by those numbers: 96 particles, twelve columns of eight, the top row pinned; size 6 cm (ClothParam 12: a diameter in cm), smaller near the hem; 252 sideways links that give 0.40 back (ClothParam `standardLink_stiffness` 0.4), 84 length links at 0.3; a particle may leave its place by up to 15 cm at the hem (ClothParam 15), softly (0.09 to 0.23); ONE step and ONE pass a frame, as every cloth of the game; and 13 collision shapes that every particle collides with: a capsule on each thigh (radius 6 cm, 18 cm long, centred on the hip: it reaches from above the hip to just short of the knee, 16.5 cm down), two on each shin (radius 4.5 and 6 cm, knee to ankle) and two spheres there (4.5 cm), the ankles (4.1 cm), the waist (9.6 cm). So her thigh IS solid, and with those sizes no knee could reach the cloth if the cloth held together. The adult women's links give 0.03 to 0.10 back (ClothParam 0.1; adult Zelda's running mesh 0.39), a townswoman's 0.9 to 1.0, Saria's 1.0. What that makes of a dress on Link's animations: twelve columns that hang from the waist and are barely tied to each other; a thigh or a shin pushes the two columns next to it apart and comes out between them, and the dress's drawn surface, which is stretched between the columns' bones, then goes through the leg ("the knees come through at the front"); in a lunge the columns close again behind the shin ("the dress falls behind the lower leg"). THIRD CUT (built and checked offline, waiting for his look). The fit test is now made in the cloth files themselves (`npc_characters.cloth_tune`, `CLOTH_FIT_*`; the folder has the 27 characters' cloth files for both ages, 54 files, in place of the cloth folder's; the ClothParam is left alone): in the skirt and dress meshes of the Hylian women and the Kokiri girls every sideways link gives at least 0.8 back, the solver takes 2 steps a frame and 4 passes a step, and a particle may leave its place 3 times as far, at most 60 cm at the hem (young Zelda 15 -> 44 cm, adult Zelda 30 -> 60, adult Malon 15 -> 45). Nothing else in a file changes (checked byte by byte against the cloth folder's file). Not known: whether a dress that holds together still looks like cloth, and what the steps cost. The slide test asks ONCE: in the camera update whose slide count is 19 (the frame after the new character's way in is done: `in_frames` reaches the whole at frame 18, and the count is raised before the places are set) each of the two characters gets one request with mode 3, the reset with its settling steps; nothing before and nothing after, so the cloth lives through the slide and is put right when the character stands. 188 bytes; 960 emulated runs. Not known: whether one reset is seen as a hitch or a jump of the dress. A read of the running game for where the collision shapes are (the debug server, read only) was cut off: the game was closed for the customization session's install at that moment. Not repeated. THIRD ROUND (snaildev, 2026-11-13). The slide test with one mode 3 request on arrival: "when they enter, the dress freezes for a second, then resets to a correct position": mode 3's settling steps are spread over about a second in which the cloth stands still, then it is right. The fit test's third cut: "The cloth physics are messed up now, and the knee problem isn't fixed. The bottom of the dress flips upward and gets stuck." So firmer links, more passes and more room are not what keeps a knee in, and they spoil the cloth: the folder `Character cloth (fit test)` is RETIRED (`npc_characters.RETIRED`; `cloth_tune` and `ClothTag` stay as tools). Where that leaves the knees: the file says every particle of the dress collides with shapes on thigh and shin that are thicker than the legs, and still the legs come through on the player whatever the cloth's own numbers are. The one thing not looked at is whether those shapes ARE where the player's legs are while he moves: a shape rides on a bone of the cloth file's own skeleton (the bone numbers at `hclSimClothData` + 0xb0), and how the game gives those bones the player's pose (by name from the model? through the cloth skeleton's own parents, which our rig changes with its offset bones?) is not known. If the shapes stay in the rest pose, a dress collides with legs that stand still while the real ones walk out of it: that would fit everything seen (fine standing, knees through in every stride, the dress behind the front leg of a lunge, and no cloth number helping). To be read from the running game (the shapes' transforms while he runs; the scratch script `probe3.py` of this session does it through the debug server, read only), not guessed at again. FOURTH CUT of the slide test (built and checked offline, waiting for his look): the same one request when the new character has arrived, with mode 1 (the request byte 0x82): the game's other reset, 0x80e64e80(controller, 0), which does not ask for the settling steps (mode 2 is the same routine with them; what else it does differently from the plain reset 0x80b2f860 was not read). This session's folders are installed by `tools\models_install.py` since the rule that a session installs only its own folders (CLAUDE.md, "Test rounds"). FOURTH ROUND (snaildev, 2026-11-13). The slide test with mode 1 once on arrival: "no freeze, leg through": mode 1 does not free a caught dress either. Of what a request can ask for, only mode 3 puts the dress right, with its second of stillness; the patch asks for mode 3 again (the third cut) until something better is found (built, not installed; the plain reset without settling steps, 0x80b2f860(controller, 0), has no request of its own, and calling it from the camera update is not known to be safe while cloth is computed in parallel). The first read of the running game for the collision shapes (he held young Zelda in the charged sword stance; `probe3.py`, `probe4.py` in this session's scratch folder) did NOT reach the player's shapes, and the game was then closed for another session's install. What it did show: the running cloth keeps its OWN copies of a file's collision shapes, one set an actor that has the cloth, 0xB0-byte objects side by side in one stretch of memory 0x120 apart (around 0x2162500000 in that run), with their transforms in WORLD coordinates and updated; a copy points at the same shape object as the file's object (+ 0x98) and at a name of its own (+ 0xa0, the pointer's low bit set); the copies read were NPCs' standing near the player (one has `Collidable_Ph_Arm_1_Assist_L`, which her file has not), taken for hers because the names are the same in every file. `probe5.py` finds the stored character's own copies (by the shapes of the file whose Waist rests where hers does) and works back to the bones they ride on. Still to be run: it needs him in the game as young Zelda in a pose that is not the rest pose. THE LIVE READ (2026-11-13 20:50, read only, while snaildev held young Zelda's charged sword stance; and again 50 s later, after he let go). THE COLLISION SHAPES DO FOLLOW THE LEGS, so the idea above (shapes left in the rest pose) is wrong. How they are found: every actor with cloth keeps its own copies of its cloth's collision shapes (0xB0-byte objects 0x120 apart, the transform at +0x20, at +0x98 a pointer at the shape object of the cloth file in memory, which tells whose they are); young Zelda's file was in memory twice and had four sets of copies: two actors (the field player, and the menu's character standing still in its idle pose near the origin), each with one set for the skirt (its 13 shapes) and one for the other meshes. From a copy and the file's rest transform follows where the bone it rides on is; in every set the bones so found have her skeleton's lengths to the millimetre (hip to knee 0.242, knee to ankle 0.223) and thigh and shin agree on the knee within 1 mm. In the charged stance the field player's waist shape was 12 cm lower than standing, the thighs turned 73.5 (left) and 87.4 degrees (right) from rest; in the waist bone's frame (x down the body, y forward, z to her right) the knees were at (0.160, 0.084, -0.262) and (0.062, 0.213, 0.169) against (0.291, 0, -+0.067) at rest, the ankles at (0.366, 0.002, -0.287) and (0.278, 0.224, 0.226): knees 46 cm apart (13 at rest), ankles 57 cm apart, the right thigh level and forward, the left out to the side. After he let go (sword out, standing): thighs 34 to 39 degrees, knees 10 to 12 cm from rest. WHAT THE CLOTH FILE SAYS ABOUT THE DRESS, read the same evening (scratch scripts fit1..fit5, helper2 of this session; `ClothTag` reads all of it): (1) The simulated skirt is not the dress but a TUBE round it: 96 particles, 8 rings of 12, the top ring pinned; in the model's space a straight tube 1.26 to 1.30 m round at every height, from y 0.66 to 0.04 (front z +0.17, back -0.25, sides x +-0.21). The dress itself (her 84 dress bones, seven a column) is a cone inside the tube: 5 to 10 cm from the body's axis at the top, 19 to 30 cm at the hem. The dress bones are set from the tube's triangles (`hclSimpleMeshBoneDeformOperator`, 84 triangle and bone pairs), so the dress moves as the tube moves and stays as far inside it as at rest: 8 to 15 cm at the top ring, 7 to 9 cm at rings 3 and 4 (the height of the hips and thighs), 2 to 5 cm at rings 5 and 6 (knee height standing), 3 to 7 cm at the hem. (2) A particle's size by ring from the top: 0, 3.8, 7.6, then 6.0 cm; the leg shapes are capsules of 6 cm (thigh) and 4.5 to 6 cm, so the tube is kept 12 to 13.6 cm from a leg's axis, and at rest it just clears them. (3) The leash (`hclLocalRangeConstraintSet::LocalStiffnessConstraint`: particle, reference vertex, the distance it may leave by, two limits along the normal that are not used, a stiffness): by ring 0, 0.1, 1.7, 1 to 4, 2 to 7, 5 to 12, 14.5, 14.8 cm, with stiffness 0.23 at the top to 0.09 at the hem (a soft pull, a share a step). So "3 times the room" in the third cut gave the hem 44 cm and the rings at hip height still 0.3 to 5 cm: the flipping hem and the uncured knees both follow. (4) The solver's order (`hclSimulateOperator::Config`, its list of constraint sets with -1 for the collision): leash, standard links, stretch links, COLLISION, transition. The collision has the last word in a step. (5) A particle's place (what the leash pulls it to, and where the pinned ring is) comes from the `Skin` operator (`hclObjectSpaceSkinPOperator`): 104 reference vertices (own packed places, `LocalBlockP`; bind matrices in a space 5.6 cm off the model's in z), each weighted to dress bones of two neighbouring heights (the pinned ring: its top bone 0.73, Waist 0.25), read from the dress bones' ANIMATED pose. (6) And that pose is driven from the legs: `HelperBoneParam` drives the twelve top bones (`Ph_Skirt_1_<column>`) from `Leg_1_L` / `Leg_1_R` read against `Waist`, in young Zelda's own file and in child Link's with nearly the same curves (adult Link: `Ph_SkirtAdult_1_*`, the like): attribute 652179061 is the leg's lift forward and back (the front columns bend up with it: 90 degrees give 80 to 85 on `Front_A`, 48 to 65 on `Front_B`, 60 to 80 at 120 on `Front` from both legs; the back columns from a lift backwards), 1920147722 the lift sideways (the side columns: 100 degrees give 70). The bones below a top hang still under it, so a column is a stick that swings with its top. So by the data the tube's places swing out with the thighs, on the NPC and on the player alike (her tops hang under Link's driven tops in our rig). WHAT IS NOT KNOWN: where the tube really is in the stance (pushed out in front of the legs with the dress too far inside it to cover a raised knee, which would fit "knees through the front"; or behind the shin, which would fit "the dress falls behind her front leg"), and whether the drivers do on our rig what the data says. A loop round both knees in the stance needs 1.17 m (bare legs) where the dress itself is about 0.9 m round, so the dress has to be stretched there whatever is done. NEXT: record it. `probe7.py` in this session's scratch folder (%USERPROFILE%\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\03928156ec2701a5\3de4b75c-1602-4162-8814-359ce78868d3\scratchpad; tested against a made-up memory with `mock7.py`) finds the skirt's particles and its reference vertices in the game's memory by the shape of the pinned ring and records them with the shapes 30 times a second while snaildev stands, holds the stance and runs (it beeps to tell him when). If it proves itself it belongs in `tools`. THE FIRST RECORDING (2026-11-13 21:47; snaildev held the stance and ran at other moments than the cues, which gave a snapshot of the stance and three seconds of running; `probe7_run1.*` in the scratch folder). (a) The simulated particles are in memory as plain arrays, 16 bytes a vector (three floats and a fourth that means nothing), in the particles' order, twice an actor (now and the step before), in world coordinates, a few megabytes from the actor's copies of the collision shapes; the pinned ring keeps its shape to a millimetre in any pose, which is how the recorder finds them. The reference vertices are not kept as such an array. (b) THE DRESS'S TOP BONES ARE NOT DRIVEN ON THE PLAYER: in the charged stance the pinned ring sat at its rest place against the waist bone to 0.6 mm, where the helper bone curves would have turned the front tops by up to 80 degrees. Our rig hangs her tops under Link's driven tops; what the game writes into a driven bone evidently does not reach a bone hung under it (that is also why hanging the NPCs' ankle helpers under Link's showed nothing: `npcrig.FOLLOW`). So on the player the tube's places are a rigid tube under the waist, whatever the legs do. (c) The tube in the stance: the rings from the hips down were 2.5 to 38 cm from their places (their leashes: 1.7 to 14.8 cm) and 1.65 to 1.74 m round (1.28 at rest); the collision holds (no point nearer than 9.4 cm to a leg's axis). Tested as a surface in space (the tube's 8 rings of 12, closed at both ends; points along each thigh and shin; scratch `ref2.py`): the LEFT leg was inside it, 6 to 9 cm from it; the RIGHT (front) leg was outside it from the lower thigh down, by up to 9 cm. That is what snaildev saw: the dress behind the front leg, the back knee just showing (the dress rides 2 to 9 cm inside the tube). (d) The same test on the tube's PLACES: as they are now, every part of both legs is outside them in the stance (by 6 to 15 cm), and while running the knee is outside them 56% of the time (by 7 cm on average), the shin 40% (8 cm, 15 at most). So the leash pulls the cloth to the wrong side of the legs; nothing in the cloth file is wrong for the NPC. THE LEGS TEST (built 2026-11-13, `tools\models_legs_test.py`, folder `Character cloth (legs test)`; checked offline, NOT seen in game when this was written). Dress bones are hung on the leg joints, which the animation does move: by a bone's rest place, from just above the hip joint down to a quarter of a thigh below the knee on the thigh of its side, below that on the shin of its side; the columns in the middle and behind the legs stay as they are. For young Zelda: the second bone of `Front_A`, `Front_B` and the side column on the thigh, the fifth on the shin (12 bone records; the bones below each go along). Every bone keeps its rest place and frame, so the cloth file, the skinning and the file's size are untouched (bone records patched in place: `bfres.Model.set_skeleton` would grow the file and with it the size table). By the same test the places then hold both legs in the stance (3 to 8 cm inside) and while running the thigh is outside 3% of the time, the knee 29% (by 2 cm, 3 at most), the shin 13% (3 cm, 6 at most): within the collision's reach of 12 cm, which pushes the cloth outward from there. Rules tried: without the hip row (thigh outside 14%), back columns on the shins too (shin outside 24%), thighs only (the front shin stays outside). If it passes it is a rule for every dress (the bones of the skirt and dress meshes from the cloth file, by geometry) and belongs in `npcrig` step 8; it needs no helper bone file and no shared file. If the knees still show when they come up to hip height, the next lever is the particles' size on the rings at hip and thigh height (the dress rides 7 to 9 cm inside the tube there). THE LEGS TEST, FIRST LOOK (snaildev, 2026-11-13 about 22:35): the stance "better, still shows", running "better", the dress "looks right"; in his words: on the Characters page her leg still pokes through, slightly better; in the sword crouch the back of the dress looks good, "but the front lower leg still sticks through fully"; running, the knees still stick through. THE SECOND RECORDING (his read of the same round, `probe7_run2.*`: 42 s with the skirt's particles, standing, the stance, running). CLOTH THAT IS ONCE ON THE WRONG SIDE OF A LEG STAYS THERE. Standing still, before and after everything else, her right shin was outside the tube by 8 to 9 cm while every other part of both legs was 5 to 13 cm inside: ring by ring, the wall of her right side (the side and `Front_B` columns from the knee down) lay between her legs, resting against the inside of the right shin's collision shape, 18 to 24 cm from its place on the outer side. Why it stays: the leash is soft (stiffness 0.09 to 0.23: that share of the way back a step, and only of what is beyond the leash's length), the collision has the last word in a step and pushes a point out on the side it is on. How it gets there is not recorded (a leg moving more than the collision's reach of 12 cm in a step: an attack, a warp, the page's slide); for an NPC that stands and walks it never matters. In the stance of that recording the front leg was outside from the lower thigh down (by 3 to 9 cm), as before; while running each leg was outside in about half of the seconds. A REPLAY on recorded moments (scratch `sim1.py`: from the recorded tube, 30 solver steps without motion in the game's order, leash, links, stretch links, collision with the 13 shapes as recorded): with the leash as it is the caught wall stays caught (as in the game); with the leash of the leg-following points FIRM (stiffness 1, and no longer than the place is from that leg's axis less 4 cm) it comes free in one to a few steps and both legs are inside the tube, standing (right shin 10 cm inside), in the stance (3 to 10 cm) and at the running moments tried (2 to 13 cm). SECOND PART OF THE LEGS TEST (built and checked offline, NOT seen in game when this was written): `tools\models_legs_test.py` also ships her cloth file with that firm leash: the 30 points of rings 4 to 8 of the six leg-following columns (places 12.8 to 17.1 cm from their leg's axis): stiffness 0.09 to 0.18 becomes 1, the length 1.3 to 14.7 cm becomes 1.3 to 13.1 (16 shortened); 176 bytes of the installed cloth file change, in place. The skirt's collision shapes, for the record: thigh a capsule of 6.0 cm; shin two capsules (4.5 and 6.0 cm) and two spheres of 4.5 (knee, mid shin); ankle a capsule of 4.1; waist 9.6. What a firm leash may cost: those thirty points can be no further from a place on the thigh or shin than their leash, so the front and sides of the dress move with the legs more than before. MILESTONE (snaildev, 2026-11-13 about 22:55, the legs test with both parts, young Zelda as child Link): the front lower leg in the charged stance "Covered", the knees while running "Gone", her leg on the Characters page after she slides in "Covered", and the dress "Looks right" (not stiff, not stuck to the legs). His read of that round (the third recording): in the stance every part of both legs is 4 to 9 cm inside the tube (before: the front leg up to 9 cm outside). While running the test still counts the knee outside the tube in 42% of the samples (53% before), by 4 cm on average, which he does not see: the shapes and the particles of a sample are read some milliseconds apart and a running leg moves centimetres in that time, so the running numbers are good for comparing, not as a measure. So the two things together are the fix: dress bones that hang on the legs (the places follow the legs), and a firm leash no longer than the place is from the leg for the points that follow (cloth cannot stay on the wrong side of a leg). The page's slide no longer needs a cloth reset for her. FOR EVERY DRESS (surveyed the same night, not built yet): 47 of the 96 characters with a cloth file have a skirt or dress mesh. What the first version of the rule did not know: most skirts hang all their columns on ONE root bone (41 bones: a root and 8 columns of 5), so a column has to be taken as an unbranched chain and not as everything under the top; 24 files keep the leash as `LocalConstraint` (16 bytes an entry, no stiffness of its own: the set's stiffness counts) and not as `LocalStiffnessConstraint`; the game's own designers hang skirt bones on the thighs in some models (adult Ruto, the Kokiri boys: `Leg_1_L` / `Leg_1_R` are parents of skirt bones), which is the same idea. IN THE CLOTH FOLDER FOR EVERY SKIRT AND DRESS (2026-11-13, late; built and checked offline, NOT seen in game beyond young Zelda when this was written). The test tool became the library `tools\clothlegs.py` (`model()`: the dress bones on the legs; `cloth()`: the firm leash), and `npc_characters.cloth_all` uses both for every character with a mesh named Skirt or Dress and both ages (`CLOTH_LEGS`); `tools\models_legs_test.py` is gone. Nothing changes size, so the size table is as it was. Young Zelda's two files as child Link come out byte for byte as in the test that passed. Of the 47 characters with such a mesh, 41 get dress bones on the legs (4 to 12 bones given a leg joint as parent; 6 short tunic hems and Darunia's have nothing below the hip). What the general rule had to learn: a column is an unbranched chain of dress bones (most skirts hang 8 columns of 5 on one root bone; the root stays); a skirt whose bones the game itself hangs on the thighs (adult Ruto, the Kokiri boys, a few men) keeps those and gets the lower ones on the shins; ADULT ZELDA's dress bones come before her leg bones in the file (her dress hangs on a belt bone under `Spine_1`), and a bone's parent must come first, so her bones are put in another order, in place (the bone records, the bone dictionary, the table from matrices to bones and the shapes' bone numbers written again: `clothlegs.hang`; the check compares by name). The leash: 26 meshes have entries with a stiffness of their own (0.00 to 1.00: adult Ruto's is 0 to 0.05, a free cloth until now), 24 have entries without (the set's stiffness counts: 1.0 in all but one, 0.8, raised to 1 for that mesh); Saria's skirt is skinned in bone space (`hclBoneSpaceSkinPOperator`) and its leash is left as it is (her bones still hang on her legs). A short skirt's points are 6 to 9 cm from the thigh: there the firm leash keeps half the way to the leg and not the way less 4 cm. RETIRED with this: `Character cloth (legs test)` (it is the cloth folder now), `Character cloth (adult skirt test)` (it hung dress tops under Link's driven tops: now known to do nothing) and `Character cloth (slide test)` (a cloth reset on the Characters page: the firm leash frees a caught dress by itself; its code patch at 0x80964e98 and in `SeqOneShotSetLODJudgeLayer` is no longer built, both are free again). What only the game can say: how the other dresses look with it (a leash that was free and is firm now is the largest change: adult Ruto, some of the women), adult Zelda's reordered model, Dark Link and everybody without a skirt (their files are not touched).
- A model file with the external file `LoadAttachAnim.flag` (0 bytes; Link and every NPC have it) also gets `Model/<project>.anim.bfres`: Link's has `Player_Child_FacialCheck` and one material animation on `Mt_Hairs`, an NPC's `<model>_1stModelPose`. Materials are named alike across characters (`Mt_Face`, `Mt_Skin`, `Mt_Eye_Inner`, ...), but the hair is `Mt_Hairs` only on Link (both ages) and child Zelda: 87 of the 98 humanoid NPC models have `Mt_Hair`, 10 no hair material (Zoras, bald men).
- The player's code needs a material named `Mt_Hairs` with the shader parameter `ns_tex_mtx0`. 0x804d8240 (called from 0x810cceb0 on every equipment sync of slot 0, and from 0x804d7f78, 0x80a9a5b8, 0x80a9abc0, 0x80a9abe8) asks every model unit of the actor for the material (unit vtable 0x84c28cc8: +0xd0 = 0x835b5880, a lookup by name in the model's material dictionary; +0xe0 = 0x80516860, parameter index by name; +0x188 = 0x835b6870, set a texture matrix) and does not check the answer: without the material it uses unit 0xFFFF and material -1 (`array[-1]`, then a call through a null vtable). It writes scale (1, 1), rotation 0, translation 0 there. 0x804d84b0 -> 0x82e21940 does the same with values from the head gear's parameters (the hair's texture is squeezed under a mask), also unchecked. Link's `Model/Player_<Age>.anim.bfres` animates `ns_param_scalar5` of the same material. No other material name is required: `Mt_ShoulderBelt` (name table at 0x84b6e0b8, users 0x82e91a88 and 0x82e96448) and `Mt_Belt_A` / `Mt_Belt_C` (0x802347a0) are searched and skipped when missing, `EquipmentUserParam.HiddenMaterialGroupList` (materials an armour hides: `G_Head` = `Mt_Hair`, `Mt_Hairband`; `G_Face_Off` = `Mt_Face`; ...) names mostly materials Link does not have. Of Link's own names the executable has 2 materials (`Mt_Hairs` in code, `Mt_ShoulderBelt` / `Mt_Inner` in tables; `Mt_Inner` belongs to the big Octorok), no shape name and 79 bone names (all in our rig, which has every Link bone).
- Materials in the file (FMAT 0xB0; layout in the head of `tools\bfres.py`): all 1,097 materials of the humanoid NPC models and Link's use shader `Model` / `model` with the same 129 parameters and 0x390 bytes of parameter data, so what the code sets by name on Link's materials exists on every NPC material. Parameter names are stored as 64-bit keys (`ns_tex_mtx0` = 0x5e0f8e359deb7624 at data offset 840, `ns_param_scalar5` = 0xa2be69e1fe91cba0 at 108; matched to names by reading the running game's parameter dictionary); a texture matrix is {u32 mode, scale x y, rotation, translation x y}, the identity on most materials, among them every hair and teeth material (the others have scale 5-35: tiled detail).
- Non-skeletal animations in the player's files: only shader parameter animations (`Mt_Hairs` in the attach file, `Mt_Face` / `Mt_Hand` in `Player_Adult_Demo_Animation`, `Mt_Eye_Inner` for Dark Link, an empty `Dummy` in `Player_Skin_Animation`); no texture pattern, bone visibility or shape animation, so nothing there names a texture an NPC file lacks.
- A frozen game still answers the debug server: after the crash above `read_memory` worked as before, which gave the loaded model (name, materials), the vtables and the array the code had indexed with -1 without a new run.
- `RSDB/ActorInfo` (8,363 rows by actor: class, radii, model, animation resources, two heap sizes): `PreActorRenderInfoHeapSize` fits the actor's stock model to the byte: 3714 x shapes + 1491 x materials + 179 x bones + 10 x meshes - 21 x smooth matrices + 1953 (least squares over 555 actors, mean error 0.9 KB; Player_Child 185,384). `InstanceHeapSize` is the actor's own heap (Player_Child 5,692,448, Player_Adult 10,245,064, PouchPlayer_Child 1,112,560, PouchPlayer_Adult 1,234,248, NPCs 2.4-2.9 MB; among NPCs about 1.9 KB per bone). A model with more bones, shapes or materials than the actor's own needs these raised. Rows are not shared, so inline ints can be changed in place (`bympatch`).
- Models as the player: only `Player_Child` + `PouchPlayer_Child` use model `Player_Child`, `Player_Adult` + `PouchPlayer_Adult` + `Player_Base` use `Player_Adult` (Dark Link and the opening have their own). Adult Link's tunics, boots, gauntlets and the child's masks are separate `Armor_*` actors with their own models bound to his skeleton. No `Bake/Model` data exists.
- Material colours (customization session, 2026-11-13; from the model files and the code, the first use of it is built and not seen in game yet). All character materials use one shader with the same parameters (above); what a parameter does depends on the material's kind. HAIR (the materials with the colour ramp `_gn0` = `CmnTex_Chara_Hair_LUT_*` and the thickness map `_th0`): `ns_param_color0` and `ns_param_color1` are the hair's colour; 58 different values among 90 hair materials, and two women (`Npc_Hylia_WF_003` / `_006`) share one hair texture and differ in colour 0 alone (0.64, 0.27, 0.27 against 0.45, 0.64, 0.27: red and green). Link and young Zelda: (0.5, 0.169, 0.017) / (0.40, 0.16, 0.03); Saria (0.46, 0.63, 0.10); Nabooru (0.72, 0.06, 0.02); Impa (0.45, 0.80, 0.87). Values are linear. `Mt_Eyebrow` (12 models; the others have brows in the face texture) is a grey mask coloured the same way. THE IRIS (`Mt_Eye_Inner`, 74 of the player models): 50 characters share the texture `Eye_Inner_Iris_CmnTex_Chara_Eye_Alb` (blue, mean hue 0.608) and differ in `ns_param_color2`, which reads as a shift of the texture's colour: x = hue (0.5 = as the texture; Impa 0.97 and Sheik 0.89, the two with red eyes; a green-eyed man 0.22), y = saturation and z = brightness (0.5 = as it is; 0.31 to 0.72 and 0.41 to 0.52 in the game), w something else (0.31 to 0.64). 14 other iris textures (Gerudo amber, hue 0.115; Ganondorf 0.081; Darunia 0.751; Ruto 0.712) under (0.5, 0.5, 0.5). SKIN: colour 0 = (1, 0.03, 0) on most faces and bodies (scattering, by the look of it). CLOTH: nothing colours plain cloth (colour 0 is (1, 0, 0) or (0, 0, 0), the others white); velvet and satin have colours 0 and 1 (sheen); recoloured clothes in the game are other textures (`..._Alb.00` / `.01`). A parameter's 64-bit keys: colour 0 to 3 = 0x68E131E72BCD91AD, 0xE647277D46E8C1D8, 0x0187464EC6541F6E, 0x961383B79667F911 (`tools\npcportrait.py` `PARAMS`). SETTING A VALUE ON A LIVING MODEL, as the game does it (0x80232e2c sets colour 0 of every material of a model to (1, 1, 1, a); the string `ns_param_color0` is at 0x83dd2ed1): a model unit's virtual functions (vtable 0x84c28cc8), +0xc8 the number of materials, +0xd0 (unit, &name) a material's index by name, +0xe0 (unit, material, &name) a parameter's index by name, then pairs of get / set by type from +0xe8, among them +0x150 / +0x158 (unit, material, parameter, &four floats) and +0x180 / +0x188 the texture matrix; -1 = not there. Names are given as the address of a pointer to the characters. Underneath it is nn::g3d: unit +0x108 the material objects (0x80 each; +0x110, if not 0, a list of pointers to them instead), a material object's +0x0 the material of the model file (FMAT) and +0x48 its own copy of the values; the file's own values are at `[FMAT + 0x58]` with the same offsets (`[[[FMAT + 0x10]] + 0x20]` + 0x18 x parameter, u16 at +0x10), which is where a model's own colour can be read back. FROM AN ACTOR TO ITS UNITS, as the hair routine goes (0x804d825c): `[actor + 0x270]` the model component, its `[+8]` an object whose vtable's +0xf8 is `[0x84cd9d58]` for the usual kind, that object's `[+0x18]` a list (+0x20 count, +0x28 pointers; a unit is `[[entry]]`). THE EQUIPMENT COMPONENT is `[player actor + 0x470]` (0x833cbff0 syncs slots 4, 0, then 1, 2, 3 unless bit 0 of `[[0x84e9cfa8] + 0x5c0]`, then 8, 9, 10, 12), its `[+0x18]` the actor, its slots 13 handles of 0x18 bytes from +0xe0 (+0x8 link, +0x10 id, -1 = empty; the actor is `[link + 0x40]` while `[link + 0x48]` is the id, as a menu slot); 0x810cceb0 (component, slot, x) syncs one slot and calls the hair routine first when the slot is 0. Which slot holds what is not read yet (0 is the head; 0x828f3028 and 0x81033f08 sync slot 1 next to the code that names `EquipmentUser_Weapon`). The game's `Armor_*` actors by name: `Armor_Adult_Normal` / `_Goron` / `_Zora`, `_HeavyBoot` / `_HoverBoot`, `Armor_Child_Mask_Keaton` / `_Skull` / `_Spooky` / `_Bunny` / `_Truth` / `_Goron`, `Armor_Child_Gauntlet_Goron`. THE MENU ACTOR MANAGER, as the menu's own nodes get it (0x80c384b8): `[[0x84e9cfa8] + 0x4e8]` (0x84e9cfa8 is the variable itself, not a table entry). UNUSED NODE CLASSES beyond the one-shot ones (a scan of every `Seq...` class name against every graph): `SeqExecuteLoadFile` (enter 0x832e1620, update 0x832e16d0, to 0x832e19f0: 976 bytes, the colours' code), `SeqExecuteSaveFile` (0x832e39d0 / 0x832e3b00, to 0x832e3c00: 560 bytes, the colours' tables), `SeqQueryCreate3DLayer` (enter 0x8103ae80-0x8103b160, 736 bytes, free), `SeqExecuteRequestSaveGameData` (0x83bcdc10-0x83bcdde0, 464, free), `SeqExecuteLoadStartupMap` (0x83bcce70-0x83bcd000, 400, free). A check for "no branch goes into it" must look at the code only: beyond .text a data word can read as a branch (0x83e52ac4 reads as one into `SeqExecuteSaveFile`).
- Material colours, corrections and more (customization session, 2026-11-13, after snaildev's look and a read of his running game). (1) Hair: colours 0 and 1 colour the SHINE; the hair's colour is its texture's (measured: every character's hair texture has the character's colour, only the shared townswoman's is near black). (2) The iris: colour 2's three numbers are each added to the texture's hue / saturation / brightness, twice their distance from a half, and clamped (not wrapped: both ends of the hue are red). (3) An actor's components hang on `[actor + 0x270]`: +0x8 the model component, +0x200 the equipment component (the head gear update 0x804d7b20 reads it so; `[[0x84c9e0d0 -> variable]] + 0x470` is the same component of the pouch's target, the field character, which is how a menu routine reaches him: his actor is that component's `[+0x18]`; seen live: actor name `Player`, state 4, while the menu character `PouchPlayer_Child` is in state 6, asleep, with the menu closed). (4) Equipment slots seen live on a child: 0 `Armor_Child_Mask_Skull`, 4 `Weapon_Sheath_Kokiri`, 8 `Item_Ocarina_Time`, 9 `Armor_Child_Gauntlet_Goron`, 12 `Item_Ocarina_Time_Holder` (each an actor with a name at `[actor + 0x20]` and the "shown" bit 0x10000 at +0x4ac; the menu character has the same five of its own); the component's bytes +0x13c4 / +0x13c6 are the weapon's / shield's drawn state (the pouch player's node `ExecuteSetPouchPlayerDrawn`, 0x80a9aa60 / 0x80a9a440, sets them from the hovered item). (5) What runs every frame for a player: the field player's update is 0x82c3d740 (vtable slot at 0x84b45fb0); it calls 0x804d7190, which calls 0x804d74b0(part at actor + 0x7c0, 1), and that runs four updates in a row with the part in x19 (0x804d6eb0, 0x804d7b20 the head gear, 0x804d7690, 0x804d7810) unless a virtual call says otherwise (0x804d755c); 0x80e56f18 and 0x82e8fa5c call 0x804d74b0 for two other actor classes; the part's +0x110 is the actor. The equipment sync's call of the hair routine (0x810ccee4) is NOT passed when a field character is made.
- Clothing colours (customization session, 2026-11-14; from the 234 player model files of the full build, offline). 2,651 materials under 193 names. A clothing material's first sampler is always `_a0`, its colour texture; clothing has 4 to 8 samplers. Of the 106 colour textures of what is clothing by name, most are ASTC (8x5 42, 8x6 18, 6x6 14, 8x8 4, 5x4 2, 10x8 2), the others BC1 13, BC7 10, BC3 1; 256 to 1536 texels square; all sRGB with the components in their own order. Link's clothes: the child's tunic is `Mt_Cloth` with a tiling weave (`Angin_Tile_A_Alb`, green in the texture itself), `Mt_Vest` and the hat's `Mt_Band` share one 1536 texture, then `Mt_Cloth_Pants`, `Mt_Cloth_Parts`, `Mt_Leather`; the adult has `Mt_Vest`, `Mt_Angin` (the same tile), `Mt_Angin_Acs`, `Mt_Inner` (the undershirt), `Mt_Leater_Upper`, `Mt_Glove`, `Mt_Boots`. A material's name does not say what is a garment: Sheik's and Impa's blue suits are `Mt_Leather`, a Goron's `Mt_Body` is his skin and Sheik's a wrap. No parameter was found that tints a cloth material (colour 0 differs between cloth materials but is not the cloth's colour; Dark Link's model has adult Link's textures and ONE set of parameter values on all its materials, colour 0 = 1 1 1 1 everywhere: he is not darkened by his materials' parameters). In memory a material's texture names (+0x20) point at strings with a 16-bit length before the characters (the model loader reads the first character at +2, 0x803819d8). ASTC: a block is sixteen bytes that decode by themselves, and a texture's data is a run of blocks (every mip, in the console's order, padding blocks of zeros between), so it can be decoded, repainted and encoded again block by block without knowing the order or the mips (`tools\texrecolor.py`, `astc_blocks`: blocks the repainting does not change keep their bytes; about a second and a half for a 1536 texture, mean error 0.0015 to 0.003 of full scale at the encoder's quality 80).
- Menu Link placement (`GameParameter/MenuActorParam/PouchPlayer_<Age>`): child at (0, 0.3, 0) turned -30 degrees, camera base (0, 0.2, 4.5) aiming (0.07, 0.95, 0), fovy 20; adult at (0.1, -0.2, 0) turned -40, camera (-0.2, 0.45, 5.0) aiming (0.2, 0.7, 0), fovy 26. With the live camera of the child view (README above) 1.44 m above the feet is visible: an adult NPC shown as child Link loses its head without the zoom. `PouchPlayerParam` has the + menu's other cameras (weapon, shield, armor, boots, head armor).
- The 3D UI camera update (0x8289d110-0x8289d80c) uses FP registers 0-15 only and x0-x2, x8-x13, x19-x24; it keeps its return address in its frame (one return), so a hook there may use `bl`, x14-x17 and v16-v31.
- Emulator: NSO segments are mapped page-aligned and the whole module image is patchable, so besides the padding after `.text` there are 3,968 zero bytes at the end of `.rodata` (0x8486c080-0x8486d000, read-only: room for tables); not used yet. The built-in Dynamic FPS patch is 120 bytes at 0x83c66200 plus a call at main+0x6E6CC4 (Fritchii 4.1 source, `patch_manager.cpp`).
- All six save slots are child Link (2026-11-09); slots 00-02 have the character flag, 03-05 predate it.
- Models outside `Npc_*` (scan of 2026-11-13, offline only, nothing built or seen in game; the first scan took only files named `Npc_*`). Of the 1,278 model files that are not terrain, 120 have all 21 core bones (`npcrig.CORE`): the 98 NPCs, the four `Player_*` models (`Player_Adult_Dark` = Dark Link, `Player_Child_Op` = the opening's child Link), three `Armor_Adult_*` (Link's tunics), and 15 more: `Enemy_SkullKid`, `Enemy_DungeonBoss_Kotake` (two models in that project: the boss, and `Npc_DungeonBoss_Kotake` for the actors `Dm_Npc_Kotake` / `Dm_Npc_Koume`), `Enemy_DungeonBoss_Twinrova`, `Enemy_DungeonBoss_Ganon` (757 bones, head at 7.9 m), `Enemy_IronKnuckle_Silver` (also the black one's model), `Enemy_MiddleBoss_IronKnuckle_Nabooru` (+ `_Demo`), `Enemy_Stalfos`, `Enemy_Gibdo`, `Enemy_ReDead`, `Enemy_Moblin`, `Enemy_Moblin_Club`, `Enemy_Wolfos`, `Animal_Cow`. The normal build's rig (retargeted legs, faces) builds and passes `check_rig` for both ages on all of them except: `IronKnuckle_Silver`, `Gibdo`, `ReDead`, `Moblin` have no material among `HAIR_STAND_INS` (they need one more stand-in whose `ns_tex_mtx0` is the identity), and `Stalfos`' legs are not the plain chain (he would keep the first rig's legs, like `LEGS_AS_THEY_ARE`). One core bone short (`Neck`): `Enemy_Dinolfos`, `Enemy_DungeonBoss_Dodongo`, `Npc_U_Kaepora`, the five `Animal_Horse_*`; two or three short: Flare Dancer, Dead Hand (no ankles), `Enemy_Scrub_Business`. `tools\bfres.py` cannot read five files yet ("unknown skeleton arrays": `Enemy_Lizalfos`, `Enemy_Stalchild`, `Enemy_Stalchild_Big`, `Enemy_Skulltula`, Bongo Bongo's body). Not humanoid by their bones: the Poes and Deku Scrubs (no legs or no arms), the scarecrows (`Npc_U_Pierre`, no legs), the cursed Skulltula family (`Npc_Hylia_MSP_*`, 4 core bones), the Deku Tree sprout, Navi, Jabu-Jabu, Anubis.
- The "more characters" test build (2026-11-13; `mods\more_characters.py` -> `build-more\`, own emulator copy on Lichii build 160: `tools\moreenv.py`, `PLAY-debug-more.bat`, debug server 6548; first look in game, snaildev, 2026-11-13: the copy starts, the page opens, all 15 appear on the page, in the field "odd but playable", no crash). It is the normal lite build with 15 of those models appended to the character list from outside (stored numbers 100-114: Dark Link, Skull Kid, Koume & Kotake in both forms, Twinrova, the three Iron Knuckle models, Gibdo, ReDead, both Moblins, Wolfos, the cow, the opening's Link) and to the picker's roster (two full sheets of 18, placeholder portraits). No file of the other sessions is edited. Against an unmodified lite build made the same minute, only these differ: the model hook's patch, the size table, the picker's graph, layout and messages, and the model files C098 and up (the two model experiments move from C098 / C099 to C113 / C114); every existing character's model, cloth file and pack is byte for byte the same. What it took: a hair stand-in for the four models that have none of `HAIR_STAND_INS` (`more_characters.HAIR`: `Mt_Inner`, `Mt_Eye_Inner`, `Mt_Wood`, `Mt_Lens`; every material of those four has the identity as `ns_tex_mtx0`), and no cloth of their own (the enemies' `ControllerSetParam` has no `Cloth` list, which `npc_characters.cloth_source` expects). Ganon is behind `--ganon`: as a player model he has 59 shapes and 1,077 bones, a pre-actor render heap need of 465,692 bytes against the 0x60000 the player actors get (`npc_characters.HEAPS`); the flag raises the four actors' heaps for everybody (a guess for the instance heap), so he is a round of his own.
- Lizalfos and the skeleton's mirror table (2026-11-13, offline; in the test build as stored number 115, not yet seen in game). The five files `tools\bfres.py` rejects ("unknown skeleton arrays") have bit 7 (0x80) set in the skeleton's flags (0x1280 against 0x1200 / 0x1100 on the other 315 character models) and a pointer at skeleton +0x30 to one s16 per bone: the index of the bone's mirror image, its own index on the centre line, -1 for a bone without one (`Ph_Sword_1`, `Item`, a one-sided shoulder pad). +0x28 is null in all of them. Link's skeleton has no such table, so for a player model it is dropped (flag off, pointer null; `more_characters.adapted`, done on the stock bytes before `bfres.py` reads them, that file is not edited). Lizalfos then has every core bone but `Neck`: his neck is `Neck_1` > `Neck_2` > `Neck_3` > `Head`; `Neck_1` is renamed `Neck` (Link's neck animation drives the lowest of the three, the other two keep their rest pose). His materials are `Mt_Armor`, `Mt_Body`, `Mt_Eye` (the hair stand-in is `Mt_Eye`); his eye bones are `EyeBall_L` / `EyeBall_R`. The same two steps should do for `Enemy_Stalchild` (it has `Neck`), and the rename alone for `Enemy_Dinolfos`; not tried.
- In the main build since 2026-11-13 (snaildev, after seeing every picture and the page in the test copy: "good to add to the main version with dark link and skull kid on the first page"): `build.py`'s main() calls `more_characters.apply()`; the ring is 117 (100-115 the new ones, 116 the hidden "Link (adult)" whose model exists for his picture only), `build.PICKER_ROSTER` is 37 characters on three sheets. Checked offline: the main build's own checks; against a build of the same code without the hook only the files that must change differ; `mods\field_swap.py`'s build equals the main build in seven folders; `mods\npc_characters.py` by itself still builds its ring of 100. Final framing of the pictures, by his notes: `morecutout.ADJUST` and `SQUARE` (the witches' head is the wide top part of their silhouette, 25 columns apart between the two forms; the cow's head is the left end of hers).
- The full build with them, and their cloth (2026-11-14, offline only, not seen in game). Enemy actors keep cloth the way NPC actors do: a `ClothList` and a `Cloth` entry in the ControllerSetParam, the ClothParam inside the pack (Skull Kid, both witch forms, Twinrova, the three Iron Knuckle models, both Moblins, Wolfos, the cow, the opening's Link, Lizalfos; Dark Link's settings name `Player_Adult.phcl`). The Nabooru Iron Knuckle packs carry two sets, the plain Iron Knuckle's and their own; the one named as the model is taken. The exception: `Enemy_ReDead`'s settings (used by the Gibdo too) have a `ClothList` naming `Enemy_ReDead.phcl` but no `Cloth` entry, so no ClothParam and no meshes: those two get no cloth. With `npc_characters.CLOTH_MAX` raised from 103 to 127 the cloth folder covers 110 characters and its hook uses 416 of the 460 bytes of `SeqOneShotLuaScript`'s body. A layout limit met on the way: an animation key names its pane in 9 bits (`uiedit.key_set_pane`), so panes that animations address must have an index below 512; the picker with 115 characters and four panes each passed it. The detail text panes (`T_CellText_ccc`, shipped with empty messages since the picker freeze) are no longer made in the main build (`ui_sandbox.PICK_CELL_TEXT`, note for the UI session: a switch added to its file, True by default so its own build is unchanged): 433 panes for 115 characters on 7 sheets.
- Portrait captures, first run (snaildev, 2026-11-13: Lizalfos in the field "Good"; the run "ran through"): 185 frames of 3840 x 2160 from the capture copy on build 160 with its empty shader cache, 6 to 16 per character, black and white frames for all 17. `tools\morecutout.py --fit` wrote 16 pictures to `build-more\portraits\cut-fit\` (nobody has looked at them yet; the numbers say several are doubtful: the Wolfos square is 99.8 % covered, so it lies inside his body; Koume & Kotake's square sits far left of the standing line; eight characters reach the left edge of the cut-out region with weapons or limbs). The cow reaches into the patch `npccutout.backdrop` reads the backdrop colour from, so no frame of hers counted as black or white; `morecutout.backdrop` takes the calmest of five patches (the first is `npccutout`'s own; the other 15 pictures came out byte for byte the same with it).
- Page zoom for bodies the Head bone says little about, and adult Link's picture (2026-11-13; built and checked offline, the zooms seen in the capture copy by snaildev: "all in view"; the pictures and the switch not yet seen). The page's zoom goes by the Head bone's height; Koume & Kotake (a broom 4.2 m long at a head height of 0.94 m) and the cow (a four-legged body 4.3 m long) were far too close. `more_characters.ZOOM` multiplies the height the zoom is told (1.6 for both witch forms, 2.8 for the cow: page factors 1.65, 4.62, 3.72 as a child). The cut-out tool's rule "everybody's Head bone is on one row" does not hold for them (`morecutout.eyes` corrects the eye row; the boss witches' and the cow's squares are set outright). In the frames the two witch forms are the same silhouette from the neck down, the boss form 25 columns further right. Adult Link's picture: the adult model on the child's actor is one more character in the capture build only (number 116); the picker's first sheet gets a second picture pane on Link's slot (`P_FaceAdult`) and the layout an animation `Age` (frame 0: the child's pane visible, frame 1: the adult's), which the page's entry stops at the frame the GameData Bool `Sequence_IsChildhood` says (query node as `LoadBancImpl` 43, a bool selector, two stop-animation requests; in the entry's `also` list beside `Pulse`).
- Portrait captures for the new characters (2026-11-13, how it is set up): `mods\more_characters.py --capture --install` is `npc_characters.main()`'s capture build with our list, into `build-more-capture\` and a second copy of build 160 (`moreenv.CAPTURE`, `PLAY-debug-more-capture.bat`: scale 1.5 for 3840 x 2160 frames as in the first runs, and a shader cache of its own, so the normal install's prepared shaders cannot be touched by the other scale). D-pad down on the Characters page runs child Link (the cut-out's scale) and the 16. `tools\moreshots.py` (= `npcshots.py` on port 6548, list in `build-more\portraits\shots.jsonl`) saves the frames, `tools\morecutout.py --fit` (= `npccutout.py` with our list and folders) writes `build-more\portraits\cut-fit\<nnn>_<name>.png`, which the test build's picker takes in at its next build (`more_characters.portrait_files`). Eyes for the fitted square: `EYE_BONES` in `morecutout.py` where the bones have other names (Skull Kid, the Moblins, Lizalfos); the Iron Knuckles, Gibdo, ReDead and the cow have no eye bones and get a guess from the head's size.

## What we know about the field player (resident actors) and the field swap

Found by the field swap session (2026-11-10; `mods\field_swap.py`, `install-field.bat`, own emulator copy `PLAY-debug-field.bat`, separate from `install-mods.bat`). What is from code and data is marked so. Seen in game since (snaildev, 2026-11-10): the swap itself, the place and facing kept, and the camera kept (next bullet).

Note from the main build's session (2026-11-10): the normal build now takes the swap in through `build.field_swap_hooks()` (README, mods list, "The field swap in the full build"). It uses these names of `mods\field_swap.py`, so please keep them working: `MOD`, `PENDING_FLAG`, `KEEP_CAMERA`, `close_page_with_menu`, `add_page_screen`, `_swap_step_list`, `field_patch`, `check_patch`, `check_graphs`. A change in that module comes into the normal build at its next build; `build-field\` and the field emulator copy stay yours for trying it first.

- In game (snaildev, 2026-11-10, Fritchii 5.8): the swap works (several in a row, controls, Navi, Z-targeting, a door afterwards all fine); place and facing are kept; the camera stays where it was ("that fixes it"). What it took for the camera, in three rounds:
  - First version (everybody is put to sleep, as the game does for an area change; `--sleep-all` still builds it): the wake puts the camera at the player's place and heading, as after a door.
  - Nobody put to sleep (the wake only touches sleeping actors, and the new player is created asleep): the camera is untouched through the swap, then snaps behind the player about three frames after the wake. Recorded with `tools\fieldcam.py`. snaildev noticed that re-picking the SAME character kept the camera.
  - The cause (code, 0x8039b570, the player camera component's update; the component is `[[PlayerCamera actor + 0x270] + 0x360]`, reached from the camera system object `[0x84e40c08]`, whose handle on the actor is at +0x38 in the resident entry's shape: link +8, id +0x10): the camera keeps at +0xfe0 the target's camera part it followed last (`PlayerCameraTarget` component, `[[player + 0x270] + 0x368]`). Each update it compares the part of its current target with that; if it is another object, it places itself 4 units behind the target (0x80746280, which also sets bit 1 of the component's byte +0x37 for that frame). The same character is kept only because the new player is allocated at the old address. The update does not run under the `SceneTransition` pause, and +0xfe0 keeps the old (by then freed) address meanwhile. Bit 1 of the part's byte +0x24c also skips the placement, but the part rewrites that byte every frame (tried: set before the wake, gone 40 ms later, before the camera looked).
  - The fix (the default build now): our wake step writes the new, still sleeping player's part into the camera's +0xfe0 right before the wake (`fs_keep` in `mods\field_swap.py`), so every swap is the same-character case. The routine now ends at 0x83302240.
  - Other camera facts found on the way (code): camera messages are 0x4a8d0000 + n, handled at 0x82b8da70 (0 = reset request: bit 1 of byte +0x7d2 of the part at component + 0x5b8; 7 = bit 0 and "at once" +0x838); the node `OneShotGenericSendPlayerCameraMessage` (0x80bb1190) sends them, message 5 = restart the camera from the current view (0x82bace70 -> 0x80746170: copies position / look-at / up / field of view from `[component + 0x38]` (+0x60, +0x88, +0xb0, +0xc4) into the three state slots at +0xa08, +0xa30, +0xaa8); `OneShotGenericSavePlayerCamera` = 0x82bacdf0; `OneShotGenericSendReserveNotRebootPlayerCamera` sets bit 15 of the system object's +0x250. No player logic graph sends a reset (only the hookshot's has the word).
  - Timing seen in the recordings: about 1.5 to 2 seconds between the old player being gone and the new one existing (the model load), then about a quarter second to the wake. That is most of the black.

- The field swap build (as first handed over; the default build since leaves out the two sleep steps and adds the camera write, see above): every mod of `build.py` into `build-field\` exactly as the normal build makes them (checked against a scratch build of the same code: 227 files identical), plus: a Bool `CharacterMenu_FieldSwapPending` (not saved) that the picker's A sets in the frame its swap starts; a screen row `CharacterFade_00` (the game's fade layout `EventFade_00`, no class, draw unit 1, sort key 9100: over the menus, their header and the game's own fades, under the save icon and the talk window); and the folder `Character field swap`, which sorts before the normal folders and therefore wins with its own `Sequence/MinusMenuToMain.module.ainb` (the menu closing), and has the code patch and the combined size table. Switched off in the Add-Ons list, everything is the normal build. The menu closing with a swap pending: after the equipment wait, the fade screen opens over the menu and goes black (`VeryShortIn`, 15 frames); the menu closes and its pause is released as always; then, one step per frame: `SeqOneShotShooterResetShootableAll`, the stock sleep node, `SeqOneShotRequestPause` `SceneTransition`, our node in delete mode, the stock create node (child / adult by `Sequence_IsChildhood`), `SeqExecuteWaitCreateActor` twice, our node in wake mode, `WaitEquip.module`, `SeqOneShotReleasePause`, `ShortOut` (30 frames), close + unload the fade screen, the flag off. With nothing pending the file runs frame for frame as the normal one (each addition is a second part of an existing step, in the same frame). Offline: our routine emulated in unicorn (38 runs: both modes, both ages, the polling until gone / awake, an update after the node finished, a wake without a remembered place, stale handles, no manager); the graph read back; the node class and the code we overwrite checked against the image at every build. Not handled yet: a swap while riding (child saves cannot ride); a pending swap also runs when A picked the character the field Link already is.
- Resident actors (data: `Scene/Component/ResidentActorMgrParam/Default.game__scene__ResidentActorMgrParam.bgyml`): three lists. `Common`: `PlayerCamera`, `EventCamera`, `CameraXLinkControl`, `PostProcessingActor`, `Dm_Npc_RevivalFairy` (dependent parent `Player_Base`), `Exterminator` (its own wake position (0, -3975, 0)), `Npc_Navi`, `FaroreWindWarpPoint`. `Child`: `Player_Child`. `Adult`: `Player_Adult`. Per entry: `IsCancelSleepAtEnterBanc` (false for the fairy and the warp point: they are neither put to sleep nor woken by the two routines below), `HasCancelSleepPos` / `CancelSleepPos`, `DependentActorChild` / `DependentActorParent`.
- The resident actor manager (code): a node reaches it with 0x80965110(node) = `[[node + 8] + 8] + 0x1f0` (the scene's component holder) and 0x80965170(holder) (component 15, null if the scene has none). +0x28 / +0x30 common list (count, entries), +0x38 / +0x40 child, +0x48 / +0x50 adult, +0x70 "the player is the child one" (written by create), +0x71 "a delete is under way", +0x20 -> +0x150 the parameter. An entry is 0x70 bytes: +0x8 link, +0x10 handle id (-1 none), +0x18 a pending create request, +0x20 the actor's path as a string; the actor is `[link + 0x40]` while `[link + 0x48]` is the id; actor state at +0x2c: 3 / 6 asleep, 4 awake, 7-11 going away; position +0x31c, rotation 3x3 +0x328 as on the menu actors.
  - Create, 0x80965610(manager, is child) (the node `SeqExecuteSceneRequestCreateResidentActors`: enter 0x80965040, update 0x80ebf840, polled until it returns 1): stores the age, asks the OTHER age's list to go (0x809654c0 = the adult list, 0x832cb8d0 = the child list; each returns 1 when its actors are gone and, if it had asked for a delete, `[[0x84e98e28] + 0x470]` is null), then creates every entry of the common list and of this age's list that has no live actor (0x809656b0: at the origin, rotation identity; an entry whose actor is going away gets a new one at once). So an age change (a banc with `IsForceSwitchChildOrAdult`, `LoadBancImpl` nodes 22 / 36 / 34) deletes and re-creates only the player; cameras, Navi and the rest stay, asleep, across the area load. The game has no node that deletes the player of the CURRENT age: that is the one thing the field swap adds.
  - Delete all, 0x809659f0 (`SeqOneShotSceneRequestDeleteResidentActors`: `ChangeBanc` with `IsDeleteResidentActorsBeforeDestroyBanc`, return to title, scene destroy). Sleep, 0x80963920 (`SeqExecuteSceneRequestSleepResidentActors`; `SleepResidentActor.module` runs `SeqOneShotShooterResetShootableAll` first): waits while a create request is pending, then sends sleep to the common list and the current age's. Cancel sleep, 0x80963550(manager, &position, &rotation 3x3) (`SeqExecuteSceneRequestCancelSleepResidentActors`, whose enter turns its `CancelSleepRot` input, Euler angles, into the matrix with 0x809632c0): waits while a create request is pending, sends each actor that is in state 3 or 6 a wake message with the position (or the entry's own) and the rotation, and returns 1 on the first call that had nobody left to wake.
  - Where the game does these (graphs): `ChangeBanc.module` = `BeforeChangeBanc`, `UnloadBanc` (its `DoBeforeUnload`: sleep, or delete all), pause `SceneTransition`, `LoadBanc` (`LoadBancImpl`: create residents, then the banc's actors, the menu's actors), `AfterChangeBanc` (cancel sleep at `SeqQueryGetTraverseBasePos` with the GameData vector `Sequence_CancelSleepRot`, then release `SceneTransition`). So create and wake run under the `SceneTransition` pause, sleep before it. `Retry` (game over), `TitleToMainWithLoadSave` and the + menu's load go through the same module with the saved position (`PlayerStatus.SavePos`, `SavePosRadY` through `SeqCalcSavePlayerForward`). No sequence node reads the player's rotation (`SeqGetPlayerPos` gives the position only).
- Sequence node classes in the executable (code). The name getter is vtable slot +0x98 (three instructions: adrp, add, ret), so name string -> getter -> the one pointer to it -> vtable. Slots: +0xe0 registers the inputs (0x82633920, a bare `ret`, = no inputs; a Bool is registered with 0x803f94c0(node, &name, node + 0x20, 1, 0) and read as `[[node + 0x20]]`), +0xe8 enter, +0xf0 update, +0xf8 exit, +0x138 the body of a one-shot node (called by the shared one-shot enter 0x832e9ae0; the shared one-shot update is empty, so a one-shot runs once). x0 = the node. `[node + 0x18]` bits 30-31 are the state: an Execute node sets them to 2 when it is finished (`0x80000000 | low 30 bits`); stock updates start with vtable +0x80 (0x8002c800: the state) and do nothing when it is 2; the stock create node's enter clears the bits first.
  - 122 Execute / one-shot classes are in no stock graph, and none of them has an entry in `Sequence/NodeDefinition` (which lists exactly the 433 used ones). A class can be created from a graph only if it has a creator (a routine that allocates the node and stores the vtable) whose address the node factory takes (registration code around 0x81086900-0x81088000 and 0x8118e000): the classes whose vtables are at 0x84c8.... (code at 0x83bc.... / 0x83bd....: `SeqExecuteWaitFrame`, `SeqExecuteSwitchInt`, the `SeqExecuteUIPrototyping*` nodes, `SeqExecuteWaitGlobalResidentEventLoad`, a second `SeqExecuteWaitEventStart`, ...) have NO creator: their vtables are never stored anywhere, a graph cannot have them. (The check: `a64.Image().xrefs(vtable)` is empty.) `SeqQueryControllerReleaseMaskChecker`, which the picker uses, has one (0x8330f2f0, taken at 0x8108ca68).
  - Unused classes that can be created, have routines of their own and no or one simple input: `SeqExecuteCommitSave` and `SeqExecuteWaitCullingReady` (no inputs; the second has only an update of its own), `SeqExecuteWaitInitializeEventMgr` (one Bool, `IsNeedWaitResidentEvent`; enter is a bare `ret` at 0x832e74c0, update 0x832e74d0; creator 0x832e7470): the field swap takes this one over.
  - Room for code that no cave has: the bodies of unused nodes. `SeqOneShotSetGameDataAppearScarecrowFalseAll`'s one-shot body, 0x83302000-0x83302300, is one function of 0x300 bytes that only its vtable points at (the field swap uses 0x83302000-0x83302194). `mods\field_swap.py`, `check_dead_code`, is the test for such a region: its bytes are the ones the patch was written for, no jump or call from outside lands in it, no pointer but the vtable's, no address taken.
- Writable memory of our own: main's `.bss` ends at 0x84eba4c8 (MOD0 header at 0x83c6705c: bss 0x84d66b80-0x84eba4c8) and the page goes on to 0x84ebb000, mapped and zero. The field swap keeps 0x80 bytes at 0x84ebaf80 (`stash_address`): +0 valid, +4 calls in delete mode, +8 position, +0x14 rotation, +0x38 the manager, +0x40 the player actor remembered last, +0x48 calls in wake mode, +0x4c wakes finished.
- Screens for a fade: the game's own (`EventFade_00` draw unit 0, `EventFade_01` and `LoadingFade_00` draw unit 1 sort key 9000) have classes (`UIEventFadeScreen`, `UILoadingFadeScreen`) and the loading one comes with `PrepareScreensOfLoading.module` / `CloseLoadingFade.module`, which also close and reopen the HUD, the scenery sound and the main layer. Layout `EventFade_00`: `Color` frame 0 black / 1 white; `VeryShortIn` 15 frames, `ShortIn` 30, `MiddleIn` 60, `LongIn` 120 (alpha of `N_In_00` 0 -> 1) and the same four `...Out` (1 -> 0). Draw unit 1 above 9000: `Ocarina_00` 9001, `BoxCursor_00` 9002, `SaveLoadIcon_00` 10000, `Talk_00` 11000. `SeqExecuteLoadAndOpenScreen` (stock: `SystemBG_00`, `TitleMenuBG_00`, both without a class) loads and opens in one node.
- Pauses (graphs): `GamePause` (the menus: `ChangeGamePause.module`, counted in `Sequence_GamePauseRequestCount`), `SceneTransition` (area changes), `SceneBeginning`, `GameOverPause`, `EventBancChangePause`, `WarpPause`; requested and released by name with `SeqOneShotRequestPause` / `SeqOneShotReleasePause`. `MinusMenuToMain.module` (both menus close through it, input `MinusOrPouch`): `WaitEquip.module` (waits for `SeqQueryIsPouchTargetEquipmentUserCreationFinishedAll`, at most 300 frames), then `PlusMinusPauseOff.module` with the step that closes the screens.

- The quick swap, an experiment (field swap session, 2026-11-14, snaildev: "get rid of the fade-out so it's a quicker swap", and a "slight stutter-step" of the new character during the fade-in). Built with `mods\field_swap.py --release --quick` on top of the v0.2.0 zip, installed into the field swap copy only; NOT the default, NOT in the main build (`field_swap.QUICK` is False there; the release check proves the default graph and patch are byte for byte v0.2.0's). What it rests on:
  - The − menu does not show the field at all (graphs): `PlusMinusPauseCapture.module` captures the game screen (`ExecuteOneShotRequestGameScreenCapture`) and hides the main 3D layer (`SeqOneShotSetLayerVisible`, tag 460120899); `PlusMinusPauseOff.module` shows it again only in its `FinishPostGamePause` step (with `MenuBG_00` closing and the capture cancelled), input `IsSetMainLayerVisiableAfterPostGamePause`. So the field player can be swapped while the menu is still up without anything showing; the quick variant does the delete, the create and the wake right after `WaitEquip` (still under the menu's pause), and after the menu has closed only a second wake (ends at once if the first finished) and the flag off.
  - Whether a wake finishes under the menu's pause is not known (under `SceneTransition` it does, in one frame). So in QUICK a wake node gives up once, after 30 calls that did not finish (`WAKE_LIMIT`, counted at stash +0x50, cleared by every delete), with the place still remembered; the second wake node after the close polls until done.
  - Timing from the last recording (Fritchii 5.8, menu already closed): the delete 0.1 s, the new player there 1.6 to 2.0 s later, the wake 0.5 s after that (the two `SeqExecuteWaitCreateActor`). The new player lands at the same address every time. Unknown: whether the create is quicker while the menu character (the same model file) is still alive.
  - The stutter step: in the recording the new player's root does not move at all in the 1.5 s after the wake (to 0.1 mm), so what snaildev saw is the pose. The game's own scene start (`SceneBeginning.module`, three frames after its wake) puts the player where it is with `SeqOneShotSetPlayerPos` (body 0x80964cf0): the position setter 0x80964e70 with its warp flag on (0x804e6630 on `[[player + 0x270] + 0x50]`) and bit 0 of the halfword at `[[player + 0x270] + 0x358] + 0x1c30` set; the wake by message does neither. QUICK runs that node after the wake with the position from `SeqGetPlayerPos` (not the game's safety-place search, which may move the player) and the riding flag as the game asks it. The first suspect, not proven.
