Also in that module, not taken: `SeqOneShotSetClothCenter` (a global cloth centre, +0x138 of `[0x84ea7ff0]`), `SeqExecuteWaitFadeSceneForGraphics`. `FastLoadOn.module` (CPU boost, 3D layer background only) is the game's speed-up for loads.
  - `tools\fieldcam.py` also logs the wake's failed calls, the player component's flag halfword +0x1c30 and `Sequence_IsPause`.
  - Seen (snaildev, 2026-11-14, Lichii 160): when the menu closes the MENU character disappears at once (the vignette shows its black circle in place of the lit model), the menu then stays up frozen for about a second, then closes onto the new character; the stutter step is still there. So deleting the field player also takes the menu's 3D character (the pouch character shows the player's equipment; not looked into further), and the load is not shorter with the menu open: about 1.7 s from the old player gone to the new one created, the same as with the menu closed. The recording: under the menu's pause the wake routine reports done at once, but the player stays asleep until the pause ends (the message waits); the set-position's flag (+0x1c30 bit 0) was used up one frame BEFORE the wake took effect, so its reset went to the sleeping player. The quick variant is not taken further in this form.
  - Next try for the stutter (`--release --settle`, the fade version with `settle_steps`): after the pause is released, still black, 3 frames, the set-position, 10 frames in which the new player runs unseen, then the fade back.- The field swap copy is Lichii build 160 since 2026-11-14 (`tools\fieldenv.py`: `C:\Repos\Lichii\dist\build-160-field`, Lichii's data and saves shared, its own mods folder; `PLAY-debug-field.bat`, debug server 6549). Its rounds install the v0.2.0 zip's one folder with only our two files changed (`--release`: the menu graph, our records in the merged code patch, the size table entry if it has to grow), checked against the zip first. The old Fritchii copy (`Fritchii-field`) is left as it was.
## What we know about voices and sound files

Found offline on 2026-11-11. In game so far: the converted voice replacing child Link's for everybody (passed); the per-character voice (passed with Saria, then young Zelda; 2026-11-13: young Ruto, both Malons and Fado, and every voice as adult Link too).

- Link's voice is recorded clips, one set per age, inside the player's sound archives: `Sound/Resource/Player_Child.bars.zs` (2072 assets, 325 of them voice, `Vo_PlChild_<event>_<nn>`) and `Player_Adult.bars.zs` (3475 assets, 352 voice, `Vo_PlAdult_...`). `Player.bars.zs` has no voice. The rest of those archives is footsteps, cloth and equipment sounds.
- The events are nearly the same for both ages: `SwordAttack_S` / `_M` / `_B` (5 clips each, numbered from 01), `JumpAttack` (4), `RollAttack`, `RotaryCut_S` / `_B`, `Jump_S` / `_B`, `Damage_S` / `_M` / `_B`, `Death`, `FallingScream`, `Climb`, `Shield_S` / `_B`, `Wait` (50 child, 60 adult idle sounds) and so on. The adult adds horse, hookshot, hammer and big sword events; the child adds boomerang, crawling and Deku stick ones.
- Clip format: BWAV, one channel, 48 kHz, Nintendo DSP ADPCM. A sword shout is 0.13 to 0.7 seconds. Clips longer than about 0.3 s are streams: the archive holds only the first 0.30 s (a "prefetch" BWAV) and the whole clip is `Sound/Resource/Stream/<asset>.bwav` (248 child and 325 adult voice files, 5.9 and 7.7 MB). Short clips are whole inside the archive. A voice mod therefore has to replace both the archive and the stream files (the Classic Link Voice and Legend of Leon mods do).
- BARS archive (version 0x102): `BARS`, size, byte order, version, asset count, then the CRC32 of every asset name (sorted), then per asset the offsets of its metadata block (`AMTA`; the name's relative offset is at +0x24) and of its BWAV.
- The NPCs have recorded voices of their own. 98 voice types (`Voice/VoiceTypeInfo/<type>.game__sound__VoiceTypeInfo.bgyml`; story characters are `Custom_Saria`, `Custom_Zelda_Child`, `Custom_Impa`, ..., the others are shared types such as `Kokiri_Girl_Spry` or `Hylia_MA_Man_2`) with 8922 clips in `Sound/Resource/Stream/NV_<type>_<emotion><nn>.bwav` (217 MB; the story characters' clips exist once per language, `NV_USen_Custom_Saria_...`). They are talk reactions, about 40 to 80 per type: `Angry`, `CallFar`, `Painful`, `SurpriseS` / `M` / `L`, `LaughS` / `L`, `Happy`, `Sad`, `Notice`, `Think`, `Greeting`, `Ease`, `TalkPositive`, `QuestionNormal`; some types also have `Run`, `Scare`, `Stretch`, `Wakep`, `Sleep`. Nobody but three soldier types has an attack shout (`WeaponSwingS`).
- Which voice a character has: an actor's `VoiceRef` component (`Component/VoiceParam/<name>.game__component__VoiceParam.bgyml`, inherited through the `$parent` chain of its `ActorParam`) names the type file (`VoiceTypeInfoBgymlPath`). `toolsoicescan.py` (run with `venv-voice`) joins that with the models of the 100 characters and measures every type's clips; the table is `build-voice\scan.txt` (`scan.json`). Result (2026-11-11): 93 of the 98 NPC characters have a voice type, 77 types in all. Own voices: the eleven `Custom_` story characters and Mido, both Malons, Talon, Ingo (one type for both models), Dampe (plus `Dampe_Ghost`), King Zora, Great Fairy, Darunia's son, Biggoron, the mask shop man (`Hylia_MN_005`), the Poe buyer (`Hylia_MU_005`). The townspeople, Kokiri, Gorons, Zoras and Gerudo have shared types; a model used by many actors has several (the soldier `Hylia_MS_001` twelve, the Zora five, the Goron four). No voice: child-era Ganondorf and Phantom Ganon (the adult's `Custom_Ganondorf` fits), `Gerudo_GEN_011` (its leader model has one), `Hylia_MM_006` and `Kokiri_MC_004` (no actor uses the model). Material: most types have 22 to 85 s of talk reactions (trimmed). Thin: adult Zelda 2.9 s (six clips), adult Ruto and Rauru only cutscene lines (19.4 s, 6.8 s), Impa 12 s, Sheik 15 s. A type's clips are `NV_<type>_...`; a longer type name that fits owns the clip (`Dampe_Ghost` against `Dampe`, `Kokiri_Girl_Spry` against `Kokiri_Girl`). The cutscene voice archives (`Voice/Resource/<language>_EventFlowMsg-...bars`, 100 per language) hold the heads of the same `Dm_` / `Ev_` stream clips, nothing more.
- Median voice pitch, measured with Praat over each voice's clips (child Link = 0): child Link 363 Hz, his attack shouts alone 585 Hz; adult Link -2.6 semitones; Saria +2.0, young Zelda +2.7, young Ruto +2.2, Kokiri girls +2.4, young Malon +4.8, Kokiri boys -2.3, Mido -6.3, Nabooru -8, Talon -8, Impa -12. Deep voices (Darunia, Ganondorf) were measured with a 120 Hz floor and are not reliable.
- The same pitches measured again by `tools\voicescan.py` (2026-11-11, Praat floor 60 Hz so deep voices count, all of a type's clips; semitones against child Link): Saria +2.1, young Zelda +2.0, young Ruto +3.9, young Malon +4.8, adult Malon +2.5, `Kokiri_Girl_Spry` (Fado) +3.8, Great Fairy +4.7, Darunia's son +0.2, Mido -3.9, Ingo -4.6, Darunia -5.4, Talon -6.9, adult Ruto -6.9, Nabooru -8.2, Ganondorf -9.4, Biggoron -10.2, Impa -12.2, Sheik -12.5, King Zora -15.5, Dampe -19.9, Rauru -22. Where the two lists differ, this one is the later and has the floor that fits deep voices.
- Both ages (2026-11-13). Adult Link's voice is 352 clips, 286 s (four with two channels). A child voice cannot simply be reused under the adult's names: only 216 of the 352 adult clips have a child clip of the same name, the child's takes are much shorter (median 0.76 of the adult's length), and the adult-only events (big sword, horse, hookshot, hammer, magic, 21 `Wait` clips) have no child clip at all. So a voice is converted once per age, from that age's own clips. The AI voice follows the source's pitch, so a character converted from both Links at their own pitch would sound 2.6 semitones lower as the adult; the conversion takes whole semitones, and 3 passed by ear (snaildev, 2026-11-13: Saria as adult Link is the "Same voice" as her child set; adult Malon, converted from adult Link at his pitch and from child Link 3 lower, "Good" as both). The conversion's check asks for the frame-wise pitch within 2 semitones of the asked shift; the kept share is the same as without a shift (307 to 315 of 352).
- The sound link file (`SLink2/slink2.Product.111.bslnk.zs`, magic `XLNK`, 5.6 MB unpacked) names every voice asset and has `Pitch` and `Volume` among its call parameters. So the engine can pitch a clip at playback, which is the same thing as the plain pitch change below; not read further.
- Listening round 1 (snaildev, 2026-11-11; samples of ten child shouts for Saria): plain pitch +2 semitones is the best and clean, the base for Saria; +2 to +3 is the range. The Praat version that keeps the length ("matched") keeps more of the grunt, does not suit Saria and sounds slightly grainy. Her own recorded clips are out: they are call-outs, Link's are effort shouts.
- AI voice conversion (Seed-VC's 44.1 kHz model that follows the source's pitch, 50 steps, reference = the NPC's own clips, 22 to 25 s; the ten shouts in one file with 0.35 s gaps). Offline numbers: every shout is there, within 25 ms of the source's timing, the gaps stay silent, the pitch follows the source (plus the asked shift). Speaker embedding similarity to Saria's clips of another language: Link 0.41, plain +2 0.40, AI 0.58 to 0.64, her own clips 0.79. A run takes about 40 s, most of it loading the models (RTX 3090). Not yet judged by ear.
- Writing clips back (offline, 2026-11-11): a BWAV's checksum at +8 is the CRC32 of its sample data; for a stream it is the whole stream's, and the head in the archive carries the same value, the same coefficients and the stream's first 8192 bytes (14336 samples, 0.30 s). The field at +0x44 of the channel block is the first frame's header byte. All 325 child voice clips have 1 in the field at +0x38 with an end of -1: not a real loop. `tools\bwav.py` has an encoder (`encode_adpcm`) and `rewritten` (a header for new data).
- Two names can be one clip: 167 groups in `Player_Child`, 234 in `Player_Adult` share a BWAV inside the archive (child voice: `Wait_32_01` = `EscapedBreath_00`; adult voice: six pairs), each name with a stream file of its own. Three child voice assets are whole in the archive and also have a stream file with another, longer take the game cannot reach (`Climb_02`, `Climb_03`, `Dm_TimeTemple_0030_0004`). Four child voice clips have two channels (`FallingScream_For_Demo_*`, `WallMasterReturn_Scream*`).
- Code, first look: 0x8104c360 is the sound system's start-up (it hands `content://Sound/Resource/Stream/` to the audio library as the stream folder, loads the group settings, the archive list and the sound link file). 0x806eb2d0 (481 instructions, three callers: 0x806e30c0, 0x806eb068, 0x836efa04) joins `%s%s%s` with `.bwav`: the likely place where a stream file's name is made. 0x806e78c0 (eleven callers) handles `.bameta`, `.prefetch.bwav` and `.bwav`. 0x80ee9760 makes the `Sound/Resource/`, `Sound/ResourceList/` and `Sound/ChoiceAsset/` folders' paths.
- Which archives an actor has: the actor's `Component/SLink/<name>.engine__component__SLinkParam.bgyml` names its sound user (`UserName: Player_Child`), and `Sound/ResourceListPack/BarslistPack.Product.111.byml.zs` has a list of that name: `BarslistTable.<name>.Bars` (archive file names; `Player_Child`: `Player.bars`, `Player_Child.bars`; an NPC: `Npc.bars`, `Npc_Biped.bars`, its own) and `.ChoiceAsset`. 1060 lists. `LargeBars` (519 archive names, all with value 0) only picks the heap: an archive named there is loaded into the sound manager's first heap (+0x20), the others into its second (+0x28) when there is one (0x805d6270).
- The code that reads it: the sound manager keeps the table at +0x440 (`LargeBars` at +0x450; parsed in 0x8104d870). A request is (manager, handle, &name): 0x805d5b00 sends a name that ends in `.bars` to the single archive request (0x805d6070: folder at manager + 0x90, plus the name, through the resource system) and every other name to the list request (0x805d5bc0; its twin for another manager class is 0x8088d150 with 0x8088d3f0). The list request looks the name up in the table (the calls at 0x805d5c24 and 0x8088d1b4: the only two lookups by list name in the executable), makes a holder with one slot of 0x28 bytes per archive, in the list's order, and requests each. A handle that was asked once is not asked again. The actor side is 0x805d6640 (`SLinkUserName`, `IsCreateSLink`), which calls 0x805d5b00.
- How a clip is found (0x806e78c0, eleven callers; (x, the user's handle, out, &name)): it walks the holder's slots in order; for each archive that is loaded it takes the crc32 of the name (the CPU's own instruction, which is why no table shows up) and searches the archive's sorted hash table; the first archive that has it answers. The out gets the BWAV, the metadata block (after a check of its eight header bytes) and two flag bits from the block's +0x2c. No name is compared. So an archive placed first in a list overrides single clips of the later ones, and a list may hold the same name twice.
- A stream file's name is made from the metadata block's name string, not from the name the clip was asked by: 0x806eb2d0 formats `%s%s%s` = the stream folder, the string at block + 0x24 + its offset, `.bwav`. So an asset can be found under one name and play a stream file of another (what the voice archives do).
- BARS in full (version 0x102): 16-byte header (`BARS`, size, byte order mark, version, count), the name hashes (sorted), per asset the offsets of its metadata block and its BWAV (0xFFFFFFFF = none), then a second table (a count and that many hashes, sorted: a subset of the assets, in `Player_Child` the 34 looping slide and drag sounds, empty in the NPC voice archives), the metadata blocks back to back in table order, then each BWAV at a multiple of 64 (not in table order in the game's files; equal clips stored once). Metadata block (`AMTA`, version 5): size at +8, 0x34 at +0x10, an offset to further data at +0x1c for some, the name's offset at +0x24 (from +0x24), the name's crc32 at +0x28 (always equal to the table's hash), flags at +0x2c (2 = whole clip, 3 = stream head), `01 01 00 04` at +0x30, four floats from +0x38 (loudness figures), then the name.
- Size table values of sound files: an archive's entry is its size rounded up to 32, plus 584 (193 of 196 checked) or 616; the archive list's is rounded size plus 264. Stream files have no entry.
- The NPC voice clips' heads sit in archives of their own, `NV_<type>.bars` (story characters once per language), 300 to 700 KB each; `Voice/Resource/` plus a name plus `.bars` is put together in 0x80f292b8 and 0x82bfeb80 (events: `VoiceBarsNameList`), `<prefix>_<name>.bars` in 0x81038220. Not read further.
- Code space (2026-11-11, with the five normal folders installed): of the padding after `.text` (0x83c661d0 to 0x83c67000) one piece of 404 bytes is free (0x83c66a6c to 0x83c66c00, the end of the models part), the rest is pieces of 40 bytes or less. Unused code instead: 50 of the executable's 184 `SeqOneShot...` class names are in none of the game's 1942 graph files or 8385 packs. The largest bodies among them that only their own vtable points to: `SeqOneShotEnableRestoreBG` (0x832f0c80, 528 bytes: the voice patch), `SeqOneShotLuaScript` (0x832f7090, 460), `SeqOneShotSetMessageToLayout` (0x83bd5380, 340), `SeqOneShotSetDebugBancParam` (0x809d76a0, 300). (The field swap has two others.)
- The executable builds the file names in few places: `content://Sound/Resource/Stream/` has one reference (function 0x8104c360), `Sound/Resource/` two (function 0x80ee9760), `.bars` four, `.prefetch.bwav` three. The per-character voice does not hook these: it hooks the lookup of the player's archive list (above).

## What we know about Hookshot and the multiplayer mod

Read from their sources on 2026-11-12 (Hookshot v2.2.0-rc.1, commit 4aee236; the multiplayer mod at v0.1.0-test10). Nothing here was built or run by us yet.

- Hookshot (github.com/NotRexed/Hookshot, GPL-3.0) is a static recompilation of the same game build (`e0f3ccfa`, 1.1.1). Each player builds it on their own PC from their own game: `nx2/lift.py` translates the ARM64 code to C, `nx2/build.py` compiles it with `host/` (10 to 45 minutes the first time, about 60 GB free while building). Installed here: `%USERPROFILE%\Games\Hookshot`, v2.2.0-rc.1, built 2026-11-12, plain (no co-op, no `mods` folder). Its builder is at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\HookshotBuilder\versions\v2.2.0-rc.1`. Our `image.bin` is Hookshot's image, so our addresses are Hookshot's (main at 0x80020000).
- Mods on Hookshot (its readme, "Mods"; `host/mods.c`): `mods\<name>\romfs\...` beside `hookshot.exe`, or `mods\contents\<title id>\<name>\romfs`; a folder whose name starts with `-` or `_` is off. Differences from Lichii: the folder that sorts LAST wins a file (Lichii: the first); only ONE mod's size table is used, the last-sorting mod's (the button prompts' entries are merged into it); `exefs` patches (`.ips`, `.pchtxt`) are not loaded at all. A file larger than its table entry does not load, or the loading screen waits for good, as on the emulator.
- The builder's version check is the build id only (`make_game.py` `check_supported_build`: the id starts with `e0f3ccfa`), not a hash of `main`.
- How the lifter sees code (`third_party/a64-kit/lift/a64_lift.py`, `hos/nx_image.py`): a function is an entry plus everything reachable from it without crossing another entry. Entries: symbols, unwind info, relocated pointers into code, every `bl` target found by scanning the text, ADR / ADRP+ADD code addresses. A `b` to an address that is no entry is followed and becomes a `goto`; a branch into another entry is a tail call; `bl` is a C call, `blr` a looked-up call, `ret` a C return; a `br` without a jump table is an indirect tail call. A branch to an address outside the code range becomes a trap. The code range of a module is its NSO text segment exactly: main's ends at 0x83c661d0 (header of `.hookshot_exefs\main`: text size 0x3c461d0, rodata at +0x3c47000), so OUR CODE CAVE (0x83c66280-0x83c67000, the padding after the text) IS NOT CODE TO THE LIFTER; the bodies of unused node classes we also use are inside the text.
- How Hookshot itself changes the game: `Lifter.after[pc]` = C text emitted after one plain instruction (not after a call), checked against the build id and the instruction words (`nx2/oot_fov.py` is the short example); `nx2/oot_patches.py` edits the lifted C by exact text (its targets include the player camera's update `f_80644c80`, a view loop `f_804e48c0`, the text pane `f_80b1e140`: a patch of ours inside one of those functions could stop its edit from matching). Host C calls a game function with `a64_lookup(address)(ctx)`.
- Our code, for scale (the listings of the lite build, 2026-11-12): five patches (plus the voices one), 1,133 instructions in 66 blocks, 42 of them one- or two-instruction hook sites; one `blr`, no `br`.
- The multiplayer mod (github.com/daurosk/oot-remake-multiplayer, GPL-3.0, by dauro; snaildev has his permission to open pull requests): co-op for two to four players. Its kit builds Hookshot at its pin (v2.2.0-rc.1, the version installed here) with three patches of its own (`hookshot/patches`): lift hooks through `Lifter.after` (frame hook after main+0x6e6dd4 and +0x6e70f4, the AI hold after main+0x369df8, a puppet guard, platforms), a bridge (`host/coop_bridge.c`) that loads `hookshot_coop.dll` and hands it a C table (read, write, call a game function, log), and drawing in Hookshot's ImGui frame. All co-op logic is in the DLL, so it changes without a new lift. `docs/hookshot.md` there is the design.
- Its rules that touch us: on the emulator kit a mod folder with an `exefs` folder stops co-op before the game starts (its issue 136), and its emulator caves are at main+0x3c46280 and +0x3c46300 with a table at main+0x3c46400, which is inside our cave area (`CAVE_EXTRA` starts at 0x83c66280 = main+0x3c46280). So our mod and co-op never run together on the emulator. On Hookshot the DLL still writes that table at main+0x3c46400 while the AI hold is on (off by default): data of ours there would be overwritten. Test releases after test10 are Hookshot only; test11 is announced as co-op in the game's own UI and "other players as real game players".
- Its puppet pack (`mods\contents\0400486024DEC000\coop-puppet`, built from the player's own game by `tools/puppet_pack/build.py`) ships its own size table and its own `RSDB/ActorInfo` and `RSDB/GameActorInfo` tables (the game's plus its rows). With one size table per game on Hookshot, its table and ours cannot both be used: they have to be merged. The other players are actors `CoopPuppet_<Age>[_<Colour>]` with Link's model (`Player_<Age>`, or a recoloured copy `CoopTunic_...`); a pose is sent with a skeleton id (bone count + a hash of the model's name) and a puppet takes only a pose of its own model. What a player on one of our characters sends, and what the others see, is unread.
- Its contribution rules (`CONTRIBUTING.md`): nothing that came out of the game in the repository (a check runs on every push), one topic per pull request, a changed message layout comes with a new protocol version and `docs/protocol.md`, say what was tested and what is not proven, AI-assisted code is welcome, contributions are GPL-3.0, every pull request is reviewed by one of his review sessions.
- Offline check of "hand Hookshot our patched code" (2026-11-12; no build, seconds; the lifter's own `Lifter.walk` with Hookshot's `insn` and `lift_fixes` additions, run over `image.bin` with our six IPS files applied in memory; the script is not kept, this is what it did and found). With main's text as the game has it, 31 of our branches go to addresses outside the code range, which the lifter turns into traps: a patched `main` handed over unchanged would crash at the first hook. With the text segment grown to the page end (0x83c67000, +0xe30 bytes; the padding is all zero in the dump), every one of our instructions is reached from the functions we patch (the 12 lines it does not reach are data: the three signatures and the model name strings), no branch leaves the code range, and there is no `br`. None of our instructions uses x30 except to save and restore it, so `bl` as a C call and `ret` as a C return fit. Out of the ordinary in our code: 2 `pacibsp`, 2 `retab`, 1 `ldset`, 1 `ldclr` (forms the game itself uses). No clash by address: none of Hookshot's or co-op's `Lifter.after` sites (field of view, frame hook, AI hold, puppet guard, platforms) is in a function we patch, and none of the 65 functions `oot_patches.py` edits holds a patch site of ours. Not checked: that the emitter translates each instruction (it needs its compiled helper), that the loader takes a `main` with a grown text segment (by reading `nso.py` it only uses the header's sizes), and anything in game.
- Our data in co-op's way: the signature and the model name strings of Character models sit at 0x83c66400-0x83c66494 (main+0x3c46400), exactly where co-op's AI hold table is written (only with `LICHII_COOP_OBJECTS_AI_HOLD`, off by default), and co-op keeps a pointer at main+0x3c46780 (our code bytes there, which no longer matter once lifted). For a build that runs with co-op our cave data has to move out of 0x83c66400-0x83c66800.
- The Hookshot test copy with our mod (built 2026-11-12, snaildev's word: "build 0.1.2 for hookshot and be able to play it"; waiting for his look). What it is: `%USERPROFILE%\Games\Hookshot-CharacterSelect`, a game folder of its own (the normal one, `%USERPROFILE%\Games\Hookshot`, and the installed builder in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\HookshotBuilder` are only read), started with `PLAY-hookshot.bat`. How it was made: `build-hookshot\builder` is a copy of the installed builder's `tools` folder (v2.2.0-rc.1: the source, its Python and llvm-mingw, 1.3 GB) with the lift side of draft #2478 added by `build-hookshot\setup\port_exefs.py`: the draft's `nx2/exefs_patches.py` unchanged, its two lines in `nx2/image.py` and its two blocks in `nx2/make_game.py` (the Mods tab, the merged size tables and the cheats are not ported: one mod folder needs none of them). The mod is `mods\OoT Character Select` from `dist\OoT-Character-Select-v0.1.2-lite.zip`, put into the game folder BEFORE the build (the patch files are read from the game folder's `mods` when it is built). The build, run from `build-hookshot\builder`: `python\python.exe -I -u -X utf8 nx2\make_game.py --nup <the .nup> --out %USERPROFILE%\Games\Hookshot-CharacterSelect --work C:\Repos\OoT-Modding\build-hookshot\work --language en-US --no-prepare-shaders --log build-hookshot\build.log`. Its log: "exefs: code OoT Character Select/exefs/...ips (4934 bytes)", "exefs: main's code reaches 0x3c47000 now (a code cave)", "translated instructions: 100.0000% of 17647174", every one of Hookshot's own `oot_patches` applied, 0 files failed, 16 min 35 s in all (26 GB read from the .nup, lift 123 s, compile 536 s; the copied object cache was not reused because the system headers' path differs). Checked after it: the new `image.bin` differs from the normal copy's in 1,373 words, all ours; our cave routines are functions in the lift's table (12 of them); `exefs_patches.txt` in the game folder names the patch file. `build-hookshot\setup\after_build.ps1` then copied the normal copy's options, switches (`hookshot_env.txt`, plus `HOOKSHOT_NO_UPDATE_CHECK=1`: an update from the normal builder would build without our patches), save (15 files, a copy) and prepared shader caches (8.7 GB) into it. A CLI build puts `hookshot.exe` in the game folder itself (the builder's window makes a small launcher plus `bin\`). A change to the patch file needs this build again (the lift's key has the patches' hash); a change to game files only needs the mod folder replaced. Unseen until snaildev plays it: everything in game, and whether the copied shader caches are taken or the first start prepares them again.
- First run of the test copy (snaildev, 2026-11-12, an adult save): the game starts, the mod's files load, the Characters page shows with our work; loading "took forever" and play was "super laggy"; A on young Zelda crashed the game. The lag, from its log: the copied pipeline caches were read, but "the driver's shader cache kept 0 of 32 predicted pipelines", so every pipeline was compiled when first drawn (46 pipelines in 11.9 s by the title): the graphics driver's own cache did not serve this copy, and the shaders were not prepared for it (the build ran with `--no-prepare-shaders`). `hookshot.exe --prepare-shaders` in the game folder is the builder's step for that (about half an hour on this PC for the normal copy).
- THE RULE FOR CODE PATCHES THAT MUST ALSO RUN ON HOOKSHOT, and the crash that taught it. Hookshot's lifter makes `bl` a C call and `ret` a C return; a branch to an address that is no function start is followed and becomes part of the C function it came from. So a routine that is ENTERED WITH `bl` MUST LEAVE WITH `ret` (or by a tail call of a whole function, or by running a whole function from its first instruction to its `ret`, as the cloth hook's "run the original" does). Our "delete the menu Link" patch (`build.keep_camera_hook`, the `stash_slot` routine) was entered with `bl` in place of the game's call at 0x80c35c60 and came back with `b 0x80c35c64`, because its own calls use up x30. On the console and the emulator that is the same as a return. In the lifted C the rest of the function (the second hook at 0x80c35c64, the camera part, the epilogue) ran inside that call, returned to the call site and ran a second time: the frame was popped twice and the caller got its saved registers from the wrong place. The crash was two calls up, in the node executor 0x8076d7b0 right after it called a node's enter routine (vtable + 0xe8): "read ... guest 0x19" with x19, x21 and x30 zero and a frame pointer that is not 16-byte aligned, on a ModuleSystemWorker thread, at the first A in the picker (the picker deletes the menu character through that function). Fix (2026-11-12): the site is a plain `b` now (one byte of the patch: 0x94 -> 0x14 at 0x80c35c63). On the console nothing changes: every path through the routine ends with a `bl` before it jumps back, so x30 is the same either way, and the next instruction of the game is a call. Offline after the fix: the build's emulated checks pass, the three other sessions' dry runs pass, only the Character models patch file differs from the installed one. The emulator's install still has the old byte (works there as before). How it was found, for next time: the game folder's `hookshot_log.txt` has the crash (host backtrace, the registers, a guest backtrace when x29 is a frame); a CLI build keeps no link map, so host offsets were named by reading the x86 code of the function around them from `hookshot.exe`'s unwind table and collecting the guest addresses in its immediates; the lifted C of any function can be read by running the lift alone into a scratch work folder (`nx2/lift.py --standalone --write` with the build's environment, two minutes, 1.4 GB, game code: stays local). Checked for the same pattern in every patch (each routine of ours that is entered with `bl`: how it leaves): this was the only one; the cloth hook's and the voices' routines are whole-function calls or tail calls. Also checked and clean: no condition flag is carried across a call or a return (the lifter treats NZCV as dead there), no `br`, x30 only saved and restored.
- Rebuild of the test copy after a change to the code patch, measured (2026-11-12, the one-byte fix): 2 min 50 s in all (the .nup is not read again, the lift runs again because the patches are in its key, the compile took 50 s since only the changed pieces are compiled, then the game folder is put together), against 16 min 35 s for the first build. The same command as the first build; the settings, the save and the caches in the game folder stay. To put a changed patch into the test copy without packaging: the merged patch file in `mods\OoT Character Select\exefs` carries every byte of the working folders' patches, so a change can be written into it (as done for the fix, then checked against the newly built Character models patch), or the folder is replaced by a new package from `tools\package.py`.
- Second run of the test copy (snaildev, 2026-11-12, after the fix above and with the shaders prepared): "loaded smooth, crashed when I selected saria". A second, different fault, this one in Hookshot's lifter: `a64_lift.emit_function` writes a function's instructions in address order and the C function starts at the first of them, so IT ASSUMES A FUNCTION'S ENTRY IS ITS LOWEST ADDRESS. That holds for compiled code. Two routines of ours go on at a lower address than their entry: the shadow range routine at 0x83c66f60 (the cave; called with `bl` from the menu hooks at 0x83c66808 and 0x83c66840) jumps to its continuation at 0x83bd5390 (the slide's scale, in the body of the unused `SeqOneShotSetMessageToLayout`) and back; and the cloth hook's "run the original" at 0x832f70bc (`pacibsp`, then `b 0x8071fa64`). The lifted f_83c66f60 began at 0x83bd5390: the handle check and the save of x30 at its real start never ran, and it called 0x80964e70 with the slot record in x0 instead of the actor (crash log: thread BaseProcExecutor5, "guest 0xffffefffffffffff", lr 0x83c66ff8, the node executor 0x800e998c above it; found by reading the lifted C of f_83c66f60 from a scratch lift). The cloth one lost only its `pacibsp`, harmless. What was done: (1) in the builder copy only, `third_party\a64-kit\lift\a64_lift.py` `emit_function` now starts such a function with `goto L_<entry>` and prints a line for each ("lift: f_... has code below its entry"); this belongs in a report to Hookshot with the code mod draft; (2) NOT changed in our patches, because both routines are in `mods\npc_characters.py` (the NPC and model sessions' file): for those sessions, the second rule for patches that must run on any Hookshot: A ROUTINE'S ENTRY MUST BE THE LOWEST ADDRESS OF EVERYTHING REACHABLE FROM IT BY BRANCHES (a continuation in spare code below the entry needs to be a routine of its own, called with `bl` and left with `ret`, or the entry has to be the lower block). A check for both rules over a built set of patches: `build-hookshot\setup\exits.py` (routines entered with `bl`: how they leave) and `build-hookshot\setup\entrycheck.py <hookshot source> <patched image.bin> <listings>` (functions with code below their entry; it uses the lifter's own walk).
- The lifter fix in the rebuilt test copy (2026-11-12, 3 min 1 s): its log names 28 functions that had code below their entry and now start at it: our two (f_83c66f60, f_832f70bc) and 26 OF THE GAME'S OWN (f_8167ab04 and 25 between f_824c4704 and f_824f4454, one cluster of code). So stock Hookshot runs those 26 from an instruction that is not their first, if they are ever called: a fact for the report to its maintainers, not looked into further here. Read in the lifted C after the fix (a scratch lift): f_83c66f60 and f_832f70bc begin with `goto L_<entry>;`, and "delete the menu Link" (f_80c35c30) reaches our stash routine with a `goto` and has one return path. In game: waiting for snaildev's third look.
- The fix for Hookshot itself (2026-11-12, on snaildev's word). Where it goes by their rules (`CONTRIBUTING.md`: `third_party/` is not edited, fixes to a64-kit's analysis are wrappers in `nx2/lift_fixes.py`): a fourth fix there wraps `Lifter.emit_function`, adds the entry to the function's labels and puts `goto L_<entry>;` after the function's head when the body's lowest address is not the entry; `nx2/lift.py` prints the count; `tests/test_lift_entry_below.py` is the game-free check (a made-up image, no translator needed). What the 26 functions of the unmodified game are: 25 inside main+0x24a21e0..0x24dc600, which has ONE unwind entry for 238 KB and uses libcurl's strings (the SDK's libcurl, linked in without unwind entries per function; its functions are known to the lifter only as `bl` targets, data pointers and jump table targets, so one that branches to a lower function nobody calls carries it below its entry), and main+0x165ab04, the return address of a `setjmp` call (the same callee as before the address the log names in "longjmp to 0x81681690"). None is known to run in the port, which is why the unmodified game does not show the fault. Local clone: `C:\Repos\Hookshot` (fork snaildev99/Hookshot, remote `upstream` = NotRexed/Hookshot). Their checks run with the builder copy's Python: `python -I -B tests/run_all.py <part of a name>`; the two that build the translator themselves (`test_spawn_emit.py`, `test_fpx_sticky.py`) cannot run here (the builder's llvm-mingw has no C++ standard headers), CI runs them.
- v0.1.3 (2026-11-13) is v0.1.2 with the rule above applied to `stash_slot`: one byte of the Character models patch (0x94 to 0x14 at 0x80c35c63, the `bl` that became `b`). Seen on Lichii 160 after `mods\build.py --install` (the Characters page and a pick: as before) and on the Longshot test copy, whose merged patch file had the same byte since 2026-11-12. The packages before it (v0.1.1, v0.1.2) crash on a Hookshot or Longshot build with code mods at the first pick. `tools\publish.py` leaves lines between `` in this file out of the public `docs/NOTES.md`.
- Mods on Longshot, what has been tried with the code mod draft (Longshot 134; as of 2026-11-13). "By hand" is snaildev playing on Windows 11 (RTX 3090); "agent" is mooki's windowless runs on Linux (RTX 4090).

  | What | Windows, by hand | Linux, agent |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | One mod folder: RomFS files and a size table | works | works |
  | One mod folder: one `.ips` of six code patches | works | works |
  | Eight folders (the working folders): four size tables merged | works | not tried |
  | Eight folders: six `.ips` files for one build id | works (the same 4935 bytes as the merged file) | not tried |
  | A game file two mods ship | works: the lower mod in the list wins | not tried |
  | The Mods tab: list, selection, tabs | works | seen |
  | The Mods tab: Off and On, the restart question, Restart now | works | Off kept over a quit and a start; the question needs a window |
  | The Mods tab: moving a mod | works (moved and moved back) | works: the order is kept over a quit and a start |
  | A mod Off whose patch is built in | works: its files and table are left out, the patch stays until the next build | works |
  | `longshot_mods.txt` written by hand and by the game | works | by the game: works |
  | A `.pchtxt` patch, a cheat file, the mouse on the tab, the compact bar on the Mods tab | not tried | not tried |

- Longshot and the emulators settle a file that two mods ship in opposite ways: Lichii and Fritchii take the first folder by name, Longshot the mod LOWER in the Mods tab's list (out of the box the list is in the names' order, so the last by name). Our eight working folders rely on the emulators' rule for four files (`MinusMenuToMain.module.ainb`, `Player_Child_C002.Player_Child.bfres.zs`, two actor packs), so on Longshot they need `longshot_mods.txt` with the folders in reverse name order ("on <folder>" a line, top of the list first). The merged release folder has no such files twice and needs nothing.
- The eight-folder test's steps (snaildev, 2026-11-13, all passed; the same steps are in 134's description): start and load; F1, Mods tab lists eight mods On; the game's own menu has the fourth tab and the Characters page; pick a character and close the menu, the swap runs; move a mod up and down; switch `Character field swap` Off, close the menu, Restart now; the game runs and the swap in the field is gone (the Characters page's copy of the menu graph is served); switch it On, Restart now, the swap is back. The log: 4 tables merged (679 ms at the first start after a build, 69 ms later), 3 tables with that folder off (58 ms).
- Longshot's restart question (the Insert menu, vk_menu.cpp, `menu_restart.h`): after Later it is not asked again until a row that takes a restart is changed (`set_row` tells the restart state). On 134's Mods tab a mod's change was not told, so a mod switched again in the same visit to the menu did not ask again; and the texts said "the mods" whatever changed. Both are in our follow-up branch (below).
- Reading the game's code through Longshot's lift (2026-11-13). The lift writes the whole game as C: `build-hookshot\work-stock\sa\gen\code_000.c` to `code_072.c` for the unpatched game (1.4 GB; `work-lift` and `work-modfix` are lifts with our patches). One C function a game function, `void f_<link address>(Ctx *c)`, the addresses those of `image.bin` at 0x80000000; registers are `c->x[n]`, a call is `f_<address>(c)`, each file begins with the declarations of the functions it calls. `functions.txt` in the same folder has a line a function, start and size (195,000 lines; `8071fa60 1676` is the cloth file routine our cloth hook patches). It is a second opinion beside the disassembly (`tools\a64.py`): where a function starts and ends, who calls whom, and the control flow as `if` and `goto`. Longshot's `host\` folder is C for the console functions the game imports (what each `nn::` call does).
- Build time, lite against full (2026-11-13, `mods\build.py` without `--install`, the model files cached): lite 24.4 s and 20.5 s, `--full` 28.1 s. The build folder is 611 MB (139 files) lite and 793 MB (295 files) full. The lite roster saves little time in the build; it saves the copy at install and the size of what is installed and shipped.
- Code mods on Hookshot itself (2026-11-13): the code mod work of Longshot's pull request 134 ported onto Hookshot's main (the Mods tab's functions as API version 23 there, the file `hookshot_mods.txt`) builds in 13 min 8 s from nothing on this PC and runs the mod: the Mods tab, the Characters page, a pick and the swap, a switch Off with Restart now (the game runs with the mod off and its patch still built in). The size table's merge took 504 ms at the first start after the build.
- Switching a code mod Off on Hookshot or Longshot without building again leaves half of it (seen 2026-11-13 on the Hookshot test copy): its RomFS files are no longer served, its ExeFS patches stay in the built game. With our mod the game runs, and the - menu's header is drawn wrong: the four-tab header patch runs without the layout and text that `Header four tabs` ships. A clean Off is a build without the mod (or with it switched off before the build); On again needs no build while the patch is still in.
- `tools\modstate.py` (2026-11-13): run it to see whether a build is running and what is installed in the emulator's mods folder, folder by folder, against the record of the last installs (`installed.json`, `installed.log`). `build.main()` holds its lock for a whole build, so two sessions' builds run one after the other; installers call `modstate.stamp` after copying.
